# Affordable Housing Subsidy Source Report

Generated 2026-11-08, 408 total records

**Counties in scope:** Sacramento, Sonoma, Napa, El Dorado, Solano, Yolo, Contra Costa, Alameda, San Joaquin, San Mateo, Marin, Placer, Orange, San Diego, Santa Clara, San Benito, Santa Cruz, Ventura, Fresno, Madera, Mendocino, Merced, Monterey, Nevada, Riverside, San Luis Obispo


## State-Level Programs (California, statewide)


### Statewide

**Affordable Housing and Sustainable Communities (AHSC)**
- Agency: Strategic Growth Council (SGC) with HCD implementing
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Round 10 NOFA (March 4, 2026): approximately $750M (20% of Cap-and-Invest auction proceeds through 2030). Per project $10M minimum, $50M maximum; AHD loans plus HRI grants must be at least 50% of the request and no more than $35M; STI/PGM grants up to $15M; $100M per recipient per round.
- AMI requirement: At least 20% of AHD units affordable; rental AHDs must average 60% AMI or below across restricted units; 55-year covenant. Density minimums (TOD 30 units/acre or FAR 2.0; ICP lower; RIPA lowest). Three project area types: Transit Oriented Development, Integrated Connectivity Project, Rural Innovation Project Area.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Round 10 closed May 4, 2026; awards to be adopted at the Dec 16, 2026 Council meeting. Round 11 guidelines expected late 2026 or early 2027 with a spring 2027 application deadline (Round 10 pattern: draft guidelines Dec 19, NOFA March 4, due May 4).)
- Layering notes: Joint application by the developer and a public agency (city, county or transit agency) covering housing (AHD loan), housing-related infrastructure, sustainable transportation infrastructure and programs. AHD loan terms follow MHP (55-year, 3% simple, residual receipts). Guidelines state AHSC funds are public funding subject to the State Prevailing Wage Law. Concurrent 4% AHSC and 9% CTCAC applications prohibited; one switch between 4% and 9% allowed. An AHSC award qualifies the project for the CDLAC ELI/VLI set-aside. Commonly stacked: 4% credits + CDLAC bonds + AHSC + MIP or MHP + local funds. Also stacks with IIG (both infrastructure), but HCD stacking limits apply across AHD and HRI funds. West Sacramento and Sacramento projects have been frequent winners.
- Source: https://sgc.ca.gov/grant-programs/ahsc/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**CalHFA Bond Recycling Program**
- Agency: California Housing Finance Agency (CalHFA)
- Type: bond
- Typical award: Recycled volume cap subject to availability. For 100% affordable projects, recycled bonds may fund up to 45% of total development cost with CDLAC allocation for the remaining 55%; refinance or market-to-affordable conversions up to 100%; 80/20 projects up to 80%. $25,000 transaction fee plus conduit fees.
- AMI requirement: 20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (with 10% at 50% AMI); 80/20 structure requires 20% of units at or below 60% AMI. Affordability generally 55 years from closing.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling, subject to recycled cap availability.)
- Layering notes: Recycled bonds cannot generate 4% LIHTC and are excluded from the aggregate basis test, so they reduce the CDLAC allocation a project needs while the CDLAC-allocated portion still generates credits. Must be drawn within 180 days of preservation and issued within four years of the original bonds. Useful for preservation and 80/20 deals that cannot win competitive CDLAC pools. Term sheet last revised April 2022.
- Source: https://www.calhfa.ca.gov/multifamily/programs/forms/termsheet-bondrecycling.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**CalHFA Conduit Issuer Program**
- Agency: California Housing Finance Agency (CalHFA)
- Type: bond
- Typical award: Bond amount set by the lender's loan. Issuer fee: greater of $15,000 or 18.75 bps up to $20M, then $37,500 plus 5 bps above $20M. Annual monitoring 5 bps (minimum $4,000). $5,000 application fee. CDLAC allocation fee 0.035% and 0.50% performance deposit (max $100,000) pass through.
- AMI requirement: 20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (with 10% at 50% AMI); 501(c)(3) bonds require 75% of units at or below 80% AMI. Regulatory agreement for the longer of the qualified project period, bond term, CDLAC resolution term or 55 years.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling; tied to CDLAC rounds (2026 CalHFA conduit-only submittal deadlines Jan 13, April 28, Aug 18).)
- Layering notes: Statewide alternative to local issuers (CSCDA, CMFA, housing authorities). Mandatory for MIP projects and for CalHFA perm loan projects. Can issue recycled bonds and 501(c)(3) bonds. Term sheet effective for applications after Jan 1, 2023 (last revised Feb 2023).
- Source: https://www.calhfa.ca.gov/multifamily/programs/forms/termsheet-conduit.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**CalHFA Tax-Exempt Permanent Loan Program**
- Agency: California Housing Finance Agency (CalHFA)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Minimum $5M permanent loan; sized to 1.15x DSCR (max 1.20x year 1 when paired with a CalHFA subsidy loan), lesser of 90% of restricted appraised value or 100% of development cost; combined LTV up to 120% with CalHFA subordinate debt. Rate: 15- or 30-year AAA MMD plus CalHFA spread (estimated 2.20% to 3.75%). Up to 40-year amortization; fully amortizing or 17- or 30-year balloon. Fees: $10,000 application, 1.50% funding fee, $7,500 annual monitoring. HUD/FHA Risk Share credit enhancement.
- AMI requirement: Greater of existing restrictions or 20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (with 10% at 50% AMI); rents at least 10% below market.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling; CalHFA publishes application milestones keyed to each CDLAC round (2026: borrower submittals due Jan 13, April 28, Aug 18 for conduit-only; MIP workbook March 2).)
- Layering notes: CalHFA must be the bond issuer when it provides the perm loan. Works with or without 4% credits. Required first lien for MIP projects. All other soft financing must be subordinate and coterminous. Term sheet last revised January 2026.
- Source: https://www.calhfa.ca.gov/multifamily/programs/forms/termsheet-perm-tax-exempt.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**California Housing Accelerator**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: $1.75B (ARPA, 2021-22 budget); 58 projects and 4,945 units funded. Forgivable 0% loans for 20 years filling the full permanent gap for shovel-ready HCD-funded projects that could not obtain bonds or 4% credits.
- AMI requirement: Per underlying HCD program award.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Closed (Tier 2 applications due April 8, 2022; permanent loan conversion deadline Nov 30, 2026). No new rounds; the 2026 MHP Gap Funding NOFA plays a similar role.)
- Layering notes: Historical reference only. Shows HCD's pattern of periodic gap rounds for stalled awarded projects (Accelerator 2021-22, MHP Gap NOFA 2026).
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/accelerator
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Enhanced State Tax Credits for 4% New Construction (R&TC Sec. (g)(1)(B), $500M program)**
- Agency: California Tax Credit Allocation Committee (CTCAC)
- Type: tax credit
- Typical award: $500M in state credits per year (2020 through 2026 budgets), plus carryforward ($515.5M total in 2026). 2026 split: $390.5M general allocation across the three 4% rounds, $25M for farmworker housing, $100M reserved for CalHFA Mixed-Income Program projects (used in Round 2). Basis multiplier 30% of construction-related basis (75% for projects not otherwise eligible for the 130% boost).
- AMI requirement: New construction paired with 4% federal credits; must receive a CDLAC bond allocation. Ranking follows CDLAC pools and set-asides (Rural, Homeless, ELI/VLI, Mixed-Income, geographic regions).
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Awarded with CDLAC/CTCAC 4% rounds; 2027 appropriation subject to the 2027-28 state budget (program has been funded annually since 2020). 2027 round dates not posted as of 2026-11-06.)
- Layering notes: This is the main gap filler for 4% new construction deals and is the credit source CalHFA MIP relies on ($100M MIP set-aside, which CTCAC may cut to $200M statutory max or lower by agreement). Not available for acq/rehab or 9% projects. State credits are allocated by CDLAC pool ranking when oversubscribed: Emerging Developer (replacing BIPOC) 15%, Rural 15%, Homeless set-aside 25%, ELI/VLI set-aside 20%, then geographic regions. Beginning 2028, the $25M farmworker credits would pair with HDFC's Joe Serna program under proposed rules.
- Source: https://www.treasurer.ca.gov/ctcac/tax.asp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Federal 4% Low-Income Housing Tax Credit (Tax-Exempt Bond Financed)**
- Agency: California Tax Credit Allocation Committee (CTCAC), joint application with CDLAC
- Type: tax credit
- Typical award: Non-competitive at CTCAC once CDLAC bond allocation is secured; credit equals roughly 4% of qualified basis annually for 10 years, limited by threshold basis limits. No per-project federal credit cap. Application fee $1,500 CTCAC plus $1,500 CDLAC ($1,700 scattered-site or resyndication).
- AMI requirement: Federal minimum 20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (average income test allowed); CDLAC scoring drives deeper targeting. 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 joint CDLAC/CTCAC rounds closed (Feb 3, May 19, Sept 8, 2026). 2027 schedule not posted as of 2026-11-06; expect three rounds (early Feb, mid May, early Sept pattern).)
- Layering notes: Requires CDLAC private activity bond allocation (bond financing threshold reduced from 50% to 25% of aggregate basis plus land by federal H.R. 1 for bonds issued after 2025). Core stack for larger new construction: 4% credits + CDLAC bonds + state credits (base or enhanced) + HCD MHP or AHSC + CalHFA MIP + local soft funds. Enhanced state credits ($500M program) available only with 4% new construction. Acq/rehab 4% deals can access the 15% state credit set-aside. Cannot combine with 9% credits on the same building. CalHFA bond recycling can substitute for part of the bond allocation but recycled bonds do not generate 4% credits. Prevailing wage is not triggered by the credits or conduit bonds alone, but most state soft sources in the stack (HCD, AHSC) will trigger it; CTCAC gives a 20% basis limit boost for prevailing wage projects.
- Source: https://www.treasurer.ca.gov/ctcac/2026/application.asp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Federal 9% Low-Income Housing Tax Credit (Competitive)**
- Agency: California Tax Credit Allocation Committee (CTCAC), State Treasurer's Office
- Type: tax credit
- Typical award: 2026 federal ceiling $136.4M annual credit statewide ($3.416 per capita). Per-project cap $2.8M annual federal credit (Reg. 10325(f)(9)(C)). Set-asides: Nonprofit 10%, Rural 20% (incl. 14% RHS/HOME/CDBG-DR and 10% Native American apportionments within rural), At-Risk 5%, Special Needs 4%. Geographic apportionments for target counties: Capital Region (El Dorado, Placer, Sacramento, Sutter, Yuba, Yolo) 5.7%; East Bay (Alameda, Contra Costa) 7.4%; South and West Bay (San Mateo, Santa Clara) 6.0%; Northern Region (Butte, Marin, Napa, Shasta, Solano, Sonoma) 4.4%; Central Valley (incl. San Joaquin) 8.6%.
- AMI requirement: Minimum federal 20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (or average income test); competitive scoring effectively requires deep targeting. 55-year CTCAC regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 rounds closed (R1 April 7, 2026; R2 July 21, 2026). 2027 schedule not posted as of 2026-11-06; expect two rounds, spring and summer 2027. 2027 regulations to be adopted Dec 9, 2026.)
- Layering notes: Mutually exclusive with 4% credits and CDLAC bonds on the same building; a project may not hold concurrent 9% and 4% AHSC applications. Commonly stacked with HCD MHP, AHSC, VHHP, Serna, local HOME/CDBG, county and city soft loans, and the welfare property tax exemption. CTCAC awards state credits alongside 9% federal credits via geographic apportionments (state credits adjusted at 80%). Nonprofit set-aside prioritizes homeless-assistance projects with at least $500K or $10K/unit of MHP, VHHP, Homekey, NPLH, HHC or MHSA capital. Prevailing wage not required by CTCAC itself, but Reg. 10327 grants a 20% threshold basis limit increase for prevailing-wage projects and 5% more for a PLA or skilled-and-trained workforce. Tiebreaker favors high public leverage, so local soft money matters. Max four awards per applicant per round. Proposed 2027 changes (Dec 9, 2026 adoption) would let small regions access the full annual apportionment in round 1 and cap state credit requests at $150,000 per tax credit unit.
- Source: https://www.treasurer.ca.gov/ctcac/2026/application.asp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Golden State Acquisition Fund (GSAF)**
- Agency: HCD seed capital; administered by Corporation for Supportive Housing and partner CDFIs (fund managers)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Short-term acquisition and predevelopment loans from a revolving fund seeded by HCD and leveraged with private capital; terms set by fund managers.
- AMI requirement: Sites to be developed or preserved as affordable housing; terms per fund manager.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling through fund managers listed at goldenstate-fund.com.)
- Layering notes: Listed on HCD's grants and funding page with an external link; the fund's own site was not opened this pass, so terms need confirmation. Bridge financing only; repaid at construction closing from LIHTC/bond sources.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/grants-and-funding
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Homekey (Rounds 1 through 3)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: About $3.57B over three rounds; Round 3 (FY 2022-23) about $736M. 15,962 homes and 261 projects funded as of Sept 15, 2026.
- AMI requirement: Permanent or interim housing for people experiencing or at risk of homelessness.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No longer accepting applications; HCD directs applicants to Homekey+.)
- Layering notes: Legacy program. Existing Homekey sites can seek Homekey+ or MHP gap funds and qualify as adaptive reuse under CTCAC definitions. Local public entity applicants with developer partners.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/homekey
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Homekey+**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD) with CalVet
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Approximately $2.145B (Prop 1 Behavioral Health Infrastructure Bond plus HHAP supplemental): $1.033B for veteran-serving PSH, $1.11B for other target populations. Baseline $200,000 per door (acquisition plus rehab or new construction), plus up to $100,000 per door with 1:1 local capital match (1:2 for veteran units, i.e., $50,000 match earns $100,000). Operating grants per door also available with match. Awards as of July 2, 2026 listed on the HCD awardee list; March 2026 awards totaled $131.8M for eight projects.
- AMI requirement: Permanent supportive housing for people experiencing or at risk of homelessness with a behavioral health challenge; veteran and youth allocations. Applicants must coordinate with county behavioral health departments.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Over the counter until funds are exhausted (NOFA amended March 27, 2026). Applications after Aug 7, 2025 prioritized by veteran units; unrequested regional allocations moved to a discretionary reserve Oct 8, 2025. Pre-application consultation required via HCD Connect.)
- Layering notes: Eligible applicants are cities, counties, tribes and public entities, with developers as co-applicants (an LP may be co-applicant). March 2026 amendments allow projects with pending or future CTCAC/CDLAC applications (six months to secure awards), HCD portfolio projects with expiring regulatory agreements adding veteran units, and gap funding for stalled projects. Stacks with 4% credits, MHP and local funds; Homekey funding counts toward CTCAC homeless priority. NOFA Section 509 requires certification of state prevailing wage compliance or exemption under Gov. Code 65913.4(a)(9); statutory CEQA exemption available. Tribal Homekey+ ($121M) is separate.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/grants-and-funding/homekey-plus
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Housing for a Healthy California (HHC)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Article I (developers, federal NHTF funds): capital loans and operating reserve grants; 2020-21 NOFA about $160M. Article II (counties, SB 2 funds). No funding available since the 2021 Article I round.
- AMI requirement: Supportive housing for Medi-Cal eligible chronically homeless or high-cost health users; extremely low income.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Archived program; no funding available (HCD 2025 Super NOFA footnote). No new rounds expected.)
- Layering notes: Historically paired with NHTF and MHP for PSH. CTCAC still recognizes HHC commitments in the nonprofit set-aside homeless priority for existing awards. Successor PSH capital: Homekey+, MHP COSR grants.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/archive/hhc
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Infill Infrastructure Grant Program (IIG-2007 and IIG-2019)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD) / HDFC
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2025 Super NOFA Round 3: $20M. Qualifying Infill Project grants minimum $1M urban ($500,000 rural), maximum $10M per project under that NOFA. Grant sized by unit count, bedroom mix, affordability and density (net density multiplier up to 1.52 at 105+ units per acre).
- AMI requirement: Infill projects with affordable units; higher grants for deeper affordability. 55-year affordability covenant recorded against the site.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Closed as of 2026-11-06; last funded in 2025 Super NOFA Round 3 (due April 15, 2025) and included in the 2026 MHP Gap Funding NOFA for projects with prior awards. Next round via HDFC unified NOFA, timing unannounced.)
- Layering notes: Funds capital improvement projects (infrastructure, factory-built housing components, adaptive reuse) tied to a specific residential or mixed-use infill development, not vertical construction. Stacks on top of MHP or AHSC in the same Super NOFA application and reduces the basis the tax credits must cover. Catalytic Qualifying Infill Area solicitations (for local governments) are closed. Grant is public funds for prevailing wage purposes.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/iig
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Joe Serna, Jr. Farmworker Housing Grant Program (FWHG)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD) / HDFC
- Type: loan
- Typical award: 2025 Super NOFA Round 3: $120M. Multifamily rental: deferred-payment loans or forgivable-loan grants, including construction financing; loan terms follow MHP.
- AMI requirement: Households deriving 50% or more of income from agricultural employment; units at or below 80% AMI (units above 60% AMI funded at 60% AMI limits). 55-year restrictions.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Closed as of 2026-11-06; next round via HDFC unified NOFA, timing unannounced. Homeownership component funded via the Homeownership Super NOFA.)
- Layering notes: May be combined with federal and state farmworker tax credits and USDA 514/516. Proposed CTCAC 2027 rules would route the $25M enhanced farmworker state credits through Serna awards beginning 2028. Relevant for Napa, Sonoma, Yolo, Solano and San Joaquin projects. Only HCD program allowing construction-period financing.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/fwhg
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Local Government Special Needs Housing Program (SNHP)**
- Agency: California Housing Finance Agency (CalHFA), with county MHSA funds
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Historically county-funded MHSA capital loans and capitalized operating subsidies administered by CalHFA (16 participating counties as of 2019).
- AMI requirement: Permanent supportive housing units for persons with serious mental illness eligible for MHSA services.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Program discontinued effective January 3, 2020 per DHCS Information Notice 19-047; no new rounds.)
- Layering notes: No live CalHFA program page exists; the CalHFA multifamily programs page (source) no longer lists SNHP, and a DHCS notice (BH-IN-19-047) states CalHFA discontinued the program effective Jan 3, 2020 with county MHSA funds reverting to county control. Marked needs_confirmation because the DHCS notice could not be opened this pass. Successor supportive housing capital now comes from Homekey+, HCD MHP supportive units, and county Behavioral Health Services Act (Prop 1) housing allocations (research in Pass 2). CTCAC still lists SNHP commitments as qualifying homeless-assistance funding in the nonprofit set-aside.
- Source: https://www.calhfa.ca.gov/multifamily/programs/index.htm
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Local Housing Trust Fund (LHTF) Program**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Dollar-for-dollar matching grants to local and regional housing trust funds (public, joint public-private or 501(c)(3)); 2024 Round 5 was the last NOFA. Trust funds then lend to projects.
- AMI requirement: At least 30% of each allocation for extremely low-income households; no more than 20% for moderate income; 55-year rental restrictions.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No longer accepting applications; Round 5 (2024) closed. Future rounds depend on state budget.)
- Layering notes: Developers access LHTF money through local trust funds (e.g., Sacramento Housing Trust Fund, Napa, Sonoma, Yolo, Marin trust funds, Bay Area Housing Finance Authority), which should be captured in Pass 2 and 3. State match funds are public funds for prevailing wage.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/lhtf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Mixed-Income Program (MIP)**
- Agency: California Housing Finance Agency (CalHFA); transitioning to Housing Development and Finance Committee (HDFC) under the California Housing and Homelessness Agency
- Type: loan
- Typical award: 2026 round: $63M in subsidy loans to 13 developments (1,768 units); funded by 15% of SB 2 Building Homes and Jobs Act revenue. Per project: lesser of $6M, $50,000 per MIP-regulated unit ($60,000 in High or Highest Resource areas), or 50% of the CalHFA permanent loan. Sponsor limited to one award per year; no county may receive more than 25% of annual funds; age-restricted projects capped at 25%.
- AMI requirement: New construction, 4% credits with income averaging; at least 51% LIHTC units. MIP regulatory agreement (55 years): 30% of units at or below 50% AMI (including 10% at or below 30% AMI), 10% of units at 60% to 80% AMI averaging 70%, remaining units at or below 120% AMI; spread between lowest and highest targets at least 40 points; average affordability no more than 60% AMI. Rents at least 10% below market.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 round closed (application workbook due March 2, 2026, aligned to CDLAC Round 2; awards May 5, 2026). 2027 term sheet expected January 2027; check CalHFA Key Program Dates page.)
- Layering notes: Must use CalHFA Conduit Issuer Program for construction bonds and CalHFA Tax-Exempt Permanent Loan (minimum $5M perm loan, 1.15x DSCR). Draws on the $100M enhanced state credit MIP set-aside at CTCAC and the $310M State-funded Mixed Income bond set-aside at CDLAC, so MIP projects compete for bonds outside the geographic pools. Cannot be combined with 9% credits. 3% simple interest, residual receipts (50% of surplus cash), coterminous with perm loan, 1% loan fee. Term sheet is silent on prevailing wage; a 3% subordinate loan may fit the Labor Code 1720(c)(6)(E) below-market-loan exemption if 40% of units are restricted at 80% AMI for 20 years, but confirm with counsel. Governor's reorganization moves MIP into HDFC with HCD programs for a unified application.
- Source: https://www.calhfa.ca.gov/multifamily/mixedincome/index.htm
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Multifamily Finance Super NOFA (MFSN) umbrella**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD) / Housing Development and Finance Committee (HDFC)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Round 3 (Feb 13, 2025): $382M (MHP $230M, Joe Serna $120M, IIG $20M, VHHP $12M), applications due April 15, 2025; a separate $96M Disaster Impacted Areas NOFA and a Tribal MFSN. Round 4 (2026 MHP Gap Funding NOFA): $240M for construction-ready projects with an existing HCD award that lack bonds/4% credits, due June 5, 2026.
- AMI requirement: At or below 60% AMI for assisted units (80% AMI for Joe Serna). 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Window closed as of 2026-11-06. Governor's reorganization (effective July 1, 2026) consolidates HCD multifamily programs and CalHFA MIP under HDFC with a single application and unified scoring; next NOFA timing unannounced. Check hcd.ca.gov/funding/supernofa and housing.ca.gov.)
- Layering notes: One application for MHP, IIG, VHHP, Joe Serna (and SHMHP/TOD when funded). Universal scoring: lowest income levels, state policy priorities, experience, readiness, infill/amenities, cost containment, tiebreaker on public leverage. Awards sized to fill the gap after tax credit equity and permanent debt. Projects then compete at CDLAC/CTCAC; an HCD award unlocks CDLAC ELI/VLI set-aside eligibility. Emerging developer joint applications get a $25,000 per unit bonus. Gap NOFA excluded projects that already closed construction financing.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/supernofa
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Multifamily Housing Program (MHP)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD); transferring to Housing Development and Finance Committee (HDFC) under the California Housing and Homelessness Agency effective July 1, 2026
- Type: loan
- Typical award: 2025 Super NOFA Round 3: $230M MHP of $382M total. 2026 MHP Gap Funding NOFA: $240M, awards $3M to $15M per project, HCD funds capped at 60% of total development cost. Super NOFA per-project cap $50M across MFSN loans, COSR and services reserves; $80M per sponsor per round. Prop 1 (2018) authorized $1.5B for MHP.
- AMI requirement: Restricted units at or below 60% AMI; scoring rewards deeper targeting (30 points for lowest incomes). 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: No window open as of 2026-11-06. Last round: 2026 MHP Gap Funding NOFA (Round 4) due June 5, 2026, awards August 2026. Next round expected through HDFC's unified multifamily application; timing unannounced.)
- Layering notes: Core state gap loan: 55-year, 3% simple interest, 0.42% annual payment for 30 years, permanent financing only (construction must not have started at application). Delivered through the Multifamily Finance Super NOFA alongside IIG, VHHP and Joe Serna under AB 434. Pairs with 9% or 4% credits; an MHP award qualifies a project for CDLAC's ELI/VLI set-aside and CTCAC's nonprofit homeless priority. HCD stacking of multiple HCD sources on the same unit allowed within per-unit limits (Admin Notice 21-06). Gap NOFA awardees must apply in the next CDLAC round. HCD loans are public funds; state prevailing wage applies unless a Labor Code 1720(c) exemption fits. MHP can fund capitalized operating subsidy reserves (max $10M per project) and supportive services reserves as grants.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/mhp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**National Housing Trust Fund (NHTF), state allocation**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Annual federal allocation (varies); deferred-payment or forgivable loans. Most recent: 2024-25 Project Solicitation (amended) with awards posted.
- AMI requirement: Extremely low-income households (30% AMI); 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Closed as of 2026-11-06; a 2025-26 or 2026-27 solicitation has not been posted. Monitor the HCD NHTF page.)
- Layering notes: Federal NHTF itself does not carry Davis-Bacon, but the HCD loan is state public funds, so state prevailing wage applies. Pairs with 9% credits for deep ELI targeting; HCD has historically run NHTF through HHC Article I and stand-alone solicitations rather than the Super NOFA.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/nhtf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**No Place Like Home (NPLH)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Up to $2B in Prop 2 (2018) revenue bonds for county-sponsored PSH; county noncompetitive allocations plus competitive rounds (Round 4 last).
- AMI requirement: Permanent supportive housing for people with serious mental illness who are homeless, chronically homeless or at risk.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No longer accepting applications; no future rounds stated. Counties (and City of Berkeley) were the applicants.)
- Layering notes: Counties in scope may still hold uncommitted NPLH allocations or returned funds; check in Pass 2 (Sacramento, Alameda, Contra Costa had large allocations). NPLH commitments count toward CTCAC nonprofit set-aside homeless priority and CDLAC ELI/VLI set-aside eligibility. Successor: Homekey+ and county BHSA housing funds.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/nplh
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Portfolio Reinvestment Program (PRP)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Rehabilitation loans up to $25M at 30- to 55-year terms, 3% simple interest, 0.42% annual payments, plus forgivable capitalized operating subsidy reserve loans up to 25% of the rehab loan. 2022 NOFA about $332.5M; Round 2 NOFA Dec 28, 2023. Set-asides: $10M small projects (20 units or fewer), $25M residential hotels, $25M foreclosed projects.
- AMI requirement: Existing HCD-funded projects with 5 or more units whose HCD restrictions expire by Dec 31, 2033, legacy-program projects without recent rehab, or foreclosed projects; affordability extended.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Application window closed; no new round announced as of 2026-11-06. Contact portfolioreinvestment@hcd.ca.gov for eligibility list.)
- Layering notes: Only for HCD portfolio projects (expansion list published by HCD). Pairs with 4% resyndication and CDLAC Acquisition/Rehabilitation pool. Not a source for new developments.
- Source: https://hcd.ca.gov/funding/prp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Qualified Residential Rental Project (QRRP) Tax-Exempt Private Activity Bond Allocation**
- Agency: California Debt Limit Allocation Committee (CDLAC), State Treasurer's Office
- Type: bond
- Typical award: 2026 state ceiling $5.31B, QRRP share $4.77B (plus $56M carryforward). Non-geographic pools $3.0B: Acquisition and Rehabilitation $1.0B, BIPOC $130M, Rural new construction $130M, Homeless set-aside $650M, ELI/VLI set-aside $780M, State-funded Mixed Income $310M. New construction geographic apportionments $1.75B: Bay Area Region 21%, Northern Region 9%, Inland Region 16%, Coastal 21%, City of LA 17%, Balance of LA 16%. Supplemental pool $79.7M. Application fee $1,500; performance deposit 0.5% of allocation up to $100,000; allocation fee 0.035% of bond amount.
- AMI requirement: Federal minimum 20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (with 10% at 50% AMI under CalHFA issuance). ELI/VLI set-aside requires average 50% AMI or below plus an HCD or similar award. Mixed Income set-aside for projects with 50% or fewer restricted units or in CalHFA MIP. 55-year CDLAC regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 rounds closed (Feb 3, May 19, Sept 8, 2026; Round 3 awards Dec 9, 2026). 2027 schedule not posted as of 2026-11-06. 2027 regulations proposed for adoption Dec 9, 2026 (replaces BIPOC pool with Emerging Developer pool).)
- Layering notes: Prerequisite for 4% federal credits and enhanced state credits; applications are joint with CTCAC through the CDLAC online portal. Readiness points require construction start within 180, 201 or 222 days of allocation and committed soft financing at application, so HCD, AHSC, MIP and local awards must be in hand first. Bonds generally sized to at least 25% of aggregate basis plus land (federal threshold after H.R. 1; prior 50% test). CalHFA recycled bonds and 501(c)(3) bonds do not use volume cap. Conduit bond financing alone is generally not treated as public funds for state prevailing wage, but confirm with counsel; most paired state soft funds do trigger it. Issuers in target counties include CalHFA, CSCDA, CMFA, and local housing authorities.
- Source: https://www.treasurer.ca.gov/cdlac/qrrp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Section 811 Project Rental Assistance (PRA)**
- Agency: California Housing Finance Agency (CalHFA) with HCD and DHCS
- Type: other
- Typical award: $8M HUD award supporting over 600 units statewide; 20-year renewable project-based rental assistance contracts.
- AMI requirement: Units for extremely low-income Medi-Cal beneficiaries with disabilities ages 18 to 61 transitioning from institutions or homeless/at risk. Project must have state agency development financing.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Round IV NOFA closed due to oversubscription; CalHFA is not accepting applications.)
- Layering notes: Operating subsidy, not capital. Historically layered on CTCAC and HCD-financed projects to support special needs units. Monitor for a future HUD 811 PRA award.
- Source: https://www.calhfa.ca.gov/multifamily/section811/index.htm
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**State Farmworker Housing Tax Credit**
- Agency: California Tax Credit Allocation Committee (CTCAC)
- Type: tax credit
- Typical award: $500,000 in new state credits annually (plus carryforward; $1.0M available and fully awarded in 2026). Separate $25M annual enhanced state credit set-aside for 4% farmworker new construction. Basis multiplier up to 75% for farmworker projects.
- AMI requirement: Farmworker housing meeting CTCAC definition; paired with federal credits.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Awarded in regular CTCAC rounds; 2027 schedule not posted as of 2026-11-06.)
- Layering notes: Relevant to agricultural counties in scope (Napa, Sonoma, Yolo, San Joaquin, Solano). Commonly paired with HCD Joe Serna Jr. Farmworker Housing Grant and USDA Section 514/516. Exempt from the proposed per-unit state credit cap.
- Source: https://www.treasurer.ca.gov/ctcac/tax.asp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**State Historic Rehabilitation Tax Credit**
- Agency: Office of Historic Preservation (OHP) with CTCAC (allocation and regulations)
- Type: tax credit
- Typical award: $50M annual statewide: $40M for projects with QREs of $1M or more, $8M for projects under $1M, $2M for residences (capped $25,000 each). Credit is 20% of qualified rehabilitation expenditures, 25% for projects meeting criteria in Appendix D of the application instructions (includes affordable housing categories).
- AMI requirement: None required for the base 20% credit; 25% tier criteria include affordable housing. Properties must be listed or eligible for the California or National Register.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: OHP states funding for all categories is exhausted and applications are not being accepted; new allocations would be announced on the OHP page. Verify in early 2027.)
- Layering notes: Pairs with the federal 20% historic credit for dual projects and with LIHTC for adaptive reuse of historic buildings. CTCAC regulations for this credit effective Nov 12, 2024; CTCAC approved initial allocations April 8, June 18 and Sept 30, 2025.
- Source: https://ohp.parks.ca.gov/?page_id=27495
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**State Low-Income Housing Tax Credit (Base Program, R&TC Sec. (g)(1)(A))**
- Agency: California Tax Credit Allocation Committee (CTCAC)
- Type: tax credit
- Typical award: 2026 state credit ceiling $129.6M ($70M statutory base plus CPI adjustment), of which $67.3M available in 2026 after prior-year advance allocations. 15% ($10.1M) set aside for 4% acquisition/rehab projects; balance awarded with 9% credits through geographic apportionments. State credit is taken over 4 years (30% of basis for new construction: 9%/9%/9%/3%; 13% for acq/rehab basis). Separate $500K annual Farmworker credit.
- AMI requirement: Same as paired federal credit; state credit available only to projects receiving or concurrently receiving federal LIHTC. 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Awarded in the same 9% and 4% rounds; 2027 round schedule not posted as of 2026-11-06.)
- Layering notes: Paired with 9% credits automatically in geographic apportionments (CTCAC may exchange state credits for federal credits if state credits run short; proposed 2027 rule keeps at least 50% of the state request). 4% acq/rehab projects can request state credits only from the 15% set-aside and must meet criteria (A)-(D): building at least 15 years old, serving very low or extremely low income averaging 45% AMI or RHS 514/521 financed, insufficient credits due to low appraised value, substantial rehab. State credits may be certificated (sold) by nonprofit applicants at no less than $0.80 per dollar. Does not count as public funds for prevailing wage.
- Source: https://www.treasurer.ca.gov/ctcac/tax.asp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Supportive Housing Multifamily Housing Program (SHMHP)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: 55-year loans at 3% simple interest with 0.42% annual payments (2018 NOFA); $250,000 base per unit when offered through the Super NOFA.
- AMI requirement: Minimum of 5 supportive housing units or 40% of total units, whichever is greater; assisted units at or below 60% AMI.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Archived; last NOFA 2018. Residual funds were folded into the 2025 Super NOFA Disaster Impacted Areas NOFA.)
- Layering notes: Functionally replaced by MHP supportive housing provisions (AB 2483, SB 482) and Homekey+.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/archive/shmhp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Transformative Climate Communities (TCC) Round 6**
- Agency: Strategic Growth Council (SGC)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Round 6 funded by Prop 4 (2024 Climate Bond); amount not stated on the program page. Implementation grants for place-based plans in disadvantaged communities that can include affordable housing capital as one project component.
- AMI requirement: Housing components must be affordable per TCC guidelines; project must sit within a TCC-eligible disadvantaged community boundary.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Round 6 applications closed Sept 30, 2026 (implementation pre-proposals June 30, 2026); awards expected Spring 2027. Future rounds depend on Prop 4 appropriations.)
- Layering notes: Community-led collaborative applications (lead applicant typically a city or county) rather than developer-driven. Past awards included Stockton and Sacramento area communities, so San Joaquin and Sacramento sites may qualify. Housing funds within TCC follow AHSC-like terms; state grant funds are generally public funds for prevailing wage, verify in Round 6 guidelines.
- Source: https://sgc.ca.gov/grant-programs/tcc/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Transit-Oriented Development (TOD) Implementation Program**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD) / HDFC
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Historically loans and grants for higher-density affordable housing and related infrastructure near transit; 2025 Super NOFA listed TOD with $250,000 base per unit loans. AB 130 (2025) refocuses the program on affordable housing funded by a state vehicle miles traveled mitigation bank.
- AMI requirement: Assisted units at or below 60% AMI; 55-year restrictions.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Application window listed as PENDING; 2026 TOD Guidelines posted. No NOFA date announced as of 2026-11-06.)
- Layering notes: Overlaps with AHSC for transit-adjacent sites; AHSC has far more money. Watch for a VMT mitigation bank funded round in 2027. Eligible applicants include cities, counties, transit agencies and developers.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/tod
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Veterans Housing and Homelessness Prevention Program (VHHP)**
- Agency: California Department of Housing and Community Development (HCD) with CalVet / HDFC
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Prop 41 (2014) authorized $600M; 2025 Super NOFA Round 3 offered only $12M, indicating the bond authority is nearly exhausted. Loan terms match MHP (55-year, 3% simple, 0.42% payments).
- AMI requirement: Assisted units at or below 60% AMI for veterans and their families; at least 50% of funds must serve extremely low-income veteran households, and 60% of those ELI units must be supportive housing.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Closed as of 2026-11-06; funded through the Super NOFA when dollars remain. Remaining Prop 41 authority not stated on the HCD page; confirm with HCDVets@hcd.ca.gov before underwriting.)
- Layering notes: Applied for inside the Super NOFA with MHP. Pairs with HUD-VASH vouchers and Homekey+ veteran units; CTCAC counts VHHP capital toward the nonprofit set-aside homeless priority. Veteran definition includes other-than-honorable discharges. Little money left; Homekey+ ($1.03B for veteran PSH) is now the larger state veteran capital source.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/funding/vhhp
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption from Property Tax for Low-Income Rental Housing**
- Agency: State Board of Equalization (BOE) issues Organizational and Supplemental Clearance Certificates; county assessors grant the exemption
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Full exemption of property tax on qualifying low-income units (partial exemption proportional to qualifying units); typically worth 1% to 1.25% of assessed value annually. Non-LIHTC, non-government-financed properties owned by nonprofits or eligible LLCs are limited to $20,000,000 of exempt assessed value statewide per claimant.
- AMI requirement: Units occupied by lower-income households (80% AMI or below, with the 140% over-income continuation rule) under a recorded regulatory agreement or LIHTC. Limited partnership owners qualify only if the managing general partner is an eligible nonprofit or LLC holding an OCC and the property has LIHTC or government financing.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Annual claim due to the county assessor between January 1 and 5:00 p.m. February 15 (next: Feb 15, 2027); late claims receive 85% to 90% of the exemption. First-time claimants must obtain a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate (Form BOE-277) and, for limited partnerships, a Supplemental Clearance Certificate (Form BOE-277-L1, wet signature) before the assessor can grant the exemption.)
- Layering notes: Standard component of every LIHTC underwriting: nonprofit managing GP structure is required for the exemption, which in turn supports debt sizing on 4% and 9% deals. Also applies to supportive housing and welfare-exempt nonprofit-owned projects without LIHTC up to the $20M cap. County assessors in all 12 target counties administer claims (forms BOE-267, 267-L, 267-L1); construction-period exemption available once an OCC is in place and the property is under development for exempt use. Property tax exemption does not affect prevailing wage.
- Source: https://www.boe.ca.gov/proptaxes/welfarelowinc.htm
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


## County-Level Programs


### Alameda County

**Behavioral Health Services Act (BHSA) housing interventions, Alameda County Behavioral Health Department**
- Agency: Alameda County Behavioral Health Department (ACBHD), Alameda County Health, with Housing and Homelessness Services for the flexible housing subsidy pool
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Integrated Plan FY 2026-29 approved by the Board of Supervisors June 16, 2026 (public hearing April 20, 2026). BHSA budget authority about $166,188,844 a year; Housing Interventions component $37,306,397 (FY 2026-27), $41,735,347 (FY 2027-28), $44,221,971 (FY 2028-29); 52% dedicated to chronically homeless people; outreach and engagement 5% to 6%. Uses: rental subsidies, operating subsidies for existing and new PSH units (about 990 people a year), flexible housing subsidy pool, landlord mitigation, tenancy sustaining services. Capital development share: 0% in all three years; the plan states the County will consider capital development projects as needed once the BHSA transition is complete.
- AMI requirement: Adults, older adults, youth and families with serious mental illness or substance use disorder who are chronically homeless, homeless or at risk; Housing First; ACBHD eligibility and Coordinated Entry referral for PSH units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Plan in effect July 1, 2026 with annual updates each spring; no capital NOFA. Ask ACBHD and H&H about project-based operating subsidies and flexible pool commitments for PSH units; capital requests should go to Measure W Home Together Fund instead.)
- Layering notes: Unlike Orange and Contra Costa, Alameda chose not to put BHSA money into capital, so BHSA is an operating and services partner only: pair a BHSA operating subsidy or flex pool commitment with Measure W capital and HACA or city PBVs. State funds would trigger prevailing wage if capital were ever offered. Figures from the June 16, 2026 Board item (304 pages, opened) and the approved plan PDF and executive summary on acbhsa.org.
- Source: https://www.alamedacountyca.gov/board/bos_calendar/documents/DocsAgendaReg_06_16_26/HEALTH%20CARE%20SERVICES/Regular%20Calendar/BHCS_405776.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**County boomerang funds for housing and homelessness (former redevelopment residual property tax)**
- Agency: Alameda County Board of Supervisors through Housing and Community Development and Health, Housing and Homelessness Services
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2017 policy: 20% of the prior year's boomerang receipts, $5,000,000 minimum and $7,500,000 maximum a year, as a five-year commitment starting FY 2016-17 ($5,000,000 in FY 2016-17 and FY 2017-18) for housing resource centers and coordinated entry ($2,000,000 a year), anti-displacement crisis services ($3,500,000 initial) and other Home Together system costs; a one-time $9,800,000 boomerang allocation seeded Measure A1 implementation. HCD still lists boomerang funds among its development sources and recent HCD project commitments cite boomerang dollars, but no current annual amount or policy was located and the 2025 Housing Plan does not list it as a revenue source.
- AMI requirement: Follows the program funded; development uses carry County loan terms (3% simple, 55 to 59 years, at or below 80% AMI).
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Unknown; ask HCD whether boomerang funds remain in the fall development RFP or were absorbed into Measure W planning.)
- Layering notes: Recorded as needs_confirmation because the only document opened is the 2017 EveryOne Home implementation deck describing the five-year commitment; the acgov HCD page that listed boomerang funds now redirects to achcd.org without that text. Public funds, so prevailing wage applies. Housing successor (LMIHAF) reporting for the former Alameda County Redevelopment Agency was not found.
- Source: https://everyonehome.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/05/EOH-GO-Bond-Implementation-and-Boomerang-5-3-17.pdf
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Development fee waiver for affordable housing (no countywide program)**
- Agency: Alameda County Community Development Agency
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: None adopted. The County's 2025 Housing Plan lists an affordable housing impact fee on commercial development as a future unincorporated revenue option and does not describe any fee waiver or deferral program.
- AMI requirement: Not applicable.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not applicable. Budget full County and special district fees in unincorporated deals; permit fees are an eligible use of County loans.)
- Layering notes: Negative finding. Labor Code 1720(b)(4) counts waived fees as public funds for prevailing wage purposes. Housing Plan (10-Year Housing Strategy 2025-2035, approved July 2025) opened.
- Source: https://www.achcd.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/Housing-Plan-Approved-2-July-2025.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**HOME Consortium and Urban County CDBG affordable housing development loans (annual HCD RFP)**
- Agency: Alameda County Housing and Community Development Department, HOME Consortium lead agency and Urban County CDBG administrator; CDBG 510-670-5971, CDBG@acgov.org
- Type: loan
- Typical award: HCD issues one competitive affordable housing development RFP each fall combining HOME (Consortium CHDO and Urban County shares), remaining Measure A1 and other local funds; applications are due about eight weeks after release with a mandatory bidders conference. HOME Consortium: Alameda, Fremont, Hayward, Livermore, Pleasanton, San Leandro, Union City and the Urban County (all cities except Oakland and Berkeley, which are their own participating jurisdictions); the City of Alameda's FY 2026-27 draft Action Plan reports the Consortium's 2025 HOME allocation as $1,475,257, a figure to confirm with HCD. Urban County CDBG covers the unincorporated area plus Albany, Dublin, Emeryville, Newark and Piedmont, which administer their own shares; the unincorporated CDBG RFP runs December or January with applications due March or April and contracts July 1. State ESG to the County for the CoC: $402,650 (2024). Past procurements: 2023 HOME Capital RFP, 2024 CDBG NOFA (due Jan 19, 2024).
- AMI requirement: HOME: households at or below 80% AMI at HOME rents for 55 years under County documents (59-year regulatory agreement standard, 55 allowed for tax credit projects); CDBG: low and moderate income benefit. 15% of Consortium HOME reserved for CHDOs.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Fall 2026 RFP date not posted as of Nov 6, 2026; HCD's funding opportunities page says the RFP is issued each fall. Unincorporated CDBG applications are due March or April 2027 for FY 2027-28.)
- Layering notes: Federal money is a small share of Alameda County's stack: the Consortium's HOME is a few million dollars a year spread across eight members, so the County's own awards are typically under $1,000,000 and ride on the same fall RFP as Measure A1 and local funds. Davis-Bacon applies to HOME projects with 12 or more assisted units; County loans carry state prevailing wage. HCD also administers HOPWA housing for the Oakland area and runs AC Boost, Renew AC and the AC Housing Secure programs from the same department. The County's own FY 2026-27 Action Plan PDF (47MB Consolidated Plan link) was not opened; figures come from the HCD CDBG page, the funding opportunities page and the City of Alameda's draft plan.
- Source: https://www.achcd.org/community-development-block-grant-cdbg/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Measure A1 Rental Housing Development Fund (Base City Allocations, Regional Pools, Innovation and Opportunity Fund)**
- Agency: Alameda County Housing and Community Development Department (Measure A1 bond program)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: $580,000,000 general obligation bond approved by 73% of voters in Nov 2016, fully issued by 2021. Rental Development Fund of $425,000,000: a $225,000,000 Base City Allocation (every city and the unincorporated area by housing need) and a $200,000,000 Regional Pool awarded through competitive rounds in four regions, plus the $35,000,000 Innovation and Opportunity Fund ($25,000,000 Acquisition and Opportunity, $10,000,000 Innovation) and $21,500,000 moved from homeownership to the OakDays and Edes Avenue Homekey conversions. Status at June 30, 2026: 56 rental projects and 4,271 homes committed (1,360 for people exiting homelessness), $414,900,000 of A1 capital committed to rental development, 41 projects with 3,139 homes complete, 15 with 1,132 homes under construction or in predevelopment, $486,800,000 (84%) of the bond spent. Annual Report 4 showed only $30,200,000 of rental development funds uncommitted at mid-2022. Current openings are small Innovation and Opportunity pieces: the Tax-Defaulted Property Development Loan program (up to $3,000,000) and the Emerging Developers Predevelopment Loan program ($7,000,000, awards near $500,000, due Dec 2, 2024). A 2026 Program Income plan sets aside $3,100,000 for a senior ADU program.
- AMI requirement: Households at or below 60% AMI with 20% of A1-assisted units for households at or below 20% AMI; 55-year County regulatory agreement; priority populations include seniors, veterans, people with disabilities, essential workers and people leaving homelessness or incarceration.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Base City and Regional Pool dollars are essentially committed; remaining A1 money is program income and Innovation and Opportunity funds offered through HCD's fall affordable housing RFP and over-the-counter predevelopment loans. Confirm balances with HCD before counting on A1; the County's 2025 Housing Plan is studying a successor revenue measure.)
- Layering notes: A1 was the backbone of East Bay gap financing from 2018 to 2024 and is now largely spent, which is why Measure W capital matters. Loans use the standard HCD terms (3% simple, 55 to 59 years, residual receipts). Many cities still hold uncommitted base allocation balances they control locally (Pass 3), and the County reporting site lists every project by city. Bond proceeds are public funds, so state prevailing wage applies. Figures from measurea1.acgov.org (opened) and Annual Report 4 (opened); the 2017 policies PDF on achcd.org is image-only and was not readable.
- Source: https://measurea1.acgov.org/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Measure W Home Together Fund: Multifamily Homelessness Solutions capital and operating subsidy**
- Agency: Alameda County Housing and Community Development Department (HCD), 224 W. Winton Avenue, Room 108, Hayward, 510-670-6417, HCDRFP@acgov.org, with Alameda County Health, Housing and Homelessness Services (H&H)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Measure W is a half-cent, ten-year general sales tax (Nov 2020) held in escrow until the lawsuit ended in April 2025: $810,000,000 accrued through June 2025 plus about $170,000,000 a year through June 2031. Board framework of July 30, 2025: 80% of ongoing revenue (about $136,000,000 a year) plus $585,800,000 one-time to the Home Together Fund ($1,400,000,000 through 2031), $170,000,000 prudent reserve, 20% to an Essential County Services Fund. FY 2025-26 Home Together Fund allocation $168,900,000 (Housing $96,000,000, Shelter $30,000,000, Access $11,000,000, Prevention $8,000,000, System Foundations $21,000,000); one-time capital acquisition line $180,000,000 for 850 or more housing and shelter units. 2025 New Construction RFP (released Oct 27, 2025, due Dec 8, 2025): about $40,000,000 for capital and operating subsidies; Board awarded $50,000,000 on March 3, 2026 to 10 shovel-ready projects (Liberation Park, 500 Lake Park, 3135 San Pablo, East 12th Circle and Brighter Bancroft Senior in Oakland; Ephesian Legacy Court and People's Park Supportive in Berkeley; 1247 McKay in Alameda; Downtown Livermore, Eden Housing 130 units; Thornton Avenue in Newark, $2,950,000), more than 900 homes with 346 for people exiting homelessness. A separate acquisition and rehabilitation RFP of about $10,000,000 was planned for early 2026, and a one-time $15,000,000 Emergency Stabilization pool for distressed nonprofit owners closed Jan 12, 2026.
- AMI requirement: Assisted units restricted to extremely low income households at or below 30% AMI referred through the Home Together Coordinated Entry System, with supportive services; rents underwritten at the lesser of 50% AMI TCAC rent or 30% of 30% AMI; County regulatory agreement senior to bank documents for 59 years (55 years allowed in tax credit deals).
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: No multifamily RFP open as of Nov 2026. The acquisition and rehabilitation round (about $10,000,000) was expected in early 2026 and the Home Together 2030 plan due summer 2026 will set the next capital rounds; sign up for the HCD developer list. Awards of March 3, 2026 require construction starts within 12 months.)
- Layering notes: The largest new local source in the state outside Los Angeles: a dedicated $1,400,000,000 homelessness fund with a capital line and, unusually, a 15-year capitalized operating subsidy that lets PSH units pencil without PBVs. Loan terms follow Appendix B of the RFP: 3% simple interest, 59-year term, payments amortized, deferred for ELI special needs projects, or from residual receipts with the general partner allowed up to 50% as an incentive management fee. The RFP requires California prevailing wage even where state law would not. The 2026 awards were sized to make 4% and 9% deals and Homekey+ applications competitive, so the realistic ask is $175,000 to $225,000 per homeless unit as last-in money. The services side of the fund (rental subsidies, flexible housing pool, shelter rates) is run by H&H, not HCD. Sources opened: HCD Measure W page, funding opportunities page, the revised RFP PDF, the July 30, 2025 Board presentation and the Home Together FAQ.
- Source: https://www.achcd.org/measure-w-home-together-fund/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Multifamily Housing Revenue Bonds, County of Alameda conduit issuer**
- Agency: Alameda County Housing and Community Development Department (issuer staff) with the Board of Supervisors
- Type: bond
- Typical award: The County issues tax-exempt multifamily housing revenue bonds on request: example, up to $24,000,000 for SAHA's 51-unit 3268 San Pablo Apartments in Oakland (inducement Oct 9, 2018, bond resolution April 23, 2019, CDLAC Resolution 19-011). Issuer fees per that regulatory agreement: an issuance fee at closing and an annual fee of the greater of 0.125% of outstanding principal or $7,500 for the qualified project period. No 2025 or 2026 County bond issuances were located; CMFA issued for Eden Housing in Dublin, RCD in Berkeley and the Housing Authority of the City of Alameda in that period.
- AMI requirement: CDLAC and Section 142(d) minimums (20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI) under a County regulatory agreement; County-financed projects typically carry deeper restrictions.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Tied to CDLAC rounds: TEFRA hearing and inducement resolution by the Board before the CDLAC application, then a bond resolution before closing. Ask HCD whether it will issue; most recent East Bay deals used CMFA or CSCDA.)
- Layering notes: The County will act as issuer for projects it also funds, which keeps monitoring under one regulatory agreement, but it is not an active conduit shop. Bonds alone do not trigger state prevailing wage; County A1, Measure W or HOME loans in the deal do. Terms read from the April 23, 2019 Board letter and regulatory agreement (opened); confirm current fees with HCD.
- Source: https://www.acgov.org/board/bos_calendar/documents/DocsAgendaReg_05_07_19/GENERAL%20ADMINISTRATION/Regular%20Calendar/CDA_279192.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Oakland-Berkeley-Alameda County Continuum of Care (CA-502) and HHAP**
- Agency: Alameda County Health, Housing and Homelessness Services (CoC lead and HHAP administrator for the County and CoC allocations); City of Oakland administers its own HHAP
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HHAP by round, County of Alameda: R1 $8,700,000, R2 $4,000,000, R3 $11,200,000, R4 $12,100,000, R5 $13,463,754, R6 $10,783,302. CoC: R1 $9,400,000, R2 $4,500,000, R3 $12,000,000, R4 $12,900,000, R5 $14,033,347, R6 $11,516,752. City of Oakland: R5 $28,446,566, R6 about $23,000,000; regional R6 total $45,300,000 (awarded March 2, 2026). State accountability site: $210,100,000 HHAP rounds 1 to 5 in the Alameda area, $102,500,000 directly to the County ($78,100,000 obligated, $60,500,000 spent by mid-2025); Encampment Resolution Funding $30,600,000 in the area. HUD CoC program FY 2024 awards to CA-502: $16,901,825 including Shelter Plus Care TRA $9,129,290, Housing Fast Support Network $2,619,550, YHDP $1,934,338 and Linkages $1,734,446. State ESG CoC share $402,650 (2024). 2024 PIT: 9,450 people, 6,343 unsheltered, 9,963 year-round beds. HHAP uses in the County: Coordinated Entry access points, street health and outreach, HMIS, Emerging Leaders, regional interim housing (safe parking, shelters, navigation centers) and local match for Medi-Cal Housing Community Supports.
- AMI requirement: People experiencing or at risk of homelessness per HUD and state definitions; CoC permanent housing through Coordinated Entry; HHAP-6 must obligate 75% and spend 50% by June 30, 2027, and the County needs a compliant housing element for the second half of its Round 6 award.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: HHAP-6 regional application was due Aug 29, 2025 and awarded March 2026; HHAP-7 is available to HHAP-6 grantees. The CoC's HUD NOFO competition runs each summer through EveryOne Home; letters of interest are collected by the County. Home Together 2030 (summer 2026) sets system priorities.)
- Layering notes: HHAP here pays for coordinated entry, outreach, interim housing and services rather than capital; Measure W now dwarfs it and is the County's capital and operating subsidy source for PSH. Oakland and Berkeley projects should look to those cities' HHAP and CoC shares (Pass 3). CoC permanent housing dollars are mostly Shelter Plus Care tenant-based assistance. Figures from the July 17, 2025 County and Oakland HHAP-6 presentation (opened), HCD HHAP allocation lists, the HUD FY 2025 CoC award report and accountability.ca.gov.
- Source: https://everyonehome.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/HHAP-6-Presentation_Alameda-County-City-of-Oakland.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), Alameda County Urban County entitlement**
- Agency: Alameda County Housing and Community Development Department (HCD entitlement grantee for the Urban County)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HCD 2024-2028 Formula Allocation NOFA: Alameda County CY 2024 $655,510, CY 2025 $702,148, five-year estimate $4,096,938; the 2020 entitlement formula amount was $933,865. The County's PLHA Five-Year Plan and the uses of its formula funds were not located on achcd.org.
- AMI requirement: PLHA statute: rental development for households at or below 80% AMI (workforce up to 120% allowed) with 55-year covenants; 2024 NOFA requires 40% of new funds for affordable owner-occupied workforce housing unless the plan is amended.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: HCD formula allocations arrive annually; ask Alameda County HCD how PLHA is programmed and whether it is folded into the fall development RFP. Oakland, Berkeley, Fremont, Hayward and the other entitlement cities receive their own PLHA (Pass 3).)
- Layering notes: Under $1,000,000 a year for the Urban County and not visible as a separate developer source; treat as a possible add-on inside the HCD RFP. State funds, so prevailing wage applies. Amounts from the HCD 2024-2028 PLHA formula NOFA (opened this pass); County plan not found.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/sites/default/files/docs/grants-and-funding/plha/2024-2028-plha-formula-allocation-nofa.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers, Housing Authority of the County of Alameda (HACA)**
- Agency: Housing Authority of the County of Alameda, 22941 Atherton Street, Hayward, 510-538-8876; developer verifications through Jennifer Cado
- Type: other
- Typical award: HACA administers 7,145 vouchers (6,973 leased at July 1, 2026): 6,361 Housing Choice, 425 VASH, 289 Mainstream, 15 EHV. HACA's Administrative Plan (Chapter 17) allows project-basing up to 30% of voucher budget authority, allocated over a multi-year period through RFPs. The November 2021 RFP offered up to 160 PBVs for new construction serving seniors, homeless families and veterans and for existing Homekey projects in Albany, Hayward, Newark and the unincorporated area; selected projects were Downtown Hayward Senior, Mission Paradise (Hayward), Albany Family Apartments, Newark Towne Place Suites, Cherryland Place and Timber Senior Housing (Newark). PBV unit totals are not published.
- AMI requirement: Households at or below 50% AMI at admission (75% at or below 30% AMI); HAP contracts up to 20 years with extension; HACA referral and waitlist rules per the Administrative Plan.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No PBV RFP open as of Nov 2026; the last was Nov 18 to Dec 3, 2021. HACA jurisdiction: Albany, Dublin, Emeryville, Fremont, Hayward, Newark, Pleasanton, San Leandro, Union City and all unincorporated areas; Oakland, Berkeley, Alameda and Livermore have their own housing authorities (Pass 3). HACA will issue verification letters for CTCAC, CDLAC and CalHFA applications on request.)
- Layering notes: HACA PBVs are the operating subsidy for County-funded PSH outside Oakland and Berkeley; Measure W's capitalized operating subsidy reserve is the fallback when PBVs are unavailable. A HAP contract is not a prevailing wage trigger, but Davis-Bacon applies to PBV new construction or rehab of nine or more contract units. HACA is not a Moving to Work agency. Sources opened: HACA developers page, program data at-a-glance, jurisdiction page and the County HCD bulletin announcing the 2021 RFP.
- Source: https://www.haca.net/developers/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Unincorporated inclusionary zoning and ministerial approval ordinances (in development) and state density bonus**
- Agency: Alameda County Community Development Agency, Planning Department (unincorporated area)
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: No county inclusionary ordinance, in-lieu fee or affordable housing impact fee exists today. The Planning Department received $280,000 and hired Street Level Advisors to draft an Inclusionary Zoning Ordinance and a Ministerial Approval Ordinance to meet MTC's Transit-Oriented Communities policy (Bay Fair and Castro Valley BART areas) and Housing Element Program 6.J; draft ordinances for public comment early 2027, adoption recommendations spring 2027, completion by July 1, 2027. Working concepts: rental projects averaging 80% AMI or less (units 30% to 120% AMI), ownership averaging 120% AMI or less, ministerial approval for projects of 11 or more units meeting the set-aside, alternatives of in-lieu fee, off-site units or land dedication. Density bonus follows Government Code 65915 (state law), and the County code already allows ministerial approval for ADUs under 800 square feet.
- AMI requirement: Pending; MTC TOC minimums expected (about 10% to 20% affordable depending on tier). State density bonus units: 55 years for rental.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not yet adopted. Watch the Unincorporated Services Committee and Planning Commission in 2026 and 2027; draft financial feasibility analysis dated July 2026 is posted on the policies page. State density bonus is available now by right.)
- Layering notes: Alameda County unincorporated areas (Ashland, Cherryland, San Lorenzo, Castro Valley, Fairview, Sunol) have no local inclusionary production or fee revenue, so county-controlled affordable housing there depends on A1, Measure W and HACA. Expect an inclusionary requirement on market-rate projects entitled after mid-2027. Density bonus alone does not trigger prevailing wage. Sources opened: County affordable housing production policies page and the Jan 26, 2026 staff report.
- Source: https://www.acgov.org/cda/planning/ahppolicies/index.htm
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption (property tax), Alameda County Assessor**
- Agency: Alameda County Assessor, Phong La, 1221 Oak Street, Room 145, Oakland; Other Exemptions Unit 510-272-6587, exemptions@acgov.org
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Full or partial exemption of property tax on units occupied by households at or below 80% AMI under Revenue and Taxation Code 214(g); worth roughly 1.1% to 1.4% of assessed value a year depending on the tax rate area (Alameda County has high voter-approved debt rates). The Assessor posts the full BOE-267 series (267, 267-L, L1, L2, L3, L4, H, R and income worksheets) and asks claimants to contact the exemptions unit before filing with the parcel number.
- AMI requirement: Nonprofit or LP with eligible nonprofit managing general partner holding a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate (and Supplemental Clearance Certificate for LP low-income housing); units restricted by recorded regulatory agreement or occupied by households at or below 80% AMI; BOE-267 first filing and annual supplemental affidavits by Feb 15 (late claims receive a reduced exemption under Rev. and Tax. Code 270).
- Cycle: rolling (next: 2027-02-15 (annual claim deadline for the 2027-28 roll). File the first claim on acquisition so the construction period is covered.)
- Layering notes: Standard in every tax credit operating budget; underwrite only the restricted units and confirm the managing GP's OCC and SCC before closing. The Assessor's exemptions page (opened) gives the unit contact and forms but no local deadline text; the BOE's Alameda income limit letter governs tenant qualification.
- Source: https://www.acassessor.org/homeowners/decrease-your-assessment/exemptions/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


### Contra Costa County

**Behavioral Health Services Act (BHSA) housing interventions, Contra Costa Behavioral Health**
- Agency: Contra Costa Health, Behavioral Health Services Division (CCBHS), with H3 and DCD for capital awards
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Integrated Plan FY 2026-29 approved by the Board March 17, 2026. Estimated housing intervention funding (BHSA plus MHSA carryover): $41,688,148 in FY 2026-27, $37,553,061 in FY 2027-28, $38,543,498 in FY 2028-29; planned housing expenditures $32,515,162, $35,491,761 and $36,458,704. Capital development share 14.1% in year one (about $4,600,000) then 3.8% and 3.7%; 50% of housing funds dedicated to chronically homeless people; no exemptions requested. First capital awards: $2,250,000 BHSA grant to Village of Hope Pittsburg (Hope Solutions, 15 PSH cottages for transition-age youth, approved June 23, 2026, documents Aug 11, 2026) and BHSA operating and development commitments for Sherman Terrace Homekey+ (five PSH units, Pleasant Hill). No Place Like Home: Legacy Court, Richmond (13 NPLH units, Community Housing Development Corp., MOU Oct 7, 2025).
- AMI requirement: Adults and transition-age youth with serious mental illness or substance use disorder who are chronically homeless, homeless or at risk; Housing First; CCBHS client eligibility and referral. Capital grants carry occupancy preferences (five of 15 units at Village of Hope) and no repayment if the project is completed.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No standing capital NOFA; the two 2026 capital awards were negotiated with CCBHS and brought to the Board. Annual plan updates each spring; ask CCBHS about year two and three capital (about $1,300,000 a year at the planned share) and about project-based operating subsidies and rental subsidies for PSH units.)
- Layering notes: Contra Costa's BHSA housing pot (about $32,000,000 to $36,000,000 a year in spending) is now the largest county-controlled housing source, but most of it is rental subsidies, operating subsidies and services. The capital slice was front-loaded into FY 2026-27 and mostly committed (Village of Hope, Sherman Terrace). The practical ask for a PSH sponsor is a BHSA operating or rental subsidy commitment plus services, layered on HACCC PBVs and Measure X or HOME capital. State funds, so capital grants trigger prevailing wage. Plan read from the Board attachment (CCC Integrated Plan 2.27.26) on Legistar.
- Source: https://contra-costa.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=17141&GUID=8EE28BF6-DB8D-4F15-8221-F8E8DE37EA16&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Contra Costa County Affordable Housing Program: HOME and CDBG-Housing (annual NOFA)**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development (DCD), Housing and Community Improvement Division, 30 Muir Road, Martinez; Kristin Sherk, Principal Planner, 925-655-2889, dcd.housing@dcd.cccounty.us
- Type: loan
- Typical award: FY 2026/27 Board award (May 19, 2026): $4,294,840 CDBG-Housing and $3,500,000 HOME across five projects, including $2,000,000 HOME to Lakeside Recapitalization (RCD, Concord, 122 units) and $2,500,000 CDBG plus $1,500,000 HOME to Trinity Plaza (Eden and CHDC, Richmond, 65 senior units). FY 2025/26: $4,889,000 CDBG and $3,456,343 HOME plus $2,550,000 recaptured HOME-ARP. FY 2027/28 NOFA (released Sept 15, 2026) estimates up to $14,000,000 across CDBG-Housing, HOME and Measure X. FY 2026/27 Action Plan expected resources: CDBG $4,724,092 annual allocation plus $1,000,000 program income; HOME $2,927,850 plus $400,000 program income. ESG $384,307 (services and shelter only).
- AMI requirement: 55-year County Regulatory Agreement (57 years from loan documents if the occupancy date cannot be established). County policy goal: 100% of County-assisted units at or below 50% AMI and 10% at or below 30% AMI; HOME units at HOME rent limits. 15% of Consortium HOME must go to CHDO-sponsored projects; CDBG new construction only through a qualified CBDO.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026-12-07 (FY 2027/28 applications due 5:00 PM via City Data Services). NOFA released Sept 15, 2026; application live Oct 15, 2026; TA meeting Oct 21, 2026; technical questions due Nov 23, 2026; AHFC meets April to May 2027; Board approves May to June 2027. Prior cycle: FY 2026/27 applications were due Dec 11, 2025.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. Standard terms: 3% simple interest, 55-year term, annual payments from residual receipts shared 75% to soft lenders pro rata and 25% to the borrower, special payment from 75% of cost savings, due on sale or refinance; the County will not cut its rate below the home city's rate. Davis-Bacon applies to HOME projects with 12 or more HOME-assisted units and CDBG projects with eight or more units; state prevailing wage is the developer's responsibility to determine but applies to County loans as public funds. Build America Buy America applies to CDBG and HOME projects over $250,000. The County is the HUD entitlement for the Urban County (all of the county except Antioch, Concord, Pittsburg and Walnut Creek, which have their own CDBG) and lead of the Contra Costa HOME Consortium, so HOME can be used countywide including inside those four cities. Awards are sized by the HOME per-unit subsidy table and used as gap money for 4% and 9% tax credit deals; the FY 2026/27 Trinity Plaza and Lakeside awards are both acquisition-rehab recapitalizations. HOME-ARP ($12,108,330 allocation) is closed to new applications. The County staff report notes awards are made contingent on other commitments by Dec 31 of the following year, with recapture if unmet.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/8695/Federal-Programs-HOME-and-CDBG
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Development fee waiver for affordable housing (no countywide program; discretionary under 822-4.418)**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: None adopted. The County has no standing impact fee waiver or deferral program for affordable housing. Chapter 822-4.418(c) lists fee deferrals or waivers among incentives the County may offer to projects providing inclusionary units, and County loans may be used to pay permit fees. No fee waiver awards appeared in 2025 or 2026 Board records.
- AMI requirement: Discretionary; would follow the inclusionary or density bonus agreement for the project.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not applicable. Request fee deferral in the inclusionary housing plan or density bonus application and ask DCD about paying fees from the County loan.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. Negative finding recorded so the county does not look thin. Budget full County and special district fees (Contra Costa Fire, East Bay Regional Park, school, traffic mitigation) in unincorporated deals. Labor Code 1720(b)(4) treats waived or reduced fees as public funds, so any waiver that is granted could itself trigger state prevailing wage.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/8544/County-Ordinances-for-Affordable-Housing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Housing Successor Low and Moderate Income Housing Asset Fund and surplus land (former Contra Costa County Redevelopment Agency)**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development, as Housing Successor
- Type: other
- Typical award: Housing Fund balance about $8,700,000 at June 30, 2025, of which about $6,600,000 is pre-dissolution bond proceeds restricted to affordable housing; FY 2024-25 revenue $756,175 from loan payments, interest and land sales; $382,774 spent on monitoring and administration. The County holds former redevelopment land in Bay Point (near Pittsburg/Bay Point BART), North Richmond and Rodeo and solicits developers through RFPs (Heritage Point, 42 units, and Bay Point Senior, Eden Housing, 80 units, came from these parcels). No County loans between the Successor Agency and the County remain outstanding.
- AMI requirement: Health and Safety Code 34176.1: funds for households at or below 80% AMI with at least 30% for extremely low income over each five-year period; land disposed for affordable housing with recorded covenants (55 years rental).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Watch the County's Development Opportunities page and Board agendas for Bay Point and Rodeo parcel RFPs; the FY 2024-25 Housing Successor report (SB 341) was filed in 2026 and the next is due with the FY 2025-26 financial report.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. Land is the main asset: County-owned sites in Bay Point and Rodeo have been offered at nominal cost with the County as a co-applicant for state funds, and sale proceeds are recycled into affordable housing. The $6,600,000 in old bond proceeds is a one-time pot that could fund site work or gap loans on those parcels. Public land and funds trigger state prevailing wage.
