Report read from the County DocumentCenter (Contra Costa County Housing Successor Report 2024-25).
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/DocumentCenter/View/90514/Contra-Costa-County-Housing-Successor-Report-2024-25-PDF
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (Ch. 822-4) and Residential Density Bonus Ordinance (Ch. 822-2)**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development, Community Development Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Inclusionary: 15% of units in unincorporated projects of five or more units must be affordable (rental: 20% of those at very low income, remainder lower income; for-sale: 20% lower, remainder moderate), 55-year rental term, with in-lieu fee, off-site units or land dedication as alternatives. Density bonus: up to 15% for providing the required inclusionary units (822-2.402), with state law bonuses under Government Code 65915 for units beyond the requirement, land donation and child care, and incentives or concessions per 65915(d)(2). Incentives for inclusionary compliance (822-4.418(c)) may include fee deferrals or waivers, housing set-aside funds, tax-exempt financing and zoning modifications at County discretion.
- AMI requirement: Inclusionary units: 55 years for rentals; for-sale units owner-occupied with resale controls and County recapture. Density bonus target units: 30 years or longer if required by financing (822-2.410); density bonus housing agreement recorded against the property.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Not applicable; applies by right to unincorporated projects at entitlement. Developer agreements are approved by the Board (for example Grayson Road and Walnut Estates density bonus and inclusionary agreements, April 2026). Confirm current code text with DCD; the County code is hosted on Municode (blocked to automated access), and the mirror read for this record was Zoneomics.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. The ordinance text itself was read on the Zoneomics mirror of the County Ordinance Code. In-lieu fees from this chapter feed the County's In-Lieu Fee Fund (separate record). A 100% affordable project is exempt from the inclusionary requirement and takes the state density bonus and concessions directly; the County's density bonus submittal checklist and 2025 rent maximums are on the County ordinances page. In-lieu funds cannot be used for units that satisfy inclusionary or density bonus obligations. Density bonus alone does not trigger prevailing wage.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/8544/County-Ordinances-for-Affordable-Housing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance In-Lieu Fee Fund (Chapter 822-4)**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development
- Type: loan
- Typical award: FY 2026/27: $1,216,295 available; awards $1,000,000 to Orbisonia Village (Pacific West, Bay Point, 167 units) and $216,295 to Harbor Pointe (USA Properties, Discovery Bay, 168 units). FY 2025/26: $1,054,134 awarded. Fees come from unincorporated projects of five or more units that pay in lieu of building 15% affordable units; the fee is set annually by the Board (for rentals, the 55-year gap between average two-bedroom rent and the affordable rent; for sale units, the gap between the affordable price and the countywide median sale price).
- AMI requirement: New construction for households up to 80% AMI, unincorporated county only; preference for two-bedroom and larger units and for projects exceeding the inclusionary minimum. Cannot fund units required by density bonus or inclusionary obligations. Forgivable low-interest loan reconveyed after five years in service as affordable housing.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026-12-07 (included in the County housing NOFA when a balance exists; the FY 2027/28 NOFA names CDBG-Housing, HOME and Measure X, so confirm an in-lieu balance with DCD). FY 2026/27 awards required commitments by July 1, 2026 (Orbisonia) and Dec 31, 2027 (Harbor Pointe).)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. Small and lumpy, about $1,000,000 to $1,200,000 a year, and limited to unincorporated areas (Bay Point, Discovery Bay, North Richmond, Rodeo, El Sobrante, Alamo). It was used as last-dollar construction money alongside Measure X and PLHA in Orbisonia Village, which also needed a July 1, 2026 close for its 4% credits. Public funds, so prevailing wage applies. The fee calculator and 2025 rent maximums are posted on the County ordinances page.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/8702/State-and-Local-Funds
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Measure X Housing Fund (affordable housing development share)**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development (75% share); Health, Housing and Homeless Services (H3) administers the 25% services share
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Measure X is a countywide half-cent sales tax (Nov 2020, 20 years, began April 1, 2021). The Board committed $10,000,000 in year one and about $12,000,000 a year after; the County's Measure X allocation table shows the Local Housing Trust Fund line at $12,600,000 (FY 2023-24), $12,902,400 (FY 2024-25), $13,156,577 (FY 2025-26) and $13,306,957 (FY 2026-27), with a 20-year total near $258,000,000. Capital share available through the housing NOFA: $9,707,500 in FY 2025/26 (including $2,000,000 recaptured from Anton San Ramon) and $7,880,690 in FY 2026/27. FY 2026/27 awards: $5,380,690 to 949 Moraga Road (SAHA and Sunflower Hill, Lafayette, 47 IDD units) and $2,500,000 to Rumrill Commons (Novin, San Pablo, 39 units). Earlier: $5,000,000 to El Cerrito Plaza Parcel A South (Related, 69 units), $4,968,158 to Choice in Aging Senior Housing (SAHA, Pleasant Hill, 82 units, matched with $2,963,384 state LHTF), $1,770,958 to Village of Hope Pittsburg (Hope Solutions, 15 PSH cottages). Minimums: $500,000 for capital projects, $250,000 for renovation and microhome projects, $25,000 to $500,000 for predevelopment with no more than $1,000,000 a year for predevelopment (Board, Sept 16, 2025).
- AMI requirement: Priority to households at or below 50% AMI (designated target population); up to 80% AMI eligible. Rental loans 55 years; homeownership 20 years or more (80% AMI, 81% to 120% allowed). Grants only for pilot, innovation and capacity-building projects.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026-12-07 (FY 2027/28 NOFA with CDBG-Housing and HOME, applications due 5:00 PM). Loan agreement required within two years of contingent award; work must start within one year of loan execution; grants spent within two years.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. Largest discretionary local source in the 12 target counties. Same 3% simple, 55-year residual receipts terms as HOME, but no federal cross-cutting rules, so it is the preferred fill for projects inside cities without their own gap funds and for IDD, senior and farmworker deals. Predevelopment loans are not forgivable and must be repaid even if the project dies. The County used Measure X as the local match to win a $2,963,384 state Local Housing Trust Fund grant (FY 2024/25) and as match for Homekey awards (El Portal Place, $5,200,000). The 25% H3 share (about $4,068,983 in FY 2026/27) funds services, prevention and legal aid through a separate two-year RFP and is not capital. Awards are contingent on other commitments (Dec 31, 2027 for FY 2026/27 awards) and executed loans by June 30, 2028; the County recaptured $2,000,000 when Anton San Ramon stalled and pulled Harbor Pointe's Measure X request in May 2026. Measure X is County General Fund money, so state prevailing wage applies.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/8750/Measure-X-Housing-Fund
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Multifamily Housing Revenue Bonds, County of Contra Costa conduit issuer**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development, bond administration (Kristin Sherk, 925-655-2889)
- Type: bond
- Typical award: Active issuer. Board approvals: El Cerrito Plaza Parcel A South, up to $35,700,000 tax-exempt plus $10,000,000 taxable (Res. 2025-383, Nov 4, 2025, JPMorgan private placement, 6.824% TIC); Lakeside Apartments, Concord, up to $30,000,000 for RCD's 124-unit acquisition-rehab (Res. 2026-59, March 3, 2026; CDLAC allocation in hand, sale resolution expected May 2026); Res. 2026-196, up to $15,075,837 for a 124-unit acquisition-rehab (May 19, 2026); Res. 2025-362, $29,238,977 tax-exempt plus $8,000,000 taxable (Oct 21, 2025); Res. 2025-196, up to $40,000,000 for an 84-unit development (June 10, 2025); Res. 2025-117, up to $50,000,000 for 70 units (April 15, 2025). Fees per the 2018 Debt Policy appendix: $2,500 application fee, issuance fee at closing, annual fee of 0.125% of outstanding principal (minimum $5,000, may be subordinated) for the 55-year regulatory agreement term.
- AMI requirement: CDLAC and Section 142(d) minimums; County regulatory agreement typically runs 55 years. Recent deals are 100% affordable at 30% to 60% AMI. Projects must be in the unincorporated area or in a city that approves the County as issuer (cities often use CMFA or CSCDA instead).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Tied to CDLAC rounds. The program web page still says the County is not currently accepting applications, but the Board approved six bond resolutions between January 2025 and May 2026; the FY 2026/27 guidelines (section 9.4) direct any County-funded project seeking CDLAC bonds to contact DCD bond staff about the County acting as issuer. Allow for a TEFRA hearing and inducement resolution before the CDLAC deadline.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. The County issues for projects it also lends to (El Cerrito Plaza, Lakeside), which keeps one regulatory agreement and one monitor; bonds are payable solely from project revenue with no County pledge and the sponsor reimburses TEFRA and approval costs. The 0.125% annual issuer fee is in line with CMFA and CSCDA. Tax-exempt bonds alone do not trigger state prevailing wage, but County, HOME or Measure X loans in the same deal do, and Davis-Bacon follows HOME and CDBG. Program overview and 2018 policy appendix are on the County DocumentCenter.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/8694/Multiple-Family-Mortgage-Revenue-Bond
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), Contra Costa Urban County entitlement**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development (HCD entitlement grantee for the Urban County)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HCD 2024-2028 Formula Allocation NOFA: Contra Costa County CY 2024 $1,523,318 and CY 2025 $1,631,698; five-year estimate $13,021,068 per the amended Five-Year Plan (Resolution 2026-406, Oct 20, 2026). FY 2025/26 allocation $1,860,477 with $1,767,453 to projects after 5% administration; FY 2024/25 award $1,876,423 to El Cerrito Plaza Parcel A South; FY 2022/23 award $1,000,000 to Choice in Aging Senior Housing and $1,650,000 to Rumrill Commons. Earlier HCD awards: $3,373,129 (2022) and $2,170,178. Antioch, Concord, Pittsburg and Walnut Creek receive separate PLHA entitlements (Pass 3).
- AMI requirement: Activity 1: rental development for extremely low to low income households as low-interest deferred loans with a 55-year regulatory agreement, priority to units at or below 60% AMI, awards counted on units at 80% AMI or below. The 2024 HCD NOFA requires 40% of funds for affordable owner-occupied workforce housing (Activity 9: down payment assistance, homeowner rehab, 80% to 120% AMI).
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Rental PLHA is awarded through the County housing NOFA in years with a balance; the FY 2027/28 NOFA (due 2026-12-07) lists CDBG-Housing, HOME and Measure X only, so ask DCD whether PLHA will be added. HCD formula allocations arrive annually.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. Roughly $1,500,000 to $1,900,000 a year, now split with 40% to homeownership under the state's 2024 workforce housing rule, so the rental share is under $1,000,000 a year. Used as a companion to Measure X in El Cerrito Plaza (7 PLHA units of 69) and Choice in Aging. State funds with County loan terms, so prevailing wage applies. State regulations require commitment and occupancy deadlines (Choice in Aging PLHA must be occupied by 2030). The County's PLHA Five-Year Plan narrative (Sept 2025) is attached to Resolution 2026-406 on Legistar.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/8702/State-and-Local-Funds
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers, Housing Authority of the County of Contra Costa (HACCC)**
- Agency: Housing Authority of the County of Contra Costa, 3133 Estudillo Street, Martinez; Tony Ucciferri 925-957-8055 (PBV), Robert Moore, Director of Development, 925-957-8025
- Type: other
- Typical award: HACCC administers about 9,500 Housing Choice Voucher and CoC households and owns 963 public housing units plus two tax credit developments (2025 RFP). Most recent PBV invitation for proposals (REQ-12092025, released Dec 8, 2025, due Jan 8, 2026): up to 15 units for existing properties needing no more than $3,000 per unit of rehab, aimed at properties converting from CoC project-based rental assistance to PBVs. PBV waiting lists opened for partner properties in June to August 2025, Oct 27 to Nov 13, 2025 and March 2 to 19, 2026. Public housing repositioning underway (Las Deltas main campus developer RFQ due Feb 4, 2025; repositioning financial and legal consultant RFPs in 2025 and 2026).
- AMI requirement: Households at or below 50% AMI at admission (75% at or below 30% AMI); HAP contracts up to 20 years with extensions. Awards subject to HUD PBV caps and long-term affordability. Jurisdiction is the whole county except Pittsburg; the Richmond Housing Authority and Pittsburg Housing Authority serve their cities (Pass 3).
- Cycle: rolling (next: No open PBV solicitation as of Nov 2026; the last closed Jan 8, 2026 and targeted CoC PBRA conversions. HACCC also offers a Mixed Finance capital loan with operating subsidy, sale of units to HACCC, and long-term Property Rental Assistance contracts for developer-owned units (competitive selection required for mixed finance). Register on the Housing Agency Marketplace e-procurement system for notices.)
- Layering notes: PBVs are the operating subsidy of choice for Contra Costa PSH and CoC-funded buildings, and the 2025 solicitation shows HACCC converting CoC rental assistance to PBVs to free CoC dollars. Underwrite PBV rents at the HAP contract rent; a PBV HAP contract is not a prevailing wage trigger, but Davis-Bacon applies to PBV new construction or rehab of nine or more contract units. HACCC is not a Moving to Work agency. Public housing repositioning (Las Deltas in North Richmond, El Pueblo, Bayo Vista) may produce RAD or Section 18 PBV units for partner developers. Sources opened: HACCC closed solicitations page and the Dec 2025 PBV advertisement PDF; the repositioning RFP PDF supplied the voucher and unit counts.
- Source: https://contracostaha.org/closed-solicitations/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Richmond/Contra Costa County Continuum of Care (CA-505) and HHAP**
- Agency: Contra Costa Health, Health, Housing and Homeless Services Division (H3), CoC lead and HHAP grantee; Contra Costa Council on Homelessness is the CoC board
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HHAP Round 6: $6,708,895 (County $3,244,119 plus CoC $3,464,775), state agreement ratified May 12, 2026, term through June 30, 2030. HHAP Round 5: $6,683,382 (County $3,272,469, CoC $3,410,913), agreement approved March 11, 2025. State accountability site: $28,900,000 in HHAP rounds 1 to 5 awarded to the Contra Costa area, $18,300,000 spent by June 2025; Encampment Resolution Funding $5,700,000 to the County. HUD CoC program FY 2024 awards to CA-505: $11,522,492, including $8,638,557 for Contra Costa tenant-based rental assistance and $1,960,996 for ACCESS. State ESG CoC allocation $304,250 (2024). Homekey history: Delta Landing, Pittsburg (172 interim units, $21,500,000 Homekey), El Portal Place, San Pablo (54 PSH micro-units, $16,000,000 Homekey plus $5,200,000 Measure X). Homekey+: $1,500,000 standard agreement for five PSH units at County-owned Sherman Terrace, Pleasant Hill (Oct 6, 2026); $30,000,000 joint application with SAHA for Choice in Aging Senior Housing (June 9, 2026).
- AMI requirement: Households experiencing or at risk of homelessness per HUD and state definitions; CoC permanent housing requires Coordinated Entry referrals. HHAP Round 6 must obligate 75% and spend 50% by June 30, 2027.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 CoC NOFO priority listing approved Aug 6, 2026 (HUD awards expected winter 2026-27); the Council on Homelessness runs the local competition each summer. HHAP is allocated by state round with no local NOFA; H3 contracts HHAP to operators (Delta Landing, Hope Solutions, BACS, Catholic Charities) through Board-approved contracts.)
- Layering notes: HHAP money in Contra Costa goes to interim housing operations, rapid rehousing and prevention, not capital, so treat H3 as the services and operating partner for PSH rather than a gap lender. CoC TBRA ($8,600,000 a year) is being converted to HACCC project-based vouchers where possible, which is how new PSH buildings get a long-term subsidy. Homekey and Homekey+ awards have run through H3 for County-owned sites and through DCD for developer sites. The Council on Homelessness agendas (contra-costa.legistar.com) carry the CoC NOFO schedule. Figures from the May 12, 2026 Board report (file 26-1970), HCD HHAP award lists, the HUD FY 2025 CoC award report and accountability.ca.gov.
- Source: https://contra-costa.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=18435&GUID=46CBC2FD-FCDD-474F-880A-0CD959112973&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Summer Lake Affordable Housing Fund (east county)**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Department of Conservation and Development
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Balance of $913,438 as of 2026, fed by a $3,333 affordable housing fee on each new Summer Lake home collected by the City of Oakley and transferred to the County under the Cypress Corridor annexation Transition Agreement. FY 2026/27 awards: $413,438 construction loan to Harbor Pointe (Discovery Bay, 168 units) and $500,000 predevelopment loan to Casita Phase II (Hijas del Campo, Byron, 17 manufactured farmworker units) at 3% simple interest with a five-year term to start construction.
- AMI requirement: Affordable housing in east Contra Costa County; the agreement asks the County to consider reserving 20% for projects in Oakley. Awarded through the County Affordable Housing Program, so County loan terms (55 years, 3% simple, residual receipts) apply to construction loans.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026-12-07 (through the County housing NOFA when a balance exists). Casita Phase II predevelopment loan must be executed by June 30, 2028.)
- Layering notes: A geographic set-aside, not a standing program, but it is real money for Oakley, Brentwood, Discovery Bay, Byron and Bay Point projects and the County used it in 2026 to fill requests that Measure X could not. Public funds, so prevailing wage applies. Documented in the May 19, 2026 Board report (file 26-2203) read through the Legistar API.
- Source: https://contra-costa.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=18528&GUID=B677C887-8ECE-4F9B-9304-3E2A7F242109&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption (property tax), Contra Costa County Assessor**
- Agency: Contra Costa County Assessor, Gus Kramer, 2530 Arnold Drive Suite 100, Martinez, 925-313-7400
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Full or partial exemption of property tax on units occupied by households at or below 80% AMI (Rev. and Tax. Code 214(g)); worth roughly 1.1% or more of assessed value a year depending on the tax rate area (1% base rate plus voter-approved debt). File early; supplemental affidavits are required for every claim year.
- AMI requirement: Nonprofit or LP with eligible nonprofit managing general partner holding a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate; units restricted by recorded regulatory agreement or occupied by households at or below 80% AMI; BOE-267 first filing and BOE-267-L, L1 or L2 supplemental affidavits annually by Feb 15 (late claims receive a reduced exemption under Rev. and Tax. Code 270).
- Cycle: rolling (next: 2027-02-15 (annual claim deadline for the 2027-28 roll). First-year claims can be filed on acquisition; construction-period exemption is available under 214(g) once the OCC is in hand.)
- Layering notes: County pages on www.contracosta.ca.gov refuse automated fetches (403); the same pages and DocumentCenter PDFs were opened on 2026-11-06 through the site's CivicPlus origin host (ca-contracostacounty3.civicplus.com), and Board reports were read through the contra-costa.legistar.com API. Standard in every tax credit operating budget; underwrite the exemption only for the restricted units and confirm the managing GP's OCC before closing. The Assessor's site has no dedicated welfare exemption page; forms are under the Assessor's Forms link and the BOE county contacts page lists the office. The BOE's assessment practices survey of Contra Costa recommended tighter handling of late-filing provisions, so expect staff to apply the statutory late penalties strictly.
- Source: https://www.contracosta.ca.gov/191/Assessor
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


### El Dorado County

**Affordable Housing Density Bonus (Zoning Ordinance Chapter 130.31) and the Affordable Housing Ordinance in development (inclusionary with in-lieu fee)**
- Agency: El Dorado County Planning and Building Department, Planning Division (530-621-5355), with the Affordable Housing Task Force (established April 2, 2024, Resolution 046-2024)
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State Density Bonus Law (Government Code 65915) as implemented in Chapter 130.31: up to 50% density bonus for the standard affordability tiers, up to 80% or unlimited density for 100% affordable projects, plus concessions, waivers and reduced parking. No local bonus beyond state law was found.
- AMI requirement: State tiers: 5% very low, 10% low, 10% moderate for-sale, or 100% affordable. The draft Affordable Housing Ordinance presented to the Task Force October 14, 2026 would require, for West Slope residential projects of 5 or more units or lots, 10% of rental units at 80% AMI for 55 years or 10% of for-sale units at 120% AMI for 45 years, with alternatives (in-lieu fee, off-site units, land dedication, conversion of market-rate units) and exemptions including deed-restricted affordable projects, mixed-use projects that are two-thirds or more residential, shelters and transitional housing, and projects where 51% or more of units are 1,600 square feet or less.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Density bonus is by right with the planning application. Resolution of Intention 163-2025 (November 4, 2025) started the Chapter 130.31 amendments; the Task Force's fee nexus study consultant was being selected in October 2026 and the ordinance and in-lieu fee will return to the Board after the study. No adoption date set.)
- Layering notes: Entitlement tools, not money. Diamond Village II (31 units) was approved under SB 35 by the Planning Commission in January 2025, which shows the ministerial path works in the unincorporated county. Watch the inclusionary ordinance: a 100% affordable project is exempt, but mixed-income workforce projects on the West Slope would pick up a 10% at 80% AMI obligation or an in-lieu fee once adopted, and the Task Force is also studying Community Facilities Districts and Enhanced Infrastructure Financing Districts as infrastructure tools. Staff flagged litigation risk around inclusionary exactions. The Tahoe Basin portion of the county is also subject to TRPA allocations and coverage rules, which this ordinance would not cover (West Slope only).
- Source: https://eldorado.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=39161&GUID=DFA60236-9A16-41B1-9531-7361F8A62AB9&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Affordable Housing Trust Fund and Affordable Housing Special Revenue Fund (no development NOFA)**
- Agency: El Dorado County Planning and Building Department, Affordable Housing Unit (program administration contracted to Housing Group LLC dba HousingINC through December 31, 2026); Housing@edcgov.us
- Type: other
- Typical award: Small and discretionary. The Affordable Housing Special Revenue Fund held about $600,151 in April 2026, fed by building permit activity, and the Board committed $550,151 of it to the Caldor Fire Grizzly Flats Homeowner Rebuild Program (forgivable loans up to $73,318 for about 12 fire-displaced households, administered by HomeAid Sacramento). The separate Affordable Housing Trust Fund receives the County's share of CMFA bond issuance fees (for example $6,000 from the Diamond Village II issuance). No multifamily development award from either fund was found.
- AMI requirement: None codified. Staff describe the Special Revenue Fund as having no specific restrictions, with use at the Board's discretion for affordable housing purposes. The Trust Fund is designated for studies, program development or contributions toward creating housing.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA. A request would go through the Affordable Housing Unit and require Board action.)
- Layering notes: Do not underwrite County gap money in El Dorado. The county has no inclusionary fee revenue yet, no housing bond and no sales tax measure, so the Trust Fund is capitalized only by occasional conduit issuance fees and permit-related deposits. The planned Affordable Housing Ordinance (inclusionary with an in-lieu fee set by a fee nexus study now being procured) is intended to expand the Trust Fund, which is the only path to a real county gap source in the next few years. County housing staff capacity is thin: a single Administrative Analyst position was reinstated in April 2026 and HousingINC runs the program in the interim, so allow time for any County action (Board Policy A-6 resolutions, TIF offsets, TEFRA hearings).
- Source: https://eldorado.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=40257&GUID=FB19D33E-5F00-40BD-BC9C-519FBDAA8C4D&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Behavioral Health Services Act (BHSA) housing interventions, El Dorado County HHSA Behavioral Health**
- Agency: El Dorado County Health and Human Services Agency, Behavioral Health Division (Integrated Plan FY 2026-27 through 2028-29 adopted June 23, 2026)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Housing Interventions budget about $4,050,000 per year (30% component). Capital Development Projects line $500,000 in FY 2026-27 and $500,000 in FY 2027-28 (about 12% of housing interventions, under the 25% cap), both for the Forni Road Housing Services Site (county-owned interim housing with temporary tiny homes and a congregate services building, partly funded from Capital Facilities and Technological Needs). Remaining housing dollars go to rental subsidies, operating subsidies (about 15 people), a Flexible Housing Subsidy Pool, transitional rent and outreach and engagement.
- AMI requirement: BHSA eligible population: adults with serious mental illness or substance use disorder who are chronically homeless, homeless or at risk of homelessness; at least 50% of housing interventions for chronically homeless people; no more than 25% of housing interventions for capital.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Capital line is fully committed to the County's own Forni Road site for FY 2026-27 and 2027-28; no developer NOFA. The Integrated Plan can be revised annually (draft due to DHCS each March 31) and the next three-year plan starts FY 2029-30.)
- Layering notes: Unlike larger counties, El Dorado's BHSA capital is reserved for a county-owned interim housing campus, so a developer cannot expect BHSA gap funding for PSH before FY 2029-30. The operating subsidy and Flex Pool lines are the realistic ask: a PSH project with units set aside for BHSA clients could request a multi-year rental or operating subsidy agreement and services contract from Behavioral Health, which also strengthens an MHP or NPLH-style supportive housing application. BHSA money in a project is state funding and triggers prevailing wage. Contact Behavioral Health early in the annual update cycle (fall) to be included in the following year's plan.
- Source: https://www.eldoradocounty.ca.gov/files/assets/county/v/1/documents/health-amp-wellbeing/mental-health/draft-eldoradocounty_2026-2029_integratedplan-web-version-1.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**County development fee waivers and deferrals for affordable housing beyond the TIF offset (none found)**
- Agency: El Dorado County Planning and Building Department
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: None. The County has no general affordable housing fee waiver or reimbursement ordinance; the TIF offset (Policy B-14) is the only fee incentive. The housing website lists a Building Fee Deferral Program, but its page returned 404 on November 6, 2026 and its terms (likely a general deferral of building permit fees to occupancy, not an affordable housing waiver) could not be confirmed. School, fire and other agency impact fees are not waived: Racquet Way Phase 2 (24 units) still owed about $87,360 to the Office of Education and $36,240 to the fire district after a 50% TIF offset was recommended.
- AMI requirement: Not applicable.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not applicable. The Affordable Housing Task Force's Phase 2 tools work could add fee incentives; none proposed as of October 2026.)
- Layering notes: Negative finding recorded so the fee line is budgeted in full. Use the full 2025 TIF schedule less the B-14 offset on income-restricted units, plus full school, fire and County building and planning fees. Confirm whether the Building Fee Deferral Program still exists and its terms with the Planning and Building Department (Housing@edcgov.us) before relying on a deferral in the construction period cash flow.
- Source: https://www.eldoradocounty.ca.gov/Services/HomebuyerRehab-Loans-Affordable-Housing-Development/Developers/Developer-Programs/Collaborative-Opportunities
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**El Dorado Opportunity Knocks Continuum of Care (CA-525) and HHAP, El Dorado County region**
- Agency: El Dorado County HHSA as Administrative Entity for HHAP and as Collaborative Applicant (through the County PHA) for the EDOK CoC; edokcoc.org; Front Door access 530-600-2822
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HHAP Round 6: $670,181.51 accepted June 23, 2026 (County $324,069.60, CoC $346,111.91; HCD agreement 10349 through June 30, 2030). HHAP rounds 1-5 totaled about $5.4 million to the County and CoC, with $5.3 million obligated and $2.7 million spent per the state accountability dashboard. FY 2026 HUD CoC renewal competition: $160,797. State ESG 2024: $359,633 to the region. 2024 point-in-time count 284 people (135 unsheltered), 416 beds.
- AMI requirement: HHAP: people experiencing or at risk of homelessness; HHAP-6 requires a regionally coordinated homelessness action plan, encampment policy and Housing Element compliance plan. HUD CoC: Permanent Supportive Housing and Rapid Rehousing for homeless households.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: HHAP-6 is committed to bridging Navigation Center operations during its wind-down and the gap before the treatment-focused campus at 300 Forni Road opens; no competitive local HHAP NOFA. HUD CoC competition annually (FY 2026 round applied for in 2026). HHAP Round 7 timing depends on the state budget.)
- Layering notes: The region is small and the money is spoken for: HHAP has historically funded the 60-bed Navigation Center (operated by Volunteers of America) and the County is pivoting to a treatment-based interim housing campus at 300 Forni Road. A permanent supportive housing developer would use the CoC for Coordinated Entry referrals and possibly a HUD CoC PSH renewal or new project, rather than county HHAP capital. HHAP-6 dollars could fund rental assistance or operating subsidy for PSH units if HHSA agrees, but the staff report commits them to interim housing and transition services. Coordinate with HHSA Community Services, not Planning and Building, for anything homelessness-related.
- Source: https://eldorado.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=40595&GUID=6CB4D494-8396-4594-BEB7-033F9E02BA35&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), El Dorado County formula allocation**
- Agency: El Dorado County Health and Human Services Agency (HHSA), Community Services Division; hhsa-grants@edcgov.us
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2019-2023 five-year estimate $2,990,478 (Resolution 143-2025, October 21, 2025; years 1-3 were $479,995, $729,477 and $973,923). HCD's 2024-2028 formula NOFA lists El Dorado County at $308,950 (2024), $318,390 (2025) and a five-year estimate of $1,930,938. All of it is programmed to homelessness operations, not development.
- AMI requirement: PLHA guidelines allow rental housing at or below 60% AMI (up to 80% AMI) with 55-year covenants, but the County's plan directs the funds to operating the navigation center and interim housing services rather than capital.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not available to developers. The 2024 plan amendment moved all PLHA to a single capital activity for a permanent navigation center, and in 2025 staff said the Forni Road housing services site will likely not use PLHA capital, so the money is being reallocated to navigation center operations. A new five-year plan for the 2024-2028 allocations is required by HCD and could revisit uses.)
- Layering notes: El Dorado's PLHA is about $300,000 to $1,000,000 a year and is entirely absorbed by homeless services, so there is no PLHA capital for multifamily deals in the unincorporated county. The only PLHA capital nearby came through the City of Placerville's competitive PLHA award (Middletown Apartments, about $1,050,000), which is a Pass 3 item. If the County restructures its 2024-2028 PLHA plan, a developer could ask HHSA to carve out a capital or operating subsidy line for a permanent supportive housing project; HHSA, not Planning and Building, controls PLHA here.
- Source: https://eldorado.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=39157&GUID=A63338BA-252F-4735-A14B-8E5F3415DC0D&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers and Housing Choice Vouchers, El Dorado County Public Housing Authority (HHSA)**
- Agency: El Dorado County Public Housing Authority, a division of HHSA (Board of Supervisors is the governing board); 530-621-6300; 330 Fair Lane, Placerville (moving to 768 Pleasant Valley Road, Diamond Springs, effective May 20, 2026)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Small agency: 417 Housing Choice Vouchers, no public housing, qualified PHA exempt from annual plans. The PHA conducted a competitive Project-Based Voucher selection process in the 2020-2025 plan period and used PBVs with special-purpose vouchers to support the El Dorado Haven development (No Place Like Home units). Special-purpose vouchers: VASH, Mainstream, FUP, FYI; Emergency Housing Vouchers are winding down. No PBV RFP open as of November 6, 2026.
- AMI requirement: HCV eligibility at 50% AMI or below (75% of admissions at 30% AMI); PBV HAP contracts up to 20 years with extensions. HCV waitlist has been closed since 2016.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No open PBV competition. The Five-Year PHA Plan for 2026-2030 (adopted September 29, 2026) continues PBV use; a developer should contact the PHA to request a PBV RFP or a HUD-allowed non-competitive award tied to another competitive public funding source.)
- Layering notes: PBVs are the one County-controlled operating subsidy available for supportive housing in El Dorado, and with 417 vouchers the pool is about 80 PBVs at most, several already committed to El Dorado Haven. A project with 10 to 20 PSH units should open the conversation with HHSA early and align with the EDOK Continuum of Care Coordinated Entry, which the PHA references. PBVs do not themselves trigger state prevailing wage. The HCV program is federal; the PHA has no local rental assistance money. The Tahoe Basin is in the county PHA's jurisdiction but check overlap with the City of South Lake Tahoe programs in Pass 3.
- Source: https://www.eldoradocounty.ca.gov/Services/Assistance-Programs/Housing-Energy-Assistance/Housing-and-Homelessness/Public-Housing-Authority
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**State HOME and CDBG (non-entitlement county) and Affordable Housing County Participation Requests for HCD applications**
- Agency: El Dorado County Planning and Building Department, Affordable Housing Unit; Loans@edcgov.us; Board approval of resolutions under Board Policy A-6
- Type: loan
- Typical award: El Dorado County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction, so HOME and CDBG come through state HCD competitions and the County has used them for homeownership, not rental development: HOME $500,000 First-Time Homebuyer award (Resolutions 058-2026 and 080-2026) and a 2025 CDBG application for up to $1,500,000 for the First-Time Homebuyer Program (Resolution 180-2025). Developers seeking state funds that require a County co-applicant or resolution file an Affordable Housing County Participation Request Form at least 10 weeks before the HCD deadline.
- AMI requirement: HOME: at or below 80% AMI with HOME rents and affordability periods; CDBG: low and moderate income benefit. A developer-sponsored state HOME rental application through the County would carry HCD's 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Follows HCD's HOME and CDBG NOFA calendar (hcd.ca.gov/grants-and-funding/nofa-calendar). Submit the County Participation Request Form at least 10 weeks before the HCD deadline so the Board can adopt the resolution.)
- Layering notes: The County's HOME and CDBG dollars are committed to first-time homebuyer loans, so a rental project should not count on them. The practical value of the County here is as a resolution sponsor or co-applicant for state programs (HOME rental new construction, CDBG public infrastructure, Joe Serna farmworker, MHP) in a non-entitlement area where a local government signature is required. Build the 10-week lead time plus Board calendar into the HCD application schedule. State HOME in a project triggers federal and state prevailing wage and HOME cross-cutting requirements (environmental review, Section 3, Davis-Bacon on 12 or more HOME units).
- Source: https://www.eldoradocounty.ca.gov/Services/HomebuyerRehab-Loans-Affordable-Housing-Development/Developers/Developer-Programs/Collaborative-Opportunities
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Tax-exempt multifamily bonds: County TEFRA approvals for CMFA issuances (County not an issuer)**
- Agency: El Dorado County Board of Supervisors (TEFRA host approval, agenda through the Planning and Building Affordable Housing Unit); issuer California Municipal Finance Authority (CMFA), JPA member since October 2014; CSCDA also available
- Type: bond
- Typical award: Project-sized conduit issuances. CMFA TEFRA approved March 10, 2026 for up to $12,000,000 for Diamond Village Apartments II (31 units, 750 Black Rice Road, Placerville area, paired with $3,560,000 of HOME-ARP). CMFA closed $37,800,000 of bonds for SNO Foundation and Kingdom Development in El Dorado County in 2026. No County-issued multifamily bonds.
- AMI requirement: Federal 4% LIHTC and bond rules: at least 20% of units at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (or income averaging), 55-year CDLAC regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: rolling (next: TEFRA hearings are placed on regular Board agendas with published notice; allow 6 to 8 weeks from request to Board action and align with CDLAC rounds. County receives a share of the CMFA issuance fee for its Affordable Housing Trust Fund.)
- Layering notes: The County has no bond program of its own and does not take financial risk on conduit issues, so the TEFRA approval is procedural. Staff reports are prepared by the Affordable Housing Unit, which is stretched; submit the TEFRA request package early. Diamond Village II is the current template for an unincorporated El Dorado 4% deal: SB 35 ministerial approval, CMFA bonds, HOME-ARP from the state, TIF offset for the affordable units, and PBVs through the County PHA for the NPLH or homeless set-aside units. Private activity bonds alone do not trigger state prevailing wage, but tax credits plus state funds in the same deal usually do.
- Source: https://eldorado.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=39922&GUID=053DC3F5-D26C-4E77-9729-7F71D94BADDA&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Traffic Impact Fee (TIF) Offset Program for Developments with Affordable Housing Units (Board Policy B-14)**
- Agency: El Dorado County Planning and Building Department, Affordable Housing Unit, with the Department of Transportation and the Chief Administrative Office; Housing@edcgov.us; 330 Fair Lane, Placerville (Housing Unit moving to 768 Pleasant Valley Road, Diamond Springs)
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Up to $1,000,000 of TIF offsets authorized each year from federal and state transportation revenues, with unused balances carried forward; Board may award more than the annual amount. Since December 2007 about $4.7 million of offsets have supported 344 affordable units. Recent awards: Diamond Village Apartments Phase II $436,632 for 30 units at 80% AMI (about 94% of the project TIF, May 12, 2026); Racquet Way Phase 2 recommended at 50% ($139,098 of $278,196) in February 2026 and denied by the Board. Staff reported about $13 million of offset capacity remaining in the program.
- AMI requirement: Developments of 5 or more units in the unincorporated county with a submitted planning application and at least 20% of units affordable to very low, low or moderate income households. Deed restriction through a TIF Offset Affordable Housing Agreement: 55 years for rental units, 45 years for ownership units (lengthened from 20 years in the September 29, 2026 revision). Must be able to pull permits within 2 years of award (extensions in 1-year increments, up to 3).
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Round 37 applications were due January 1-15, 2026 with Board awards in April 2026. The revised policy (September 29, 2026) says requests are reviewed twice a year in February and August, so expect the next window around January 2027; watch the TIF Offset page for the Round 38 public notice.)
- Layering notes: This is the County's only recurring development incentive and it is an offset, not cash, so it reduces the fee line of the sources and uses rather than adding a source. A 30-unit project at 80% AMI saved $436,632 in 2026. Scoring criteria: complete packet, site control, developer capacity, benefit to lower income households, Housing Element alignment, mixed income, services and infrastructure, likelihood of construction within 2 years. The Advisory Committee docked Racquet Way for a market-rate developer's limited affordable housing experience, so bring a compliance track record. The 2026 revisions also changed TIM to TIF, removed ADU language and asked the Affordable Housing Task Force to study eligibility up to 150% AMI, 100% offsets for low income units and CAO-delegated awards; none of those were adopted as of September 2026. Fee reductions under Labor Code 1720(b)(4) have an unsettled prevailing wage effect, but most projects using this offset also carry tax credits or state funds that trigger it anyway. Other impact fees (school, fire) still apply. Cities (Placerville, South Lake Tahoe) are outside the program.
- Source: https://www.eldoradocounty.ca.gov/Services/HomebuyerRehab-Loans-Affordable-Housing-Development/Traffic-Impact-Fee-TIF-Offset-Program-for-Affordable-Housing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption (property tax), El Dorado County Assessor**
- Agency: El Dorado County Assessor, Jon DeVille, 360 Fair Lane, Placerville; 530-621-5719; with the California State Board of Equalization (Organizational Clearance Certificate)
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Exemption of property taxes on the share of units restricted to lower income households (Revenue and Taxation Code 214(g)): full exemption for units at or below 80% AMI in LIHTC or publicly financed projects, partial exemption for the qualifying share in mixed projects. In El Dorado the 1% base rate plus voter-approved debt applies to the exempt value.
- AMI requirement: Nonprofit or limited partnership with a nonprofit managing general partner holding a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate, recorded regulatory agreement (tax credits, bonds, HOME, PLHA, local agreement) restricting rents to lower income households.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: File BOE-267 (first year) or BOE-267-A (annual) with the Assessor by February 15 each year; late filings through December 31 receive a partial exemption. Supplemental and LIHTC-specific forms (BOE-267-L, 267-L1, 267-L-A, 267-L2) attach as applicable.)
- Layering notes: Standard underwriting item. The Racquet Way staff report noted the project does not currently qualify for a welfare tax exemption because it is developed by a market-rate developer rather than a nonprofit, which is a reminder that a for-profit TIF offset recipient without a nonprofit managing general partner pays full property tax; the developer can instead request a restricted assessed value under Revenue and Taxation Code 402.1 that reflects the rent restrictions. The County's 55-year TIF offset agreement counts as a recorded restriction for the 402.1 valuation but does not by itself qualify for 214(g).
- Source: https://www.boe.ca.gov/proptaxes/countycontacts.htm
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


### Fresno County

**Affordable Housing Development NOFO (HOME, HOME-CHDO, HOME-ARP and PLHA combined annual round)**
- Agency: Fresno County Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev@fresnocountyca.gov, (559) 600-4292)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: The February 19, 2025 NOFO offered about $15,534,572 across PLHA, HOME, HOME-CHDO and HOME-ARP; all five applications were reserved: Cherry Crossing II, Sanger (64 units, Self-Help Enterprises), Reedley Elderly and Selma Elderly (Self-Help, $2,000,000 HOME-CHDO each for 11 assisted units, $181,818 per unit), Sanger Modular now Sendero Commons (UPholdings and RH Community Builders) and Park Kingsburg (Horizon ELOM, $2,000,000). The 2026-27 Action Plan reserves $5,018,870 of HOME for projects selected under the 2026 NOFO. Historic County awards were capped at $1,000,000; the 2025 NOFO raised the recommended maximum to $2,000,000 per project with justified requests above that considered.
- AMI requirement: HOME 20-year federal affordability (County loan terms typically 55 years; Reedley and Selma Heritage: 15-year HOME affordability on 11 units, loan repaid by March 31, 2083); PLHA 55 years with half the PLHA units at 60% AMI and half at 80% AMI; HOME-ARP qualifying populations.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2025 cycle: NOFO released February 19, 2025, Round 1 applications due March 6, 2025 (timed to CTCAC 9% Round 1), reservation letters by March 12, 2025, then ongoing applications reopened March 14, 2025 while funds remained. A 2026 NOFO ran on the same calendar with awards identified before July 21, 2026. Expect the 2027 NOFO in January or February 2027 with a late February or early March deadline ahead of CTCAC 9% Round 1.)
- Layering notes: Fresno County splits housing work three ways: the Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev) runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA and the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO; the County Administrative Office's new Office of Housing and Homelessness (OHH, created November 18, 2025) administers HHAP, the Continuum of Care and Transitional Rent and is absorbing housing programs from Behavioral Health and ComDev (Phase 2 approved November 3, 2026); and the Department of Behavioral Health co-applies for Homekey+ and NPLH and funds BHSA housing. Fresno Housing (the joint City and County Housing Authorities) is the dominant developer and voucher source. The City of Fresno and Clovis are separate entitlements with their own programs (Pass 3). fresnocountyca.gov and fresnohousing.org open normally; Board records are on fresnocounty.legistar.com and the Legistar web API client 'fresnocounty'. This is the County's only standing capital program and it is explicitly sized as gap money for 9% tax credit deals: 0% or 3% simple interest at the applicant's election, repayment deferred to maturity or as residual receipts (recent deals 50/50 split), non-recourse, subordinate to senior debt, loan term at least through the affordability period, and HOME may be converted to a grant by Board action. Eligible locations are the Urban County only: Fowler, Kerman, Kingsburg, Mendota, Orange Cove, Reedley, Sanger, Selma and unincorporated areas including County islands inside Fresno and Clovis; projects inside the cities of Fresno and Clovis are ineligible. Davis-Bacon applies at 12 or more HOME-assisted units and state prevailing wage applies to PLHA. Reservation letters are written to support CTCAC applications; final agreements require NEPA, subsidy layering review and Board approval and typically land 8 to 12 months after reservation. Source is the NOFO page with the 2025 NOFO PDF and reservation letters; the Reedley and Selma Heritage agreements are at fresnocounty.legistar.com (files 26-0593 and 26-0069, June 30, 2026).
- Source: https://www.fresnocountyca.gov/Departments/Public-Works-and-Planning/divisions-of-public-works-and-planning/community-development-division/Affordable-Housing-Development-Program-%E2%80%93-Notice-of-Funding-Opportunity
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Behavioral Health Services Act Integrated Plan FY 2026-29: housing interventions, capital development and operating subsidies**
- Agency: Fresno County Department of Behavioral Health (Integrated Plan approved by the Board June 16, 2026)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Housing Interventions component expenditures: $26,929,779 (FY 2026-27), $30,078,356 (FY 2027-28), $31,885,245 (FY 2028-29). Capital development $6,098,789 to $6,118,094 a year (24.8% of the component in year one, under the 25% cap), permanent rental subsidies $7,092,044 plus bundled rental and operating $2,526,608, interim rental subsidies $1,207,577 and bundled $2,250,773, outreach $1,709,758, participant assistance $373,266, Housing Flex Pool start-up $400,000 a year, flex pool share rising from 50% to 80%. First capital commitment: Victory Village (959 N. Parkway Drive, Fresno; Fresno Housing conversion of a shelter to 42 units, 41 PSH for chronically homeless) conditional $6,300,000 capital plus $287,000 a year operating subsidy for 30 years ($8,610,000) and on-site services valued at $424,872 a year, contingent on 2026 Round 2 9% tax credits (resolution June 30, 2026).
- AMI requirement: BHSA eligible adults with serious mental illness or substance use disorder; at least 50% of housing funds for chronically homeless people; Victory Village carries a 30-year use restriction for 41 PSH units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA. Behavioral Health plans one BHSA capital project of about 42 units per plan period and will issue an RFP for a Housing Flex Pool operator; Behavioral Health Bridge Housing operating subsidies for 180 units at two sites convert to BHSA when BHBH ends in 2027. Developers should approach Behavioral Health housing staff with a PSH project that needs capital or a 20 to 30 year operating subsidy.)
- Layering notes: Fresno County splits housing work three ways: the Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev) runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA and the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO; the County Administrative Office's new Office of Housing and Homelessness (OHH, created November 18, 2025) administers HHAP, the Continuum of Care and Transitional Rent and is absorbing housing programs from Behavioral Health and ComDev (Phase 2 approved November 3, 2026); and the Department of Behavioral Health co-applies for Homekey+ and NPLH and funds BHSA housing. Fresno Housing (the joint City and County Housing Authorities) is the dominant developer and voucher source. The City of Fresno and Clovis are separate entitlements with their own programs (Pass 3). fresnocountyca.gov and fresnohousing.org open normally; Board records are on fresnocounty.legistar.com and the Legistar web API client 'fresnocounty'. Fresno is one of the few counties in this database putting BHSA into capital (about $6.1 million a year) and into long operating subsidies ($287,000 a year for 30 years at Victory Village, $15.3 million over 20 years at Sendero Commons). Tenant-based subsidies serve 869 people in year one rising to 1,049, 372 units a year. Capital money has gone first to Fresno Housing (Victory Village) and to the Homekey+ project in Sanger. Source is the revised 2026-2029 Integrated Plan PDF; budget figures from the Integrated Plan Budget attachment to Board file 26-0538 and the Victory Village resolution (file 26-0585).
- Source: https://www.fresnocountyca.gov/files/sharedassets/county/v/1/behavioral-health/mhsa/fresno-county-2026-2029-integrated-plan-revised.pdf

**CDBG, HOME and ESG, Fresno Urban County (2026-27 Annual Action Plan)**
- Agency: Fresno County Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (CDBG and HOME); County Administrative Office, Office of Housing and Homelessness (ESG)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Federal FY 2026 allocations used July 1, 2026 to June 30, 2027: CDBG $3,116,754, HOME $1,137,340, ESG $278,809. HOME program budget including carryover: affordable rental rehabilitation loans and grants $6,000,000, new construction development loans and grants $5,318,870, tenant-based rental assistance $300,000, administration $571,271, CHDO reserve $290,734 (County set-aside is 25%, above the 15% minimum). CDBG: $1,071,589 for unincorporated public facilities, $1,542,523 to the eight participating cities, $135,000 public services, $300,000 Sheriff programs. HOME match is met from $27,953,832 of banked match (June 30, 2025).
- AMI requirement: HOME rent and income limits with 20-year minimum affordability (County typically records 55-year loan terms); CDBG national objective; ESG for shelter, outreach, rapid rehousing and prevention.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Action Plan public hearing and adoption May 19, 2026 for PY 2026-27; HOME development funds are awarded through the January to March Affordable Housing Development NOFO. A new three-year Urban County Joint Powers Agreement for July 1, 2027 to June 30, 2030 was approved August 11, 2026 with Fowler, Kerman, Kingsburg, Mendota, Reedley and Selma committed, Sanger and Parlier deciding in August 2026, and Orange Cove leaving the JPA.)
- Layering notes: Fresno County splits housing work three ways: the Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev) runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA and the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO; the County Administrative Office's new Office of Housing and Homelessness (OHH, created November 18, 2025) administers HHAP, the Continuum of Care and Transitional Rent and is absorbing housing programs from Behavioral Health and ComDev (Phase 2 approved November 3, 2026); and the Department of Behavioral Health co-applies for Homekey+ and NPLH and funds BHSA housing. Fresno Housing (the joint City and County Housing Authorities) is the dominant developer and voucher source. The City of Fresno and Clovis are separate entitlements with their own programs (Pass 3). fresnocountyca.gov and fresnohousing.org open normally; Board records are on fresnocounty.legistar.com and the Legistar web API client 'fresnocounty'. HOME is the base of every County capital commitment; the County banks match and carries over unspent HOME, which is why the 2026-27 development line ($5.3 million) is several times the annual allocation. Reservations in the 2026-27 plan: Reedley Elderly $2,000,000, Selma Elderly $2,000,000, Sendero Commons $2,950,000 (HOME-ARP $2,500,000 development plus $450,000 operating, alongside PLHA $2,364,219), Park Kingsburg $2,000,000, HOME-ARP supportive services $772,651 and nonprofit operating and capacity $495,200. CDBG is not used for housing development. Source is the May 19, 2026 Board item (file 26-0444) with the Annual Action Plan attachment.

**County HOME rehabilitation and preservation loans for existing affordable senior and family rentals (Reedley Heritage and Selma Heritage model)**
- Agency: Fresno County Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Reedley Heritage (23 units, 1978, 172 S. East Avenue, Reedley; total cost $7,828,816) and Selma Heritage (24 units, 1979, 2745 Wright Street, Selma; total cost $8,493,666): $2,000,000 HOME-CHDO loan each to Self-Help Enterprises limited partnerships supporting 11 units each ($181,818 per unit), 3% simple interest, residual receipts split 50/50, balloon due March 31, 2083, HOME affordability 15 years with tax credit restrictions 30 to 55 years; conditional approvals March 17 and June 30, 2026 pending USDA financing. Park Kingsburg (Horizon ELOM) holds a $2,000,000 rehabilitation reservation in the 2026-27 plan. The 2026-27 HOME budget carries $6,000,000 for affordable rental rehabilitation.
- AMI requirement: HOME rents and incomes on assisted units for 15 years (rehabilitation at this subsidy level), with the County loan outstanding 55 years.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Apply through the Affordable Housing Development NOFO (January to March); the County will accept applications on an ongoing basis while funds remain after Round 1.)
- Layering notes: Fresno County splits housing work three ways: the Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev) runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA and the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO; the County Administrative Office's new Office of Housing and Homelessness (OHH, created November 18, 2025) administers HHAP, the Continuum of Care and Transitional Rent and is absorbing housing programs from Behavioral Health and ComDev (Phase 2 approved November 3, 2026); and the Department of Behavioral Health co-applies for Homekey+ and NPLH and funds BHSA housing. Fresno Housing (the joint City and County Housing Authorities) is the dominant developer and voucher source. The City of Fresno and Clovis are separate entitlements with their own programs (Pass 3). fresnocountyca.gov and fresnohousing.org open normally; Board records are on fresnocounty.legistar.com and the Legistar web API client 'fresnocounty'. Preservation deals in the small JPA cities are the County's most repeatable transaction: a $2 million HOME-CHDO loan to Self-Help Enterprises on a USDA 515 senior property with 9% credits. The County expects not to be repaid (negative cash flow projected by year 25) and may later convert the loan to a grant; its monitoring fee is capped at $3,000 in year one rising 3% a year. Source is the June 30, 2026 Reedley Heritage Board item (file 26-0593); Selma Heritage is file 26-0069.

**Density bonus and affordable housing incentives (Zoning Ordinance Chapter 824.3)**
- Agency: Fresno County Department of Public Works and Planning, Development Services and Capital Projects Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus law as adopted in the February 2024 Zoning Ordinance (Chapter 824.3, text current to September 23, 2026): bonuses, reduced parking and incentives or concessions for projects of five or more units with 10% lower income, 5% very low income, senior, 10% moderate for-sale common interest, 10% for transitional foster youth, disabled veterans or homeless persons, 20% lower income student housing, or 100% affordable (up to 20% moderate). The 2025 APR reports the County is amending the ordinance in 2026 to match AB 2694 and other 2024 state law changes.
- AMI requirement: Government Code 65915 affordability levels with 55-year recorded restrictions on bonus-qualifying units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Requested with the land use application; Chapter 824.3 amendments expected in 2026 per the Housing Element APR.)
- Layering notes: Fresno County splits housing work three ways: the Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev) runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA and the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO; the County Administrative Office's new Office of Housing and Homelessness (OHH, created November 18, 2025) administers HHAP, the Continuum of Care and Transitional Rent and is absorbing housing programs from Behavioral Health and ComDev (Phase 2 approved November 3, 2026); and the Department of Behavioral Health co-applies for Homekey+ and NPLH and funds BHSA housing. Fresno Housing (the joint City and County Housing Authorities) is the dominant developer and voucher source. The City of Fresno and Clovis are separate entitlements with their own programs (Pass 3). fresnocountyca.gov and fresnohousing.org open normally; Board records are on fresnocounty.legistar.com and the Legistar web API client 'fresnocounty'. The County applies state law without local enhancements. The 2025 APR also notes 2026 zoning changes to allow supportive housing and SROs ministerially in commercial zones and to implement SB 6 and AB 2011 residential uses in commercial zones, which widen the site pool for affordable projects in unincorporated communities. Source is the chapter text on gocodebook.com (official County code is hosted on ecode360, which blocks automated access).
- Source: https://gocodebook.com/library/us/ca/fresno-county-zoning/division-vi/chapter-824.3-affordable-housing-incentives-density-bonus

**HOME-ARP rental housing development and supportive services (Allocation Plan amended October 20, 2026)**
- Agency: Fresno County Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Total HOME-ARP allocation $4,960,751 (available through September 30, 2030). Amended plan: rental housing development $2,500,000 (reserved to Sendero Commons, Sanger, 87 PSH units at 30% AMI), supportive services $1,717,851, administration $742,900; the original 100% developer match requirement and the $450,000 operating reserve were removed. Housing production goal reduced to 10 qualifying-population units.
- AMI requirement: HOME-ARP qualifying populations (homeless, at risk, fleeing violence or trafficking, other at-risk) with preferences added in the amendment for Sendero Commons; 15-year minimum HOME-ARP affordability, County typically records longer.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Funds may be requested through the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO or by direct approach to ComDev; the County reported that no developer had taken on HOME-ARP in the three years before Sendero Commons, so the remaining balance is effectively available to the next PSH project that can navigate the federal requirements.)
- Layering notes: Fresno County splits housing work three ways: the Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev) runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA and the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO; the County Administrative Office's new Office of Housing and Homelessness (OHH, created November 18, 2025) administers HHAP, the Continuum of Care and Transitional Rent and is absorbing housing programs from Behavioral Health and ComDev (Phase 2 approved November 3, 2026); and the Department of Behavioral Health co-applies for Homekey+ and NPLH and funds BHSA housing. Fresno Housing (the joint City and County Housing Authorities) is the dominant developer and voucher source. The City of Fresno and Clovis are separate entitlements with their own programs (Pass 3). fresnocountyca.gov and fresnohousing.org open normally; Board records are on fresnocounty.legistar.com and the Legistar web API client 'fresnocounty'. HOME-ARP is being used as the County's capital piece in the Sendero Commons stack (Homekey+, NPLH, HHIP and PLHA). Staff flagged the program's complexity and a hard 2030 expenditure deadline, so a shovel-ready PSH project in a JPA city or unincorporated area could pick up any unspent balance quickly. Source is the October 20, 2026 Board item (file 26-0945) with the draft amended Allocation Plan.

**Homekey+ and No Place Like Home co-applications sponsored by the County (Sendero Commons, Davu Village)**
- Agency: Fresno County Department of Behavioral Health with the County Administrative Office; development partners UPholdings and RH Community Builders, and Fresno Housing
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Sendero Commons, Sanger (88 units, 87 PSH at 30% AMI, 45 veteran units; total development cost $54,007,788): Homekey+ $29,853,131 ($26,295,016 capital, $3,558,115 operating; award notice March 20, 2026, standard agreement approved October 20, 2026), NPLH Round 4 $18,147,660 ($10,000,038 capital, $8,147,660 operating, transferred from the failed Libre Commons project), HHIP $530,000, local contributions $5,476,959 (County HOME-ARP $2,500,000 and PLHA $2,364,219). County committed 20 years of supportive services ($10,241,756) and a 20-year rental subsidy on 42 units through Transitional Rent and BHSA ($15,314,934). The County also co-applied with Fresno Housing for Davu Village (former Parkside Inn, 1415 W. Olive Avenue, Fresno; total cost $22,362,000; resolution up to $35,000,000, April 3, 2025 application).
- AMI requirement: Homekey+ and NPLH: all assisted units for households with serious mental illness or behavioral health needs experiencing or at risk of homelessness, 30% AMI, Coordinated Entry referrals, 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Homekey+ applications are accepted by HCD over the counter while funds last; the County selected its co-applicants through Behavioral Health MOUs approved February 11, 2025 (RH Community Builders and Fresno Housing). A developer seeking County sponsorship should approach Behavioral Health and the Office of Housing and Homelessness with a site and a services plan.)
- Layering notes: Fresno County splits housing work three ways: the Department of Public Works and Planning, Community Development Division (ComDev) runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA and the annual Affordable Housing Development NOFO; the County Administrative Office's new Office of Housing and Homelessness (OHH, created November 18, 2025) administers HHAP, the Continuum of Care and Transitional Rent and is absorbing housing programs from Behavioral Health and ComDev (Phase 2 approved November 3, 2026); and the Department of Behavioral Health co-applies for Homekey+ and NPLH and funds BHSA housing. Fresno Housing (the joint City and County Housing Authorities) is the dominant developer and voucher source. The City of Fresno and Clovis are separate entitlements with their own programs (Pass 3). fresnocountyca.gov and fresnohousing.org open normally; Board records are on fresnocounty.legistar.com and the Legistar web API client 'fresnocounty'. The County's role is co-applicant, services provider and rental subsidy source rather than capital lender; the capital stack at Sendero Commons is state money (Homekey+ and NPLH, about 67% of cost) plus County HOME-ARP and PLHA. NPLH Round 4 money was reallocated once already, so timing of state agreements has run three to five years behind initial awards. Source is the October 20, 2026 Board item (file 26-0992).
