### Napa County

**Affordable Housing Impact Fee and Inclusionary Requirements (County Code Chapter 18.107)**
- Agency: Napa County Housing and Community Services; Planning, Building and Environmental Services
- Type: other
- Typical award: Not a subsidy; it capitalizes the Affordable Housing Fund. Fees adopted Oct 21, 2025 (3-2 vote), effective Jan 16, 2026, phased 50% in 2026, 75% in 2027, 100% in 2028: residential per sq ft $0 up to 2,500 sq ft, $43 for 2,501-3,500 sq ft (was $10.75), $86 above 3,500 sq ft (was $12.25); ADUs under 751 sq ft exempt. Nonresidential per sq ft: hotel $100 (was $9), retail and restaurant $58.40 (was $7.50), office $14.68 (was $5.25), industrial $7.70 (was $4.50), warehouse $7.50 (was $3.60).
- AMI requirement: Ownership projects: 20% of single-family detached units or 17% of attached units for moderate income (120% AMI) households, 40-year restrictions. Rental projects pay housing fees or elect the alternative of 12% of units at low income rents under a rent regulatory agreement. Exempt: farmworker units of 1,200 sq ft or less, 100% affordable projects, certain multifamily and SRO projects, projects of four or fewer units may pay a per-unit fee. Equivalency proposals (off-site units, land dedication) allowed with Planning Commission approval.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Fees apply to applications filed after Jan 15, 2026 at 50% of the new schedule; 75% in 2027; full schedule in 2028. The Board also directed staff to create a fee deferral program for owners who rent units as affordable housing; terms not yet published.)
- Layering notes: Relevant to developers two ways: 100% affordable multifamily is exempt from the fee, and the fee stream funds the County Affordable Housing Fund. A fee exemption or waiver can count as public funds under Labor Code 1720(b)(4), so confirm prevailing wage exposure with counsel. Applies only in unincorporated Napa County; each city has its own inclusionary and linkage rules (Pass 3). Code text was read from the Zoneomics mirror (Ordinances 1334 of 2010, 1386 of 2013, 1491 of June 4, 2024 and 1495 of Sept 24, 2024); fee amounts come from Sonoma Index-Tribune and CBS Bay Area coverage of the Oct 21, 2025 vote. napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; the official FAQ is https://www.napacounty.gov/3951/Affordable-Housing-Impact-Fee-Housing-Co.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/napa-county-unincorporated-CA/chapter_36
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Affordable Unit Permit Fee Waiver and Expedited Processing (County Code 18.107.120)**
- Agency: Napa County Planning, Building and Environmental Services
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Building permit application fee waivers for compliant affordable units, flexibility in unit size and finishes, attached units allowed in single-family projects, and expedited processing. Housing impact fees are also refundable if a project does not proceed and a refund is requested within 90 days.
- AMI requirement: Units must satisfy Chapter 18.107 affordability (40-year minimum; 55 years for density bonus rentals).
- Cycle: rolling (next: The Oct 21, 2025 fee action also directed staff to design a fee deferral program for owners renting units as affordable housing; watch for the ordinance in 2026.)
- Layering notes: Modest value for multifamily since the chapter is written around inclusionary units in small unincorporated projects. Waived fees count as public funds under Labor Code 1720(b)(4) and can trigger state prevailing wage, so confirm with counsel. Code text read from the Zoneomics mirror; napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; confirm with the County.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/napa-county-unincorporated-CA/chapter_36
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**County conduit multifamily housing revenue bonds (none; CMFA used in Napa County)**
- Agency: Napa County (not an active conduit issuer); California Municipal Finance Authority used instead
- Type: bond
- Typical award: No county issuance program found. Recent Napa County deals used CMFA: Napa Park Homes, City of Napa, 140 units acquisition-rehab, $43,575,000 (2026); St. Anton Partners refinancing, City of Napa, $20,135,000; BRIDGE Housing, Calistoga and St. Helena, $20,544,520.
- AMI requirement: Per CDLAC: at least 20% of units at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI, 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not applicable. Use CMFA, CSCDA or CalHFA as issuer; the Board of Supervisors or city council holds the TEFRA hearing for sites in its jurisdiction.)
- Layering notes: Negative finding recorded so later passes do not search again. 4% deals in Napa pair CMFA bonds with CTCAC credits, County Affordable Housing Fund or City of Napa funds, and HCD programs (Manzanita used AHSC, NHTF and HOME). Conduit bonds alone do not trigger state prevailing wage; the deal's public subordinate loans usually do.
- Source: https://www.cmfa-ca.com/cmfa-issues-43575000-tax-exempt-financing-napa-park-homes/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**HOME and CDBG, Napa County (state non-entitlement status)**
- Agency: California HCD state HOME and CDBG programs; Napa County Housing and Community Services as applicant
- Type: grant
- Typical award: None directly. Napa County is not a HUD CDBG entitlement urban county or a HOME participating jurisdiction. The City of Napa is the only CDBG entitlement in the county (FY 2024-25 entitlement $583,519) and draws HOME and ESG through State HCD grants. No County of Napa state HOME or CDBG award for 2024 or 2025 surfaced in HCD award lists.
- AMI requirement: State HOME: 20% of HOME units at 50% AMI, rest at 60% AMI, 55-year HCD covenant. State CDBG: 51% of units for households at or below 80% AMI.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Follow HCD state HOME and CDBG NOFAs (state records). A developer in unincorporated Napa or the four small cities needs the County or city to apply as the state HOME or CDBG grantee.)
- Layering notes: There is no county-level HOME or CDBG pipeline to layer in Napa; this is a negative finding. The City of Napa CDBG and HOME programs are Pass 3. Napa County received $207,965 of State ESG for CA-517 in the 2024 NOFA, usable for shelter and rapid rehousing, not capital. HOME and CDBG carry federal Davis-Bacon (CDBG at 8+ units, HOME at 12+ units) and state prevailing wage. Status inferred from the City of Napa 2025-2030 Consolidated Plan, the City FY 2024-25 CAPER and HCD award lists; confirm with the County.
- Source: https://www.cityofnapa.org/DocumentCenter/View/14570/DRAFT-City-of-Napa-2025-2030-Consolidated-Plan
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Napa City and County Continuum of Care (CA-517) and HHAP homelessness funding**
- Agency: Napa County Housing and Community Services (CoC collaborative applicant and HHAP administrative entity) with the Napa City and County CoC board
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HHAP Round 5: Napa County $698,089.89 and Napa City & County CoC $727,623.09 (regional total $1,425,712.98, awarded individually). HHAP Round 6: nearly $1,000,000 awarded March 2, 2026 for rapid rehousing and rental subsidies, permanent housing services, coordination and the City of Napa prevention and diversion program. HHAP Round 2: County $160,645 plus CoC redirect $250,000. State dashboard: $4,900,000 HHAP rounds 1-5 to the Napa area 2019-2025 ($3,000,000 direct to the County) and $15,000,000 Encampment Resolution Funding rounds 1-3. HUD FY 2025 CoC awards to CA-517 were small: $21,564 coordinated assessment, $40,874 HMIS, $50,000 planning; the 2026 local priority listing (July 23, 2026) ranks about $300,000 County engagement and housing navigation, $400,000 City of Napa outreach, $214,000 HACN Shelter Plus Care PSH renewal, $108,000 DV bonus and $500,000 CoC bonus. State ESG via CA-517: $207,965 (2024 NOFA).
- AMI requirement: HUD CoC and HHAP target people experiencing homelessness; HHAP permanent housing uses include operating subsidies, rental assistance and capital for PSH. Homekey Round 2 funded Burbank Housing's Wine Valley Lodge conversion (54 PSH units, $18,113,600 with the City of Napa).
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: HUD FY 2026 CoC competition: local priority listing finalized July 2026 (HUD NOFO released June 1, 2026, modified July 24, 2026). HHAP Round 7 timing depends on the 2026-27 state budget; Napa's small HHAP shares are spent mostly on rental subsidies and services, not capital.)
- Layering notes: For PSH underwriting the usable pieces are HHAP operating or rental subsidy commitments and HACN Shelter Plus Care or PBVs, not capital; the CoC is small (Point-in-Time 2024 found 494 people). Napa County has no Measure-type housing sales tax and no NPLH balance surfaced. HHAP capital is state money and triggers prevailing wage. Sources opened: HCD HHAP Round 5 awardee list, HCD HHAP Round 6 award release, HUD FY 2025 CoC award report, HCD 2024 ESG award list, the County's HHAP Round 2 application and the state accountability dashboard. napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; the CoC page is https://www.napacounty.gov/1036/Napa-Continuum-of-Care and the 2026 priority listing figures come from search excerpts of a County PDF that could not be opened.
- Source: https://hcd.ca.gov/about-hcd/newsroom/hhap6-batch2-awards
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Napa County Affordable ADU Forgivable Loan Program (single-unit, recorded for completeness)**
- Agency: Napa County Housing and Community Services
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Forgivable construction loans of $45,000 to $105,000 per ADU (by bedroom count and design) for homeowners in Napa County; launched March 2023, updated underwriting guidelines adopted March 10, 2026, information session April 30, 2026. Funded from the Affordable Housing Fund (piloted with $100,000 in 2017).
- AMI requirement: ADU rented to a household at or below 80% AMI for at least five years; loan forgiven on compliance, otherwise repayable.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Open until funds are exhausted; applications through the County.)
- Layering notes: Not a multifamily source; recorded so the Affordable Housing Fund's competing uses are visible (ADU loans, farmworker centers, homeless services and fair housing all draw on the same fee revenue). Terms from the Napa Valley Community Foundation launch notice (March 17, 2023) and County news releases quoted in search results; napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; the 2026 notice is https://www.napacounty.gov/CivicAlerts.aspx?AID=858.
- Source: https://www.napavalleycf.org/?p=9363
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Napa County Affordable Housing Fund**
- Agency: Napa County Housing and Community Services (County Executive Office)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Gap loans historically $500,000 to $2,000,000 per project: $2,000,000 requested for Burbank Housing Stoddard West (51 units, 2017), $650,000 plus $148,000 for Turley Flats in St. Helena (8 units, 2017-2018), $500,000 for SAHA Manzanita Family Apartments (51 units, 2019, with $750,000 HEAP), $200,000 for 963 Pope Street in St. Helena (5 units, 2025). The 2010 NOFA offered $5,000,000 plus $2,000,000 reserved for farmworker housing. By 2017 the fund had loaned $22,000,000 for more than 1,000 units since 1992; fee revenue runs about $2,000,000 a year and the fund also pays for farmworker centers, homeless services and fair housing.
- AMI requirement: 55-year affordability covenant for rental projects (40 years for County-assisted ownership). County preference for projects in the unincorporated area, deeper affordability, permanent affordability and local workers where allowed by law. Loans may be forgiven after 40 to 55 years by Board action.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling since the 2010 NOFA; the County accepts applications year-round under the 2010 application and the 2020 joint underwriting guidelines. Requests go to the Napa County Housing Commission and then the Board of Supervisors; time them to the CTCAC round.)
- Layering notes: Capitalized by residential inclusionary in-lieu and impact fees and nonresidential housing impact fees under County Code Chapter 18.107 (Ordinance 1026, 1992); the October 21, 2025 fee overhaul (phased in from Jan 16, 2026) should raise annual revenue. Terms per the joint guidelines: 3.0% simple interest (lower if needed for feasibility), 55-year term from permanent loan conversion, 50/50 split of residual receipts between the City/County and developer, balance due at maturity, subordinate to senior debt, 90% maximum LTV on senior debt, monitoring fee $100 per unit for the first 40 units. Deferred developer fee may be repaid from cash flow within 15 years before residual receipts split. Most funded projects are inside the cities; the County obtains RHNA transfer credits in exchange (80% of County RHNA goes to the City of Napa under the Napa Pipe agreement through 2058), which limits funds for other jurisdictions. County loan is public money, so state prevailing wage applies under Labor Code 1720 unless an exemption fits. napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; the program page is https://www.napacounty.gov/443/Affordable-Housing-Projects and the source given is the joint underwriting guidelines hosted by the City of Napa.
- Source: https://www.cityofnapa.org/DocumentCenter/View/8030
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Napa County Behavioral Health Services Act (BHSA) housing interventions**
- Agency: Napa County Health and Human Services Agency, Behavioral Health Division
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Unknown. Under Proposition 1, 30% of each county's BHSA allocation must fund housing interventions beginning FY 2026-27, with up to 25% of that for capital; counties under 200,000 population (Napa qualifies) may request exemptions from the housing requirements in the FY 2026-29 Integrated Plan. Napa's draft Integrated Plan (FY 2026-27 through 2028-29) was out for comment April 1 to May 1, 2026 with a Behavioral Health Board hearing May 13, 2026; whether Napa requested the exemption or budgeted capital is not confirmed.
- AMI requirement: Housing interventions serve people with serious mental illness or substance use disorder who are homeless or at risk, with half of housing funds for chronically homeless individuals unless exempted.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Integrated Plan took effect July 1, 2026; annual updates follow. Ask Napa HHSA Behavioral Health whether capital or operating subsidies will be offered to PSH sponsors.)
- Layering notes: Napa's small BHSA allocation makes operating subsidies or rental assistance for a handful of PSH units the realistic ask; pair with Homekey+ or HHAP. Public funds, so capital triggers prevailing wage. No Napa-specific document could be opened: napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; the BHSA page is https://www.napacounty.gov/338 and the Integrated Plan Executive Summary is DocumentCenter/View/41683. Facts above come from the DHCS BHSA policy manual and Integrated Plan template plus search excerpts.
- Source: https://www.napacounty.gov/338
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Napa County Density Bonus and Incentives (County Code 18.107.170-18.107.190)**
- Agency: Napa County Planning, Building and Environmental Services
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Density bonus, incentives and concessions, parking reductions and development standard waivers per Government Code 65915; the chapter defers to state law where they conflict.
- AMI requirement: State density bonus thresholds; County requires 55-year affordability for rental bonus units and 45 years for for-sale units. Requests must be filed with the first discretionary permit with detailed submittals; denial of incentives or waivers requires specified written findings.
- Cycle: rolling
- Layering notes: Unincorporated Napa County is mostly Agricultural Preserve and Agricultural Watershed zoning, so multifamily sites are scarce; the 2023-2031 Housing Element (adopted Dec 19, 2023, HCD compliance letter Feb 22, 2024) relies on a 106-unit RHNA and RHNA transfers to the City of Napa. Inclusionary units count toward the state bonus (Latinos Unidos del Valle de Napa y Solano v. County of Napa, 2013). Ordinance 1495 (Sept 24, 2024) updated the incentives and density bonus sections. Code text read from the Zoneomics mirror; napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; confirm against the official code at library.municode.com or the County.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/napa-county-unincorporated-CA/chapter_36
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Napa County Housing Authority Farmworker Centers (CSA No. 4 assessment and HCD matching funds)**
- Agency: Napa County Housing Authority (Board of Supervisors sitting as the Authority)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Operating support for three county-owned dormitory centers (180 beds, about 400 farmworkers a year): County Service Area No. 4 vineyard assessment at $14 per planted acre raises about $650,000 a year (about 30% of operating cost); HCD Napa County Farmworker Centers Account match up to $250,000 a year (Health and Safety Code 50711.5, funded by SB 2); AB 1890 (Chapter 905, Statutes of 2026, signed Sept 30, 2026) raises the match to $500,000 a year once the Legislature appropriates it; AB 317 (2017) $200,000 General Fund support expires FY 2028-29. The County Affordable Housing Fund also subsidizes operations.
- AMI requirement: Centers serve migrant and seasonal farmworkers; AB 1890 bars use of the state funds to house H-2A workers.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not available to outside developers; funds flow only to the County-owned centers. CSA 4 assessment is reauthorized by growers every five years.)
- Layering notes: Negative finding for private sponsors: Napa's only county housing authority is a farmworker-center operator with no Housing Choice Vouchers or development lending; the public housing agency for the county is the Housing Authority of the City of Napa. Farmworker rental projects in Napa instead layer the state Joe Serna Jr. Farmworker Housing Grant, the State Farmworker Housing Tax Credit (Pass 1) and the County Affordable Housing Fund, which once reserved $2,000,000 for farmworker projects and whose fees exempt farmworker units of 1,200 sq ft or less. The 2024 Napa County Farmworker Housing Needs and Impacts Assessment found 16% of year-round and 26% of seasonal workers in overcrowded housing. napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; the Authority page is https://www.napacounty.gov/467/Housing-Authority (707-253-4112).
- Source: https://california.public.law/codes/health_and_safety_code_section_50711.5
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), Napa County non-entitlement formula share**
- Agency: Napa County Housing and Community Services (HCD formula grantee)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HCD CY 2024-2028 Formula Allocation NOFA: Napa County CY 2024 $219,600 and CY 2025 $241,967; five-year estimate 2024-2028 $1,372,500. By comparison the City of Napa (entitlement) is $223,362 and $239,253 for the same years. Earlier rounds: 2020 formula awards to the City of Napa only; the City also administers PLHA for American Canyon, Calistoga, St. Helena and Yountville under delegation agreements, not for the County.
- AMI requirement: PLHA statute: rental assistance at or below 60% AMI unless the plan targets up to 120% AMI (150% in high-cost areas); 55-year covenant for rental development; County five-year plan governs local uses.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: HCD releases the annual formula allocation each year; the County must hold a resolution and five-year plan for 2024-2028. No County NOFA to developers was found; the County's plan and uses could not be read because its site blocks automated access.)
- Layering notes: Small dollars (about $220,000 to $240,000 a year) better suited to ADU loans, predevelopment or operating subsidy than to a construction gap. State funds, so prevailing wage applies to construction. Confirm with Housing and Community Services (707-253-4112) whether the County's five-year plan directs PLHA to multifamily or to its ADU forgivable loan program ($105,000 maximum per ADU as of April 2026).
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/sites/default/files/docs/grants-and-funding/plha/2024-2028-plha-formula-allocation-nofa.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers, Housing Authority of the City of Napa (countywide HCV administrator)**
- Agency: Housing Authority of the City of Napa (HACN, HUD CA073), a City of Napa agency that administers Section 8 for all of Napa County
- Type: other
- Typical award: About 1,455 active vouchers as of early 2024 (City Consolidated Plan); the 2021 Administrative Plan allows up to 20% of budget authority to be project-based, with a 45-PBV limited preference for Napa Continuum of Care coordinated entry referrals and 15 tenant-based vouchers. Recent allocations: 8 PBVs for Stoddard West (City of Napa, 2017), 38 PBVs for Heritage House (3700 Valle Verde Drive), 8 PBVs for Napa Cove (66 units, American Canyon). Shelter Plus Care PSH renewal about $214,000 in the 2026 CoC listing.
- AMI requirement: Households at or below 50% AMI (75% at 30% AMI on admission); PBV units limited to 25% of units in a building unless serving elderly, disabled or supportive services households.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No open PBV RFP found. HACN selects owner proposals either by RFP under 24 CFR 983.51(b)(1) or without competition under 983.51(b)(2) for projects already selected through a competitive public funding process (LIHTC, HOME) within the prior three years. A revised Administrative Plan was scheduled for HACN board approval April 7, 2026; confirm adoption.)
- Layering notes: Recorded at county level because HACN is the only public housing agency in Napa County and attaches PBVs to projects in American Canyon and other cities; the Napa County Housing Authority runs farmworker centers only and holds no vouchers. Developers normally request PBVs after a CTCAC or HCD award, which lets HACN use the non-competitive path. PBV HAP contracts run up to 20 years with extensions. Federal Davis-Bacon applies to PBV new construction or rehab of 9 or more contract units; state prevailing wage is not triggered by PBVs alone. City of Napa records in Pass 3 will cover HACN's city-only programs.
- Source: https://www.cityofnapa.org/DocumentCenter/View/7814/2021-Section-8-Administrative-Plan-PDF
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption processing, Napa County Assessor**
- Agency: Napa County Assessor-Recorder-County Clerk (John Tuteur)
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Full exemption of property tax on qualifying low-income rental units under Revenue and Taxation Code 214(g) once the BOE issues an Organizational Clearance Certificate; the Assessor rules on the property-use side of the claim.
- AMI requirement: Units occupied by households at or below 80% AMI (with the 140% AMI over-income allowance on BOE-267-L3); regulatory agreement, LIHTC or public financing required; managing general partner must be a qualifying nonprofit or the project a qualifying LLC.
- Cycle: rolling (next: File BOE-267 (first filing) or BOE-267-A (annual) with the BOE-267-L, L1 and L2 supplements by February 15 each year for the full exemption; late claims through December 31 receive a partial exemption.)
- Layering notes: Standard statewide exemption (Pass 1 BOE record); recorded per county because the assessor administers it. Napa has no local property tax abatement JPA. Assessor valuation line 707-259-8740, main office 707-253-4467, 1127 First Street, Suite A, Napa. Contact is from the BOE county assessor directory; napacounty.gov pages and DocumentCenter PDFs sit behind Cloudflare and refused automated access on 2026-11-06, so the county's own page could not be opened; the Assessor's exemptions page is https://www.napacounty.gov/1081/Exemptions-From-Property-Taxes.
- Source: https://www.boe.ca.gov/proptaxes/countycontacts.htm
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


### Nevada County

**7th Cycle Housing Element (2027-2035) RH rezone program and lower income site inventory**
- Agency: Nevada County Community Development Agency, Planning Department
- Type: other
- Typical award: Draft 7th Cycle Housing Element released for 30-day review October 5, 2026 and directed to HCD on October 20, 2026. RHNA 3,712 units (1,703 lower, 730 moderate, 1,279 above moderate). Site inventory shows a 677-unit lower income shortfall, so the County must rezone about 42 acres to the RH (Residential High) zone at a minimum 16 units per acre by May 15, 2030 (if adopted and certified within 120 days of the May 15, 2027 deadline). Candidate sites are near community regions and rural centers with services.
- AMI requirement: Rezoned sites must accommodate lower income units; state law requires by-right approval for projects with at least 20% lower income units on shortfall rezone sites.
- Cycle: rolling (next: HCD review November 2026 to early 2027; adoption hearings spring 2027; certification deadline May 15, 2027; rezoning complete by May 15, 2030.)
- Layering notes: Nevada County's Housing and Community Services Department (HCS, Tyler Barrington, Director, 950 Maidu Ave, Nevada City, 530-265-1645, Housing@nevadacountyca.gov) sits inside the Health and Human Services Agency and administers the Western Nevada County Regional Housing Trust Fund, PLHA (as delegated applicant for Grass Valley and Nevada City), state HOME and CDBG awards, the MORE mobile home program and the new Down Payment Assistance Program; the Behavioral Health and Homelessness Department (Cari Yardley, Director) holds Homekey+, BHSA and the Truckee Navigation Center; HHAP is administered by the Homeless Resource Council of the Sierras as the CoC CA-531 lead; and the Regional Housing Authority (Sutter, Nevada, Yuba, Colusa and Lake counties, Yuba City) supplies vouchers. Grass Valley, Nevada City and Truckee run their own programs (Pass 3). The County has no inclusionary ordinance, no housing impact fee and no affordable housing fee waiver program; the only land use incentive is the state density bonus ordinance. nevadacountyca.gov opens directly and the Legistar web API client 'nevco' exposes staff reports and attachments at legistar.granicus.com/nevco/attachments/. The rezone list is the pipeline of multifamily sites in unincorporated Nevada County for the next decade. Developers should comment on candidate sites now and pair a rezone site with the Prohousing Designation, density bonus and PLHA Round 4 and 5 funds. The 2025 Housing Element Annual Progress Report was accepted January 27, 2026.
- Source: https://nevco.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=49989&GUID=C0E8A145-FAF4-411A-AAA0-CE1D7EA5B50E
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Behavioral Health Services Act (BHSA) Housing Interventions, 2026-2029 Integrated Plan**
- Agency: Nevada County Behavioral Health Department
- Type: other
- Typical award: Three-year BHSA plan (FY 2026/27 to 2028/29, approved June 2, 2026, Resolution 26-231) budgets $27,502,891 in BHSA spending, with the required 30% for housing interventions (about $2.75 million a year) and half of that for chronic homelessness. The County will use housing funds for rental subsidies and operating costs of its PSH units and for outreach (HOME Team, Commons Resource Center, 865 people a year); it will NOT use BHSA for capital development (0% of the allowed 25%) or for CalAIM-covered tenancy services. BHSA-funded PSH beds grew from 20 (2019) to 94 (2026) plus 24 Homekey+ units coming.
- AMI requirement: BHSA-eligible adults with serious mental illness or substance use disorder experiencing or at risk of homelessness; BHSA is the funding source of last resort for PSH.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Integrated Plan runs through FY 2028/29 with annual updates; contact Nevada County Behavioral Health (530-265-1437) about operating subsidies for PSH units.)
- Layering notes: Nevada County's Housing and Community Services Department (HCS, Tyler Barrington, Director, 950 Maidu Ave, Nevada City, 530-265-1645, Housing@nevadacountyca.gov) sits inside the Health and Human Services Agency and administers the Western Nevada County Regional Housing Trust Fund, PLHA (as delegated applicant for Grass Valley and Nevada City), state HOME and CDBG awards, the MORE mobile home program and the new Down Payment Assistance Program; the Behavioral Health and Homelessness Department (Cari Yardley, Director) holds Homekey+, BHSA and the Truckee Navigation Center; HHAP is administered by the Homeless Resource Council of the Sierras as the CoC CA-531 lead; and the Regional Housing Authority (Sutter, Nevada, Yuba, Colusa and Lake counties, Yuba City) supplies vouchers. Grass Valley, Nevada City and Truckee run their own programs (Pass 3). The County has no inclusionary ordinance, no housing impact fee and no affordable housing fee waiver program; the only land use incentive is the state density bonus ordinance. nevadacountyca.gov opens directly and the Legistar web API client 'nevco' exposes staff reports and attachments at legistar.granicus.com/nevco/attachments/. Nevada County's BHSA plan is operations-only, so a PSH developer should plan on BHSA as an operating or rental subsidy source for a handful of units, not as gap capital. The plan notes a large gap in permanent rental subsidies and that the Regional Housing Authority will not open or expand vouchers in 2026. Capital has come from BHBH, BHCIP, ERF and Homekey+ instead.

**Residential Density Bonus and Incentives for Affordable and Senior Housing (Land Use and Development Code Section 12.03.160)**
- Agency: Nevada County Community Development Agency, Planning Department
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Implements Government Code 65915 for projects of five or more units: 10% lower income, 5% very low income, 10% moderate income (for-sale), 50% senior, 50% workforce housing (60% to 150% AMI, a local category), 10% transitional foster youth, disabled veteran or homeless units, or 100% affordable projects. Bonus and incentive tables track state law (20% to 50%, up to 80% for 100% affordable within one-half mile of transit); senior developments get 20%. Parking ratios reduced per state law. Additional bonus and incentive provisions for transitional and supportive housing are in the Section governing those uses.
- AMI requirement: 55-year covenants for rental very low and lower income bonus units; moderate income for-sale units per state law.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Not applicable; applied with the land use entitlement. The 7th Cycle Housing Element (2027-2035) will rezone at least 42 acres to the RH zone at 16 units per acre to cover a 677-unit lower income shortfall, with rezoning due by May 15, 2030.)
- Layering notes: Nevada County's Housing and Community Services Department (HCS, Tyler Barrington, Director, 950 Maidu Ave, Nevada City, 530-265-1645, Housing@nevadacountyca.gov) sits inside the Health and Human Services Agency and administers the Western Nevada County Regional Housing Trust Fund, PLHA (as delegated applicant for Grass Valley and Nevada City), state HOME and CDBG awards, the MORE mobile home program and the new Down Payment Assistance Program; the Behavioral Health and Homelessness Department (Cari Yardley, Director) holds Homekey+, BHSA and the Truckee Navigation Center; HHAP is administered by the Homeless Resource Council of the Sierras as the CoC CA-531 lead; and the Regional Housing Authority (Sutter, Nevada, Yuba, Colusa and Lake counties, Yuba City) supplies vouchers. Grass Valley, Nevada City and Truckee run their own programs (Pass 3). The County has no inclusionary ordinance, no housing impact fee and no affordable housing fee waiver program; the only land use incentive is the state density bonus ordinance. nevadacountyca.gov opens directly and the Legistar web API client 'nevco' exposes staff reports and attachments at legistar.granicus.com/nevco/attachments/. The density bonus is the County's only land use incentive; there is no inclusionary ordinance, no local fee waiver and no housing impact fee. Most multifamily sites are in the Grass Valley and Nevada City spheres, Penn Valley and Lake of the Pines area, and the eastern county near Truckee (Pacific Crest Commons, 55 units, is in the Town). Watch the RH rezone list in the draft Housing Element submitted to HCD in November 2026.
- Source: https://nevadacountyca.gov/DocumentCenter/View/56807/Read-about-Density-Bonuses-Section_1203160
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**State CDBG (non-entitlement) housing and public facility grants**
- Agency: Nevada County Housing and Community Services Department (funds from California HCD)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2025 state CDBG application (approved February 17, 2026, Resolution 26-052; application to HCD approved February 2, 2026) for the $3,600,000 over-the-counter maximum: Eastern County Homeless Center purchase $1,850,000, owner-occupied mobile home repair and replacement $1,500,000, administration $250,000. Prior award 24-CDBG-10023 funds Gold Country Community Services. No CDBG has gone to multifamily rental development.
- AMI requirement: Low and moderate income (80% AMI) benefit under state CDBG rules.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2025 NOFA applications were due April 3, 2026. The County holds public meetings each February to pick activities for the next state CDBG NOFA; the 2026 NOFA cycle is expected in early 2027.)
- Layering notes: Nevada County's Housing and Community Services Department (HCS, Tyler Barrington, Director, 950 Maidu Ave, Nevada City, 530-265-1645, Housing@nevadacountyca.gov) sits inside the Health and Human Services Agency and administers the Western Nevada County Regional Housing Trust Fund, PLHA (as delegated applicant for Grass Valley and Nevada City), state HOME and CDBG awards, the MORE mobile home program and the new Down Payment Assistance Program; the Behavioral Health and Homelessness Department (Cari Yardley, Director) holds Homekey+, BHSA and the Truckee Navigation Center; HHAP is administered by the Homeless Resource Council of the Sierras as the CoC CA-531 lead; and the Regional Housing Authority (Sutter, Nevada, Yuba, Colusa and Lake counties, Yuba City) supplies vouchers. Grass Valley, Nevada City and Truckee run their own programs (Pass 3). The County has no inclusionary ordinance, no housing impact fee and no affordable housing fee waiver program; the only land use incentive is the state density bonus ordinance. nevadacountyca.gov opens directly and the Legistar web API client 'nevco' exposes staff reports and attachments at legistar.granicus.com/nevco/attachments/. The County directs its CDBG capacity to shelter facilities and mobile home repair (the MORE program), so developers should not count on CDBG for rental projects. It could fund off-site infrastructure for a project if proposed during the February community meetings.
- Source: https://nevco.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=49302&GUID=1CD54278-9D5A-4FB0-A49E-DBE5B3C43CB1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**State HOME Investment Partnerships Program loans (County as HCD HOME grantee)**
- Agency: Nevada County Housing and Community Services Department (funds from California HCD)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Lone Oak Senior Apartments II: HOME loan of $11,041,628 (State Loan Agreement 23-HOME-16301) approved May 19, 2026 (Resolution 26-197) after a March 26, 2024 application of up to $12,200,000 (Resolution 24-119). Loan is deferred, repaid from residual receipts, 3% interest, 55-year term commencing at final certificate of occupancy, secured by deed of trust with senior and junior regulatory agreements and a replacement reserve agreement.
- AMI requirement: HOME-assisted units at 50% and 60% AMI for 55 years (state HOME rental program), layered under TCAC restrictions.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Depends on HCD's next state HOME rental NOFA; the County applies on behalf of a specific developer and project. Contact HCS before HCD releases the NOFA.)
- Layering notes: Nevada County's Housing and Community Services Department (HCS, Tyler Barrington, Director, 950 Maidu Ave, Nevada City, 530-265-1645, Housing@nevadacountyca.gov) sits inside the Health and Human Services Agency and administers the Western Nevada County Regional Housing Trust Fund, PLHA (as delegated applicant for Grass Valley and Nevada City), state HOME and CDBG awards, the MORE mobile home program and the new Down Payment Assistance Program; the Behavioral Health and Homelessness Department (Cari Yardley, Director) holds Homekey+, BHSA and the Truckee Navigation Center; HHAP is administered by the Homeless Resource Council of the Sierras as the CoC CA-531 lead; and the Regional Housing Authority (Sutter, Nevada, Yuba, Colusa and Lake counties, Yuba City) supplies vouchers. Grass Valley, Nevada City and Truckee run their own programs (Pass 3). The County has no inclusionary ordinance, no housing impact fee and no affordable housing fee waiver program; the only land use incentive is the state density bonus ordinance. nevadacountyca.gov opens directly and the Legistar web API client 'nevco' exposes staff reports and attachments at legistar.granicus.com/nevco/attachments/. Nevada County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction, so HOME comes only through state HCD competitive rounds with the County as applicant and the developer as sponsor. The Lone Oak II award shows the County will carry an $11 million HOME loan for a 31-unit 4% project when paired with a trust fund loan and CMFA bonds. Grant administration was contracted to Adams Ashby Group (July 28, 2025).

**Welfare exemption from property tax for nonprofit-owned affordable housing**
- Agency: Nevada County Assessor, 950 Maidu Avenue, Nevada City, 530-265-1480
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Exempts the affordable share of assessed value for qualifying nonprofit or LP-with-nonprofit-GP owners under Revenue and Taxation Code 214(g); worth roughly 1.0% of assessed value plus voter-approved debt rates each year.
- AMI requirement: Units restricted to lower income households (and up to 140% AMI continuing residents) under a recorded regulatory agreement; BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate required.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: File BOE-267 (first year) or BOE-267-A with BOE-267-L supplements with the Assessor by February 15 each year.)
- Layering notes: Nevada County's Housing and Community Services Department (HCS, Tyler Barrington, Director, 950 Maidu Ave, Nevada City, 530-265-1645, Housing@nevadacountyca.gov) sits inside the Health and Human Services Agency and administers the Western Nevada County Regional Housing Trust Fund, PLHA (as delegated applicant for Grass Valley and Nevada City), state HOME and CDBG awards, the MORE mobile home program and the new Down Payment Assistance Program; the Behavioral Health and Homelessness Department (Cari Yardley, Director) holds Homekey+, BHSA and the Truckee Navigation Center; HHAP is administered by the Homeless Resource Council of the Sierras as the CoC CA-531 lead; and the Regional Housing Authority (Sutter, Nevada, Yuba, Colusa and Lake counties, Yuba City) supplies vouchers. Grass Valley, Nevada City and Truckee run their own programs (Pass 3). The County has no inclusionary ordinance, no housing impact fee and no affordable housing fee waiver program; the only land use incentive is the state density bonus ordinance. nevadacountyca.gov opens directly and the Legistar web API client 'nevco' exposes staff reports and attachments at legistar.granicus.com/nevco/attachments/. The Assessor page was opened but Nevada County publishes no welfare exemption page or local forms online; confirm filing practice and e-signature acceptance with the Assessor. A 2021 BOE survey noted first-time filers were granted the exemption without field inspection, so expect a routine process. Underwrite the exemption as an operating saving for all LIHTC and PSH projects.
- Source: https://www.nevadacountyca.gov/1876/Assessor
