**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), County formula allocation and PLHA development loans**
- Agency: Riverside County Housing and Workforce Solutions (HWS)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: HCD formula allocation to the County: $2,805,041 (2024 allocation year), $3,004,612 (2025), estimated $17,531,506 for the 2024-2028 five-year plan. Uses in 2026: PLHA loan of $500,000 to Arroyo Crossing Phase I, Indio (184 units; February 10, 2026), PLHA loans to Villa Verde Apartments, Coachella (March 3, 2026) and Palm Villas at State Phase I, Hemet (October 6, 2026), about $1.8 million for Shady Lane Mobile Home Park in Thermal, and a PLHA down payment program (up to $100,000 or 20% of price, combinable with HOME to $200,000, households to 120% AMI).
- AMI requirement: Arroyo Crossing terms: 49% of non-manager units (89) restricted to very low and low income with at least 20% of PLHA units (17) at very low income, 55-year covenant to at least July 1, 2080, 3% simple interest, annual payments from 25% of residual receipts shared with other public lenders.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No standing PLHA NOFA; HWS commits PLHA to projects in the HACR PBV pipeline by Board action. Contact HWS Community and Housing Development.)
- Layering notes: Riverside County's Housing and Workforce Solutions (HWS) department runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA, HHAP, Homekey+ sponsorships and project loans, while the Housing Authority of the County of Riverside (HACR, same Board sitting as Commissioners) supplies project-based vouchers, housing successor funds and land; rivcohws.org and harivco.org block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors files on riversidecountyca.iqm2.com and media.rivcocob.org, RUHS Behavioral Health, the Assessor and the County Housing Element. PLHA is the County's most flexible recurring source but is spread thin across homebuyer assistance, mobile home parks and $500,000 loans to tax credit deals; it is typically the smallest piece of a stack led by 4% credits, HACR PBVs and a HOME or HOME-ARP loan. Source is the Arroyo Crossing PLHA loan Board file (loan agreement FileOpen ID 85874); allocation figures from the HCD 2024-2028 PLHA NOFA tables.
- Source: https://riversidecountyca.iqm2.com/Citizens/Detail_LegiFile.aspx?ID=29098
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Project-Based Vouchers, Housing Authority of the County of Riverside (HACR)**
- Agency: Housing Authority of the County of Riverside (Board of Supervisors sitting as Commissioners), 5555 Arlington Avenue, Riverside
- Type: other
- Typical award: HACR administers 10,187 Housing Choice Vouchers. The FY 2026-27 Streamlined Annual PHA Plan (adopted March 24, 2026) programs about 684 PBVs across 24 new developments, including Desert Marigold, Cathedral City (96 of 97 units), Villa Verde, Coachella (87 of 152), Summit View, Beaumont (47 of 48), Palm Villas Phases I and II, Palm Desert (45 each of 88), Camino Terrace, Jurupa Valley (42 of 80), Bell Ranch, Wildomar (32 of 129), Murrieta Phase II and Oakview II (32 each of 81), Viscar Terrace, Murrieta (31 of 151), JFM Villas I and II, Indio (25 each), Cambern, Lake Elsinore (19 of 76), Mary McDonald Riley Street, Lake Elsinore (15 of 16), Sparrow at Mulberry Gardens, Riverside (14 of 59), 5th Street, Corona (11 of 11), Sunrise at Bogart (11 of 23), Thomas Village, Palm Desert (8 of 40) and Murrieta Phase I (8 of 119). PBVs are awarded through competitive RFPs (most recent reported round July 27 to August 22, 2025) and AHAP and HAP contract forms are approved by the Board (2026 approvals for 5th Street, Linwood Rose, Villa Verde I and II, 6th Street Seniors, Palm Desert Housing, Jefferson Family and Palm Villas).
- AMI requirement: HUD PBV rules (not an MTW agency): 20% program cap plus exceptions, 75% of admissions at or below 30% AMI, 20-year HAP contracts with HACR, CoC coordinated entry referrals for PSH set-asides.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: No PBV RFP was open as of November 7, 2026; HACR has issued a competitive PBV RFP roughly annually (last confirmed round closed August 22, 2025). Monitor harivco.org procurement.)
- Layering notes: Riverside County's Housing and Workforce Solutions (HWS) department runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA, HHAP, Homekey+ sponsorships and project loans, while the Housing Authority of the County of Riverside (HACR, same Board sitting as Commissioners) supplies project-based vouchers, housing successor funds and land; rivcohws.org and harivco.org block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors files on riversidecountyca.iqm2.com and media.rivcocob.org, RUHS Behavioral Health, the Assessor and the County Housing Element. PBVs are the County's primary contribution to 4% and 9% deals and nearly every County loan, PLHA commitment, Homekey+ sponsorship and HHAP operating subsidy attaches to a PBV project; developers should treat the annual HACR PBV RFP as the entry point to the County system. Davis-Bacon applies to PBV new construction and rehabilitation of nine or more units. Source is the HUD-50075 Streamlined Annual Plan attached to the March 24, 2026 Board file (Detail_LegiFile.aspx?ID=29959); voucher count from the FY 2025-26 SEMAP CEQA notice (ceqanet 2026071100).
- Source: https://riversidecountyca.iqm2.com/Citizens/FileOpen.aspx?Type=4&ID=86677&Inline=True
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Tax-exempt multifamily bonds: County TEFRA approvals for CMFA, CSCDA and CalPFA issuances**
- Agency: Riverside County Housing and Workforce Solutions (public hearings) and Office of Economic Development; Board of Supervisors adopts the approving resolution
- Type: bond
- Typical award: The County is not an active issuer; it approves conduit issuances under Section 147(f) for projects in the unincorporated area or where a joint powers authority needs host approval. Examples: Resolution 2025-22 (February 4, 2025) approving up to $20,000,000 of CMFA multifamily housing revenue bonds for Rancho Las Bolsas Apartments, Temecula area (Jamboree, 55 units, 26 PSH); Resolution 2025-15 (January 28, 2025) approving up to $80,000,000 of bonds for mobile home park acquisitions including Oasis Villas; CSCDA bonds for a Wildomar project (October 2023); CalPFA educational facility bonds (June 9, 2026).
- AMI requirement: Bond regulatory agreements (20% at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI) under CDLAC rules.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Request a TEFRA hearing through HWS at least six weeks before the CDLAC application; no County fee was stated in the Board file.)
- Layering notes: Riverside County's Housing and Workforce Solutions (HWS) department runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA, HHAP, Homekey+ sponsorships and project loans, while the Housing Authority of the County of Riverside (HACR, same Board sitting as Commissioners) supplies project-based vouchers, housing successor funds and land; rivcohws.org and harivco.org block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors files on riversidecountyca.iqm2.com and media.rivcocob.org, RUHS Behavioral Health, the Assessor and the County Housing Element. HACR does not issue multifamily bonds; CMFA and CSCDA are the usual issuers with the County or the host city holding the TEFRA hearing. Source is the February 4, 2025 Board item on media.rivcocob.org.
- Source: https://media.rivcocob.org/proceeds/2025/p2025_02_04_files/21.01001.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Welfare Exemption (property tax), Riverside County Assessor-County Clerk-Recorder**
- Agency: Riverside County Assessor-County Clerk-Recorder (Peter Aldana), (951) 955-6200 or (800) 746-1544
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Full exemption of the ad valorem property tax (about 1% of assessed value plus voter-approved debt) on qualifying low-income rental housing, applied by restricted unit share under Revenue and Taxation Code 214(g).
- AMI requirement: Nonprofit owner or limited partnership with a nonprofit managing general partner holding a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate (BOE-277); first filing on BOE-267 with the Findings Sheet and a copy of the OCC for each year of use, then annual BOE-267-A with the BOE-267-L series for low-income housing; annual deadline February 15 with partial exemption for later filings.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: February 15, 2027 for the 2027-28 roll.)
- Layering notes: Riverside County's Housing and Workforce Solutions (HWS) department runs the Urban County HUD programs, PLHA, HHAP, Homekey+ sponsorships and project loans, while the Housing Authority of the County of Riverside (HACR, same Board sitting as Commissioners) supplies project-based vouchers, housing successor funds and land; rivcohws.org and harivco.org block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors files on riversidecountyca.iqm2.com and media.rivcocob.org, RUHS Behavioral Health, the Assessor and the County Housing Element. Standard California process; the Assessor page describes the OCC-first sequence and the first-filing packet, and the BOE income limit tables for Riverside County apply. Source is the Assessor's welfare exemption page.
- Source: https://rivcoacr.org/welfare-exemption
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### Sacramento County

**Community Development Block Grant (CDBG), Urban County of Sacramento**
- Agency: SHRA for the County of Sacramento
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2027 Action Plan: $6,151,763 allocation plus $2,025,000 prior-year resources ($8.18M); about $3.63M targeted to the housing development, preservation and homeownership goal (27 rental units rehabilitated, 10 constructed, 100 owner rehabs), the rest to infrastructure and public services. June 2025 notice listed $0 County CDBG for multifamily; June 2026 notice did not list CDBG.
- AMI requirement: At least 51% of assisted units for households at or below 80% AMI.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Through SHRA rounds when offered (rehabilitation projects only); 2027 program year begins January 2027.)
- Layering notes: Limited developer value: CDBG cannot fund new construction unless carried out by a Community Based Development Organization, and SHRA offers it only for rehabilitation. Davis-Bacon applies at 8 or more units. Useful for acquisition-rehab or preservation deals paired with 4% credits and CalHFA or SHRA bonds. CDBG-CV balances are committed to the Mather Community Campus shelter.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2027-County-AP-Narrative_Final.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**HOME Investment Partnerships Program, Sacramento County HOME Consortium**
- Agency: SHRA for the County of Sacramento (lead entity) and consortium cities Citrus Heights, Folsom, Rancho Cordova, Galt and Isleton
- Type: loan
- Typical award: 2027 Action Plan: $2,825,253 annual allocation plus $900,000 program income ($3.73M total). 2024 entitlement $2,965,817; year-end 2024 balance $15.7M ($9.1M uncommitted). $2,500,000 posted as available June 1, 2026. Recent project loans $1.5M to $8.9M (Arden Star $8.9M for 48 HOME units; Madison Square $4.45M; San Juan Phase II $4.2M).
- AMI requirement: 20% of HOME-assisted units at 50% AMI (Low HOME rents), remainder at or below 60% AMI with rents at 65% AMI; federal affordability period 5 to 20 years but SHRA records 55-year restrictions. 15% CHDO set-aside.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Through SHRA multifamily pre-application rounds (next expected January 2027); HUD program year is the calendar year, 2027 Action Plan drafted August 2026.)
- Layering notes: Applies to the unincorporated county and the five consortium cities (Elk Grove and the City of Sacramento are separate HUD entitlements). HOME on 12 or more units triggers Davis-Bacon, and SHRA policy requires the higher of state or federal wage by trade when both apply; applicants must flag wage assumptions in the SHRA narrative. HOME units stack with CTCAC units but the HOME rent and income rules are the binding constraint on those units. SHRA may assign HOME rather than local fee funds to a request, so underwrite wages conservatively. Environmental review (NEPA) deadline falls two months after SHRA's application deadline. SB 802 (Chapter 417, Statutes of 2026) requires the County and cities to form the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency, a regional JPA that absorbs SHRA, by January 1, 2028; expect program administration, fee funds and NOFA calendars to migrate to that agency during 2027-2028.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2027-County-AP-Narrative_Final.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), County of Sacramento**
- Agency: County of Sacramento (HCD entitlement recipient); SHRA administers the multifamily share
- Type: loan
- Typical award: 2024 PLHA income $6,666,613; year-end 2024 uncommitted multifamily balance $2,245,525 and single-family (down payment assistance) balance $2,804,135. Recent multifamily commitments: Arden Star $2.42M (13 units), San Juan $1.7M (7 units), San Juan Phase II $2.7M then increased to $4.7M (70 units). $0 posted as available for County projects June 1, 2026.
- AMI requirement: Multifamily share for households at or below 60% AMI (with workforce flexibility under the County's PLHA plan); 55-year restriction. At least 25% of each allocation is reserved for down payment assistance below 50% AMI.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Through SHRA multifamily rounds as annual HCD PLHA disbursements arrive (SB 2 recording-fee revenue, variable). Next SHRA round expected January 2027; check the funds-available notice for a PLHA line.)
- Layering notes: State formula money without federal strings, so it is the preferred SHRA source when Davis-Bacon must be avoided (state prevailing wage still applies unless the below-market-loan exemption is met). Often paired with HOME in the same SHRA award (San Juan Phase II). The separate HCD Local Housing Trust Fund match program is no longer funding; the County's last LHTF award was 2024. SB 802 negotiations included language keeping PLHA flowing to all jurisdictions once the regional agency forms. SB 802 (Chapter 417, Statutes of 2026) requires the County and cities to form the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency, a regional JPA that absorbs SHRA, by January 1, 2028; expect program administration, fee funds and NOFA calendars to migrate to that agency during 2027-2028.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/ATT-4-PLHA-Annual-Report.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers, Housing Authority of the County of Sacramento**
- Agency: SHRA (the County housing authority administers all vouchers for both the City and County)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Competitive RFP awards from the tenant-based HCV allocation or HUD-VASH; no RFP anticipated in 2026. Cap of 25 units or 25% of a building, whichever is greater, with exceptions for elderly or disabled households and units with qualifying supportive services (common for PSH). HAP contract up to 20 years with extensions to 40 years total.
- AMI requirement: HCV income limits (very low income, 75% of admissions at or below 30% AMI); PBV units leased through the Coordinated Access System for homeless set-aside units under a Housing First model.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: SHRA states it does not anticipate a Project-Based Voucher RFP in 2026; updates come through the SHRA listserv. HUD's 2026 budget guidance also asked PHAs to pause new PBV commitments except for public housing repositioning, so a 2027 RFP is uncertain. Non-competitive PBV remains possible for SHRA-controlled sites and for adding units to existing HAP contracts.)
- Layering notes: PBV HAP contracts underwrite debt on PSH units (rents at the payment standard, up to 120% of FMR with approval) and were the operating engine for Homekey, NPLH and MHP supportive units in Sacramento. With no 2026 RFP, new PSH deals should plan on Homekey+ operating grants, BHSA rental subsidies or the County Flexible Housing Pool instead. Referral mechanics for PBV PSH run through Sacramento Steps Forward (see Continuum of Care record). SB 802 (Chapter 417, Statutes of 2026) requires the County and cities to form the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency, a regional JPA that absorbs SHRA, by January 1, 2028; expect program administration, fee funds and NOFA calendars to migrate to that agency during 2027-2028.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/SHRA-HCV-2027-Admin-Plan-Draft.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**SHRA Multifamily Lending Program (County gap financing)**
- Agency: Sacramento Housing and Redevelopment Agency (SHRA) on behalf of the County of Sacramento and the Housing Authority of the County of Sacramento
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Gap loans sized to the financing gap after all other sources; no stated per-project cap. County funds posted June 1, 2026: HOME $2,500,000, Housing Trust Fund $900,000, Affordable Housing Ordinance fund $6,000,000, PLHA $0 (total about $9.4M). Recent County commitments: San Juan Phase II $11.7M (HOME $7.0M plus PLHA $4.7M, 70 units), Shiloh Arms $2.12M HOME, South Sacramento Homekey $1.9M. Projects must have 12 or more units.
- AMI requirement: 55-year regulatory agreement. Minimum per source: HOME 20% of HOME units at 50% AMI and the rest at or below 60% AMI; AHO fund 20% at 50% AMI and 80% at 60% AMI; Housing Trust Fund 50% to 80% AMI workforce households. Priorities: preservation at risk, then new construction (PSH and homeless, veterans, workforce 30% to 60% AMI, other), then rehab. Deeper affordability preferred.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Two rounds a year aligned to CTCAC 9% rounds; 2026 pre-application deadlines were Jan 2 and July 1, 2026 with full applications March 2 and Sept 1 (Board/Council approvals June 16, 2026 and Jan 19, 2027). SHRA was not accepting pre-applications as of 2026-11-06; the 2027 schedule and funds-available notice are expected on the SHRA multifamily page about 30 days before the next pre-application deadline (likely early January 2027). Pre-applications go through Builder's Patch.)
- Layering notes: This is the primary local soft source for 9% and 4% deals in the unincorporated county (City of Sacramento projects use the parallel City pool, Pass 3). Terms: up to 4% simple interest, term up to 40 years from conversion or 55 years when senior public lenders require, 1.10x to 1.20x combined DSCR, 90% combined LTV, payments deferred during construction and deferred developer fee then amortized, residual receipts only when state or federal lenders require it. Nonprofit managing general partner required; applicants need prior affordable housing ownership experience. $12,500 good-faith deposit at full application; annual administration fee 12.5 bps of loan (max $15,000) or 5 bps of bonds (min $15,000). SHRA issues the CTCAC local needs letter and conduit bonds (see separate bond record). Applicants request gap funds without specifying a source; SHRA picks the source, so Davis-Bacon exposure depends on whether HOME (12+ units) or CDBG (8+ units) is assigned, and state prevailing wage applies unless the below-market-loan exemption (40% of units at 80% AMI for 20 years) fits. Pre-applications are timed so SHRA commitments are in hand before CTCAC and CDLAC deadlines, satisfying CDLAC readiness and the CTCAC tiebreaker. SB 802 (Chapter 417, Statutes of 2026) requires the County and cities to form the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency, a regional JPA that absorbs SHRA, by January 1, 2028; expect program administration, fee funds and NOFA calendars to migrate to that agency during 2027-2028.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/multi-family-housing-financing-lending-resources/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**SHRA Multifamily Mortgage Revenue Bonds (Housing Authority of the County of Sacramento conduit issuance)**
- Agency: Housing Authority of the County of Sacramento, staffed by SHRA
- Type: bond
- Typical award: No minimum or maximum bond amount; sized to the senior lender's loan. Issuance fee 25 bps of the bond amount at closing; annual administration fee 5 bps of outstanding bonds (minimum $15,000) plus $100 per SHRA-regulated non-bond unit per year; when another issuer is used SHRA still charges up to 0.25% for TEFRA review. CDLAC fees and 0.5% performance deposit pass through.
- AMI requirement: Federal and state minimum: 20% of units at 50% AMI, or 30% at 60% AMI plus 10% at 50% AMI (H&S Code 34312.3); non-competitive CTCAC projects targeting above 60% AMI must average no more than 59% AMI. Rents capped at 30% of 50% or 60% AMI. Bond regulatory agreement for the qualified project period or 55 years from 50% occupancy, incorporating the CDLAC resolution.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Pre-application and full application on the SHRA schedule tied to CDLAC rounds (2026: applications March 2 and Sept 1 for the July/Sept and March/May 2027 CDLAC rounds). Inducement resolution and TEFRA hearing go to the Board of Supervisors or City Council before the CDLAC filing.)
- Layering notes: Local alternative to CalHFA, CSCDA and CMFA for 4% deals in the City and unincorporated county (other cities by cooperation agreement). SHRA requires projects using other issuers to meet SHRA affordability and due diligence standards and pays for TEFRA review. Nonprofit owners seeking the welfare exemption must show ongoing day-to-day involvement (services, management or maintenance) under SHRA policy 9.1.11. Bonds alone are generally not treated as public funds for state prevailing wage, but SHRA gap loans or HOME in the same deal usually are. Private placements waive the investment-grade rating requirement. SB 802 (Chapter 417, Statutes of 2026) requires the County and cities to form the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency, a regional JPA that absorbs SHRA, by January 1, 2028; expect program administration, fee funds and NOFA calendars to migrate to that agency during 2027-2028.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2026-SHRA-Multifamily-Lending-Policies-County-final.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sacramento Continuum of Care (CA-503) funding and PBV PSH referral system**
- Agency: Sacramento Steps Forward (CoC lead agency and HMIS lead) for the City and County of Sacramento
- Type: other
- Typical award: HUD CoC Program awards described as tens of millions per round across more than 30 renewal projects; competition now runs every other year. 2026 competition: local letters of intent for new projects due July 2, 2026, local applications July 14, 2026, consolidated application to HUD Sept 30, 2026. Youth Homelessness Demonstration Program total $8,082,381 across seven projects (as of 2025).
- AMI requirement: CoC PSH serves households experiencing homelessness (chronic homelessness priority) through the Coordinated Access System; rental assistance and leasing, not capital.
- Cycle: biennial (next: 2026 round closed (HUD submission Sept 30, 2026). Next local competition expected 2028 under the every-other-year cycle, with renewals in between.)
- Layering notes: CoC dollars rarely fund capital, but CoC PSH rental assistance or leasing can replace PBV as the operating subsidy on a share of units, and CoC participation is required for Homekey+ and HHAP coordination. For PBV PSH buildings, SSF runs the lease-up: referrals begin 120 days before the temporary certificate of occupancy, are over-referred by 20%, and target full occupancy within 60 days of TCO, with SHRA paying the subsidy within 30 days of move-in. The 2023 City-County Affordable Housing Plan (SHRA, SSF, City, County) tracks a 649-unit PSH pipeline and a proposed joint PSH fund.
- Source: https://www.sacramentostepsforward.org/sacramento-continuum-of-care/2026-coc-program-competition-notice-of-funding-opportunity/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sacramento County Affordable Housing Fee Deferral and Waiver Program (county impact fees and SacSewer impact fees)**
- Agency: County of Sacramento Department of Finance with SHRA certification; Sacramento Area Sewer District (SacSewer, formerly SASD and Regional San) for sewer impact fee waivers
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Deferral or waiver of county development impact fees (Antelope, North Vineyard Station, Vineyard and Florin Vineyard plan area fees, library facilities fees, county transportation development fees) and SASD/Regional San sewer impact fees. Waivers are proportional to the share of very low-income units. Annual cap on waivers: greater of 200 dwelling units or 5% of prior-year new residential permits in the unincorporated county. SacSewer reported over $900,000 waived across five projects as of October 2025. $1,000 application fee; deferrals require a 10% fee deposit.
- AMI requirement: Deferral: at least 10% of units affordable to very low income (50% AMI) or 49% affordable to low income (80% AMI). Waiver: at least 10% of units at or below 50% AMI. Fees become due with interest penalties if the units are not delivered within 24 months.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling, before building permit issuance; requires an SHRA Certification Letter, preliminary title report, and a recorded agreement, note and deed of trust (subordinable to construction financing). Deferred fees are due at close of escrow or 24 months after the agreement, whichever is first.)
- Layering notes: County impact fee relief applies to unincorporated projects; SASD and Regional San sewer impact fee waivers are available in all cities in the county and the unincorporated area. Sewer impact fees in Sacramento run several thousand dollars per unit, so the waiver is worth roughly $5,000 to $10,000 per very-low-income unit; it reduces basis and improves the CTCAC cost-containment score. Labor Code 1720(b)(4) treats fees waived or forgiven by a public agency as public funds, so a waiver can itself trigger state prevailing wage unless the project is otherwise exempt; confirm with counsel. City of Sacramento has its own fee deferral and fee reduction program (Pass 3).
- Source: https://feedeferral.saccounty.gov/us/en/affordable-housing.html
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sacramento County Affordable Housing Incentive Program (density bonus, Zoning Code Section 6.5.4)**
- Agency: Sacramento County Planning and Environmental Review
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State Density Bonus Law as implemented in the County Zoning Code: 20% bonus for 5% very low income rising to 50% at 15%; 20% for 10% low income rising to 50% at 20%; 80% bonus for 100% affordable projects (up to 20% moderate) plus a three-story or 33-foot height increase within a half mile of a major transit stop; separate schedules for senior, special needs, moderate-income for-sale and land donation. Up to five concessions (one for 5% VLI, three at 15% VLI, four at 16% to 99%, five for 100% affordable; one more for child care). Table 6.4.1 (added June 11, 2026) adds bonus for projects at or below 50% restricted units.
- AMI requirement: Minimum five units with 5% very low, 10% low, 10% moderate (for-sale), or special population set-asides; rental target units restricted 55 years with professional management; units built concurrently and dispersed on site.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling; filed with the entitlement application together with an Affordable Housing Strategies Memorandum; the County does not charge a processing fee for eligible applications (Housing Element page). Affordable Housing Agreement must be recorded before grading and building permits.)
- Layering notes: Parking maximums: 1 space studio and 1-bedroom, 1.5 for 2- and 3-bedroom, 2.5 for 4-bedroom; within a half mile of major transit 0.5 per bedroom, 0.5 per unit for 100% lower-income rental, none for special needs housing. Waivers of standards that physically preclude the project are unlimited but do not count as concessions. Land donation (one acre or 40 units, zoned 30+ units per acre) earns bonus and can satisfy the AHO. Combine with the AHO fee exemption (20% affordable at 17+ du/ac) and the Countywide Rezone Program sites for by-right or streamlined approval. 100% affordable LIHTC projects typically use the 80% bonus and parking reductions to cut costs rather than add units.
- Source: https://landuse.saccounty.gov/szc/ch6/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sacramento County Affordable Housing Ordinance Fund (AHO affordability fee)**
- Agency: County of Sacramento (Planning and Environmental Review collects fee); SHRA administers the fund and lends it through the Multifamily Lending Program
- Type: loan
- Typical award: $6,000,000 available for new County projects as of June 1, 2026. 2024 fee revenue $5,128,243; year-end 2024 balance $11.1M ($6.3M uncommitted). 2014 to 2024 cumulative $32.8M, funding eight projects (650 units); AHO money averaged about 9% of total development cost and reached 61% of new affordable units built in the unincorporated area. Typical commitments $400,000 to $6.4M per project (Cornerstone South $6.43M for 29 AHO units; San Juan $5.2M for 21 units; Beech Hill $2.47M for 11 units).
- AMI requirement: Fund-assisted units: 20% at 50% AMI and 80% at 60% AMI, workforce (labor force) households, 55 years. At least 10% of fees must buy down units to extremely low income (30% AMI); at least 50% of fees must produce housing within large (750+ unit) development projects, which steers money to the Vineyard, North Vineyard Station, Florin Vineyard and similar growth areas.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Awarded only through SHRA's twice-yearly multifamily pre-application rounds (next expected January 2027). Fee side: $3.91 per habitable square foot of market-rate unit as of March 27, 2026, adjusted each spring by the ENR building cost index.)
- Layering notes: Unincorporated-county-only money (fee is charged only on unincorporated residential permits). Normally paired with 4% credits plus bonds or 9% credits, SHRA HOME, PLHA and the County Housing Trust Fund in one SHRA award. Developer side of the ordinance: market-rate projects may instead dedicate land, build units, enter a development agreement with an Affordable Housing Strategy, or buy banked unit credits from SHRA; multifamily projects with at least 20% affordable units at 17 or more units per acre are exempt from the fee, which is itself a meaningful incentive for mixed-income rental. AHO is a local below-market loan, so the Labor Code 1720(c)(6)(E) exemption may apply if no HOME or CDBG is in the stack; confirm with counsel. The County is mid-way through an AHO amendment study (Housing Element Program E3): KMA found 2% to 14% affordable production versus the 10% target; Board workshop Oct 6, 2026, nexus study through spring 2027, ordinance hearings expected winter 2027, possibly adding an inclusionary option and resource-area targeting. SB 802 (Chapter 417, Statutes of 2026) requires the County and cities to form the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency, a regional JPA that absorbs SHRA, by January 1, 2028; expect program administration, fee funds and NOFA calendars to migrate to that agency during 2027-2028.
- Source: https://planning.saccounty.gov/us/en/planning-environmental-review/projects/long-range/aho.html
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sacramento County Behavioral Health (MHSA/BHSA) housing capital for permanent supportive housing**
- Agency: Sacramento County Department of Health Services, Behavioral Health Services, with SHRA screening projects through its multifamily rounds
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Not published. SHRA's June 2025 funds notice stated projects may also be considered for County Behavioral Health MHSA housing program financing for PSH units serving homeless adults with serious mental illness; the 2022-23 MHSA plan dedicated $11M of Community Services and Supports funds to the MHSA Housing Program (secondary source). Recent example: $2.5M MHSA to Auburn Oaks PSH (Hope Cooperative, with SHRA). Beginning July 1, 2026 the Behavioral Health Services Act requires 30% of county BHSA funds for housing interventions, half of that for chronically homeless people, with up to 25% of housing dollars allowed for capital development.
- AMI requirement: PSH units restricted to persons with serious mental illness or substance use disorder who are homeless or at risk, referred through County Behavioral Health; typically 30% AMI or below with rental or operating subsidy.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No stand-alone capital NOFA found as of 2026-11-06; requests ride on SHRA multifamily pre-applications (next expected January 2027). County BHSA Integrated Plan covers July 2026 to June 2029 and is the document to watch for a housing capital line; the County is also procuring a Flexible Housing Pool administrator for BHSA rental subsidies.)
- Layering notes: Successor to the CalHFA Special Needs Housing Program and county NPLH allocations (NPLH is closed; Sacramento County ran its last Request to Partner in 2020). BHSA capital and operating subsidies can underwrite PSH units in 4% and 9% deals and satisfy CTCAC's nonprofit set-aside homeless priority (MHSA listed as qualifying capital) and the CDLAC Homeless set-aside. Pair with Homekey+ (county received one award in 2025) and HHAP. County funds are public funds for prevailing wage. Amounts and application process should be confirmed with Behavioral Health Services and SHRA before underwriting.
- Source: https://dhs.saccounty.gov/us/en/behavioral-health/about/bhsa.html
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sacramento County Housing Trust Fund (commercial linkage fee)**
- Agency: County of Sacramento (Chapter 16.89, fee collected at building permit); SHRA administers and lends the fund
- Type: loan
- Typical award: $900,000 available as of June 1, 2026 (was $0 in June 2025). 2024 fee revenue $1,605,084; year-end 2024 balance $6.36M ($1.2M uncommitted). Since 1991: $33.1M in fees, 29 developments, 3,037 total units. Recent loans $816,000 to $5.25M per project (San Juan $5.25M for 21 HTF units; Terracina at Wildhawk $4.6M for 18 units).
- AMI requirement: Households at 50% to 80% of county AMI who are in the labor force (jobs-housing linkage); 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Through SHRA multifamily pre-application rounds (next expected January 2027). Current fee schedule (2024 report): office and hotel $3.26, research and development and commercial $2.17, manufacturing and warehouse $1.08 per gross square foot, adjusted annually.)
- Layering notes: Oldest local source (1990). Revenue tracks commercial construction and is volatile, so the balance is usually smaller than the AHO fund. Used mainly for new construction workforce units at 50% to 80% AMI, which complements deeper-targeted HOME or MHP units in the same project. Local below-market loan; state prevailing wage exemption may be available absent federal funds. The companion City of Sacramento Housing Trust Fund (City Code 18.56) is a separate pool covered in Pass 3. SB 802 (Chapter 417, Statutes of 2026) requires the County and cities to form the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency, a regional JPA that absorbs SHRA, by January 1, 2028; expect program administration, fee funds and NOFA calendars to migrate to that agency during 2027-2028.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/ATT-1-Housing-Trust-Fund-Annual-Report-for-2024.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption processing, Sacramento County Assessor**
- Agency: Sacramento County Assessor, Institutional Exemptions Section (BOE issues the Organizational Clearance Certificate)
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Exemption of the qualifying low-income share of assessed value from the roughly 1.1% to 1.2% total property tax rate in Sacramento County (plus any direct levies that remain). Annual filing with the Assessor.
- AMI requirement: Per state law: lower-income units (80% AMI, 140% continuation rule) under LIHTC or a recorded regulatory agreement; limited partnerships need a nonprofit or eligible LLC managing general partner with an OCC and a Supplemental Clearance Certificate.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Annual claims due February 15 (next Feb 15, 2027; postmark next business day if a weekend or holiday). First-time filers submit BOE-267 with the BOE-277 OCC and the low-income supplemental affidavit (BOE-267-L or 267-L1 for partnerships). Contact (916) 875-0720 or ASR-InstitutionalExemptions@saccounty.gov.)
- Layering notes: Sacramento assessor applies the statewide rules (see Pass 1 BOE record); no county-specific abatement JPA exists. SHRA bond policy 9.1.11 additionally requires the nonprofit owner to have material day-to-day involvement (services, management or maintenance) for bond-financed projects claiming the exemption, which aligns with BOE managing-GP scrutiny. File the construction-period claim as soon as the OCC is issued and the site is under development to avoid a year of taxes.
- Source: https://assessor.saccounty.gov/content/assessor/us/en/exemptions/nonprofit-exemptions.html
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


### San Benito County

**Affordable housing in-lieu fee fund and impact fee waivers for 100% affordable developments**
- Agency: San Benito County Resource Management Agency, Planning and Building; County Executive Office (impact fee program)
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Section 21.03.012 (as amended December 13, 2022) allows in-lieu fees to fund an affordable housing trust and to reimburse the County for impact fee waivers granted to developments that are 100% affordable. Fee balance and any waiver approvals were not published in the 2026 Board agendas; the FY 2024-25 Annual Development Impact Fee Report (adopted January 13, 2026) covers the County's public facilities impact fee funds administered since the late 1980s. Historic voluntary affordable housing fees were $4,500 per market-rate unit.
- AMI requirement: Waiver reimbursement applies only to 100% affordable developments; trust fund uses follow Chapter 21.03.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA. Request an impact fee waiver with the entitlement application and confirm the in-lieu fund balance with Planning and Building; the County has not issued a housing fund NOFA.)
- Layering notes: San Benito County is a small non-entitlement county with no housing department: the Resource Management Agency (Planning and Building, Abraham Prado, (831) 637-5313) administers the inclusionary ordinance, in-lieu fees, density bonus and TEFRA hearings; the Health and Human Services Agency (Community Services and Workforce Development) runs HHAP, the shelter and the Migrant Center; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz administers vouchers and PBVs for Hollister and San Juan Bautista. The County has no HOME or CDBG entitlement, no housing trust fund NOFA and no County loan program; Hollister runs its own PLHA and CDBG programs (Pass 3). sanbenitocountyca.gov and the amlegal code library block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors packets on cosb.granicus.com (attachments on d2kbkoa27fdvtw.cloudfront.net), HCD allocation tables and hacosantacruz.org. Confirmation needed: the ordinance language creating the waiver reimbursement was read in the 2022 Board packet, but the current code text, the fund balance and any adopted waiver procedure could not be opened (sanbenitocountyca.gov and amlegal 403). Treat as a negotiable cost reduction for a 100% affordable project rather than a funded program.
- Source: https://d2kbkoa27fdvtw.cloudfront.net/cosb/6404819104d9eef39ad033a9e4860b430.pdf
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-07)

Dollar amounts, any small-county housing exemption request and any capital development project were not in the Board packet; the County's BHSA page lists supporting documents for a capital development project and for housing production need but the site blocks automated access.

- Cycle: rolling (next: Behavioral Health plans requests for proposals to contract a range of providers to meet BHSA goals and a 62% licensed workforce gap; approach the department about housing intervention contracts for FY 2026-27 and later.)

**Conduit multifamily revenue bonds through CMFA with County TEFRA approval**
- Agency: San Benito County Board of Supervisors (TEFRA hearings via Resource Management Agency); California Municipal Finance Authority as issuer
- Type: bond
- Typical award: Baler Place, 340 Bridgevale Road, Hollister (CRP Baler Place LP, 53 family units, three stories, all-electric): TEFRA hearing and Resolution 2026-55 approving up to $21,000,000 of CMFA tax-exempt bonds on April 28, 2026; the County joined the CMFA joint powers agreement at the same meeting and may receive a share of issuance fees. The project also holds 25 project-based vouchers from the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz.

The County is not an issuer and bears no liability; it hosts TEFRA hearings for CMFA (and could for CSCDA or CalPFA) because Hollister projects sit in the county. - Type: density bonus

The CoC allocation of $3,725,577.82 is shared with Monterey County through the Coalition of Homeless Services Providers. Prior rounds: HHAP-1 $308,848.53, HHAP-2 $141,188, HHAP-3 $547,468 (plus a $294,330 CoC HHAP-1 pass-through for rapid rehousing). HHAP-7 preliminary allocations: County $786,253.74, CoC $4,166,944.25.

HHAP is the County's only recurring homelessness money and it is fully committed to shelter operations, so it is not a capital or operating source for new PSH; a developer would need to negotiate with the Coalition of Homeless Services Providers for CoC-level funds or PBV referrals. **Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (Chapter 21.03 Affordable Housing Regulations) and in-lieu fee**
- Agency: San Benito County Resource Management Agency, Planning and Building (sbcplan@sanbenitocountyca.gov, (831) 637-5313)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement rather than a subsidy: 15% of units affordable on site (for-sale and rental) or 20% off site under Sections 21.03.006 and 21.03.007; developments of 1 to 5 units exempt, 6 to 10 units pay the in-lieu fee automatically, 11 or more units build on or off site and pay the fee on fractions below 0.7 unit. In-lieu fee adopted December 13, 2022 (Ordinance 1052, Resolution 2022-149): $30 per square foot for for-sale homes based on the 15% requirement (Vernazza Wolfe affordability gap study). Before 2022 developers paid a negotiated $4,500 per unit through development agreements (Sunnyside Estates). Example compliance: Lands of Lee subdivision affordable housing plan approved September 15, 2026 under Resolution 2024-146 (about 30 affordable units, 21% of the project).
- AMI requirement: Very low (50% AMI), low (80% AMI) and moderate (120% AMI) income units per Section 21.03.003; off-site rental projects of 24 or more lots carry an updated income breakdown (Ordinance 1059, September 12, 2023); affordable housing plans are approved by the Planning Commission and Board (Housing Advisory Committee dissolved 2023).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies with each land use entitlement. The Board's Housing Policy Reform Ad Hoc Committee (formed February 11, 2025) has reviewed Chapter 21.03 but no 2025 or 2026 amendment was found in Board agendas; in-lieu payments qualify only where sites are on septic or wells, zoned for large lots, or more than 10 miles from an incorporated city.)
- Layering notes: San Benito County is a small non-entitlement county with no housing department: the Resource Management Agency (Planning and Building, Abraham Prado, (831) 637-5313) administers the inclusionary ordinance, in-lieu fees, density bonus and TEFRA hearings; the Health and Human Services Agency (Community Services and Workforce Development) runs HHAP, the shelter and the Migrant Center; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz administers vouchers and PBVs for Hollister and San Juan Bautista. The County has no HOME or CDBG entitlement, no housing trust fund NOFA and no County loan program; Hollister runs its own PLHA and CDBG programs (Pass 3). sanbenitocountyca.gov and the amlegal code library block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors packets on cosb.granicus.com (attachments on d2kbkoa27fdvtw.cloudfront.net), HCD allocation tables and hacosantacruz.org. For an affordable developer the ordinance matters in two ways: market-rate projects in the Hollister fringe (Santana Ranch, Lands of Lee, builder's remedy filings) must deliver or fund affordable units, and Section 21.03.008(F) lets them satisfy the obligation by donating land or subsidizing a nonprofit rental project, which is the County's only recurring local capital source. In-lieu fees go to an affordable housing fund (see separate record). Source is the December 13, 2022 Board packet adopting the amendments and fee; current code text is on codelibrary.amlegal.com (blocked to automated access).
- Source: https://d2kbkoa27fdvtw.cloudfront.net/cosb/6404819104d9eef39ad033a9e4860b430.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

- Agency: San Benito County Resource Management Agency, Planning and Building
- Type: other
- Typical award: Not a subsidy: adopted October 14, 2025 under the Local Tenant Preferences to Prevent Displacement Act (Government Code 7061). Preferences for displaced San Benito County residents, neighborhood residents, local workers and school district employees, households with high housing costs, families with children in a local school district, agricultural employees, seniors and persons with disabilities. The ordinance was developed by the Board's Housing Policy Reform Ad Hoc Committee with the San Benito High School District, which wants workforce housing for teachers; developers should expect the County to ask for school employee and local worker preferences in any affordable housing agreement. **Project-Based Vouchers for San Benito County projects, Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz**
- Agency: Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (serves Hollister and San Juan Bautista; Hollister office (831) 637-0487; PBV RFP contact Eric Johnson, Senior Analyst)
- Type: other
- Typical award: HACSC had 823 PBVs in 33 developments as of February 2025, including in Hollister: Sunrise Senior Apartments (48, CHISPA), West of Fairview (40, in development) and Baler Place (25, in development). PBVs are awarded through a competitive RFP (last issued October 2024 as the Fall 2024 PBV RFP); the planned Spring-Summer 2025 RFP was cancelled because of federal funding uncertainty and no RFP has been reissued as of November 2026. Scoring: 200 base points (project type 50, waiting list opportunities 30, poverty deconcentration 25, location 25, experience 25, readiness 25, leverage 10, design 10) plus bonus points; 150-point threshold; supportive services supplement 40 points with a 30-point threshold.
- AMI requirement: PBV tenants pay 30% of income; HCV waiting list, site-based lists or Coordinated Entry referrals; HACSC administrative plan has carried a limited preference for homeless San Benito households.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No open RFP. Join the HACSC PBV RFP interest list (Eric Johnson) to be notified; HACSC states it is not in HUD shortfall but is holding new commitments. Twice-yearly RFPs timed to CTCAC rounds were the stated plan before the pause.)
- Layering notes: San Benito County is a small non-entitlement county with no housing department: the Resource Management Agency (Planning and Building, Abraham Prado, (831) 637-5313) administers the inclusionary ordinance, in-lieu fees, density bonus and TEFRA hearings; the Health and Human Services Agency (Community Services and Workforce Development) runs HHAP, the shelter and the Migrant Center; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz administers vouchers and PBVs for Hollister and San Juan Bautista. The County has no HOME or CDBG entitlement, no housing trust fund NOFA and no County loan program; Hollister runs its own PLHA and CDBG programs (Pass 3). sanbenitocountyca.gov and the amlegal code library block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors packets on cosb.granicus.com (attachments on d2kbkoa27fdvtw.cloudfront.net), HCD allocation tables and hacosantacruz.org. PBVs from HACSC are the only operating subsidy available to San Benito projects and they underwrite the Baler Place and West of Fairview deals; with the RFP paused, new projects must line up the next round or rely on tenant-based vouchers. Sources are the HACSC request for proposals page (RFP status), the PBV program page, the 2022 owner guidance PDF and the February 2025 in-service PBV project list.
- Source: https://hacosantacruz.org/general-information/vendors-contracts/request-for-proposals/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**State CDBG (non-entitlement) and CDBG-DR applications by the County**
- Agency: San Benito County Executive Office (Esperanza Colio Warren) and Resource Management Agency, Public Works
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2025 State CDBG NOFA application of $3,600,000 approved March 31, 2026 (Resolution 2026-41): $3,000,000 San Benito County Free Library renovation, $300,000 senior and homeless food assistance, $300,000 youth enrichment; the NOFA allowed up to $1,500,000 for housing programs and $3,000,000 for housing projects but the Board chose non-housing uses. CDBG-DR Infrastructure $1,911,246 plus Mitigation Resilient Infrastructure and Planning $9,563,290 (total $11,474,536, applications due February 28, 2026, projects complete by Q2 2030) programmed for roads.
- AMI requirement: CDBG national objective (low and moderate income benefit).
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: HCD's 2025 CDBG application window ran February 2 to April 3, 2026; the 2026 NOFA is expected in early 2027 with a $3.6 million jurisdiction cap and a three-application limit.)
- Layering notes: San Benito County is a small non-entitlement county with no housing department: the Resource Management Agency (Planning and Building, Abraham Prado, (831) 637-5313) administers the inclusionary ordinance, in-lieu fees, density bonus and TEFRA hearings; the Health and Human Services Agency (Community Services and Workforce Development) runs HHAP, the shelter and the Migrant Center; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz administers vouchers and PBVs for Hollister and San Juan Bautista. The County has no HOME or CDBG entitlement, no housing trust fund NOFA and no County loan program; Hollister runs its own PLHA and CDBG programs (Pass 3). sanbenitocountyca.gov and the amlegal code library block automated access, so records were built from Board of Supervisors packets on cosb.granicus.com (attachments on d2kbkoa27fdvtw.cloudfront.net), HCD allocation tables and hacosantacruz.org. The County is eligible for state CDBG and HOME as a non-entitlement jurisdiction but has not used either for multifamily housing; a developer could ask the County to sponsor a state HOME or CDBG housing application, as nonprofit developers suggested at the August 20, 2025 Board workshop on affordable and farmworker housing. Source is the March 31, 2026 Board packet.

**Welfare exemption from property tax for nonprofit-owned affordable housing, San Benito County Assessor**
- Agency: San Benito County Assessor, Tom J. Slavich (1131 San Felipe Road, Hollister)
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Standard state welfare exemption for low-income housing owned by nonprofits or limited partnerships with a nonprofit managing general partner holding a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate; the County's BOE-267-H-A supplemental form is published for low-income housing units.
- AMI requirement: Units restricted to households at or below 80% AMI under a recorded regulatory agreement; exemption prorated to qualifying units.
