**Welfare exemption (property tax) processing, Santa Clara County Assessor**
- Agency: Santa Clara County Assessor, Exemptions Division (Assessor Neysa Fligor, 408-299-5500)
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Full exemption of property tax on qualifying low-income rental units owned by a nonprofit or by a limited partnership with a nonprofit managing general partner holding a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate; worth roughly 1.1 to 1.2 percent of assessed value per year plus most direct levies.
- AMI requirement: Recorded regulatory agreement with a public agency or tax credit restriction; annual BOE-267 (first filing) or BOE-267-A (annual) with BOE-267-L, L1 or L2 supplemental affidavits and tenant income data.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: February 15, 2027 for the 2027-28 lien date (late filings accepted with reduced exemption through the following year).)
- Layering notes: santaclaracounty.gov subdomains (osh, asr, bhsd, news, files) returned HTTP 403 to automated access on 2026-11-07; records were built from the Board of Supervisors IQM2 legislative files (sccgov.iqm2.com), the County Housing Element, Santa Clara County Housing Authority documents, HCD and BOE pages. The Assessor's own welfare exemption page (asr.santaclaracounty.gov/tax-savings/exemptions/welfare-exemption) is blocked to automated access, so contact details come from the BOE county contacts list. Santa Clara County is the subject of BOE's welfare exemption white paper on supplemental clearance certificates; assessors apply the exemption only after the BOE issues the OCC or SCC. File the BOE-267 claim in the first year after acquisition and again annually; underwrite the exemption in the operating budget from the first full lien date after completion. Larry Stone retired in 2025 and Neysa Fligor is the current Assessor.

### Santa Cruz County

**Affordable Housing Impact Fee (AHIF): exemption for deed-restricted affordable units and fee fund**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Community Development and Infrastructure, Planning Division, Housing Section
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Current rates (2026 Affordable Housing Guidelines, Chapter V): market-rate rental units $2 per habitable square foot (ADUs of 750 square feet or less exempt); ownership projects of fewer than seven units $2 to $15 per square foot scaled by unit size and project size (5 or more homes $15 per square foot at all sizes); non-residential $3 per net new square foot ($1 for agricultural barns). Deed-restricted affordable rental units, Measure J units and subsidized housing are exempt, and density bonus projects that include affordable rental units may qualify for a waiver of the fee on the market-rate units. Fees fund the AHIF account used in the County housing NOFA. The September 1, 2026 KMA nexus study supports maximum fees of $17.30 to $24.50 per square foot, far above current rates.
- AMI requirement: Exemption applies to units deed-restricted to lower income households; waiver on mixed-income projects negotiated with Housing staff in exchange for a set number of affordable rental units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Fees are due at building permit (Planning Director may defer to occupancy for hardship). Watch for a Board fee update in 2027 following the 2026 nexus and feasibility studies.)
- Layering notes: Santa Cruz County housing programs sit in the Community Development and Infrastructure Department (CDI) Planning Division, Housing Section (HousingProgramsInfo@santacruzcountyCA.gov, (831) 454-2332), which runs the Measure J inclusionary program, the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, LMIHAF, PLHA and the County housing NOFA; the Human Services Department's Housing for Health Division (H4H, the CoC CA-508 lead) runs HHAP, Homekey and homeless programs; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (HACSC) supplies vouchers. The County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction for CDBG and HOME. County programs apply only to the unincorporated area (about half the population); Santa Cruz, Watsonville, Capitola and Scotts Valley run their own programs (Pass 3). cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov, santacruzcountyca.gov PDFs, santacruzhealth.org and housingforhealthpartnership.org open normally; the Board's IQM2 portal (santacruzcountyca.iqm2.com) renders by script and could not be listed, so Board items were taken from NOFA appendices and press coverage. The AHIF is both a cost the affordable developer avoids (exempt units) and the source of part of the County NOFA money; at $2 per square foot on rentals it raises little, which is why the NOFA is capped at $500,000 per award. Other County development fees (roadside, parks, drainage) are not waived for affordable projects in the guidelines. Source is the 2026 Affordable Housing Guidelines PDF (August 2026 revision).
- Source: https://cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov/Portals/35/CDI/Planning/Housing/2026%20AFH%208_10_26_1.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Behavioral Health Services Act Integrated Plan FY 2026-29: housing interventions (rental and operating subsidies, flexible housing subsidy pool)**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Health Services Agency, Behavioral Health Division (MentalHealth.ServicesAct@santacruzcountyca.gov)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Housing Interventions component $7,796,621 (FY 2026-27 BHSA funds) plus $942,228 of MHSA carryover for a year-one total of $8,738,849, $7,796,621 in year two and $8,267,735 in year three (30% of a BHSA allocation of about $26 million). Year-one uses: permanent rental subsidies $6,539,529 (260 units), bundled permanent rental and operating subsidies $914,623, interim housing bundled subsidies $781,451, operating subsidies on 200 units; no capital development projects and no housing exemption; FSP to BHSS transfer of $1,819,212 a year does not touch housing. Behavioral Health is building a flexible housing subsidy pool with Housing for Health that will serve BHSA-eligible people first, and new NPLH PSH units came online in spring 2026.
- AMI requirement: BHSA eligible adults and youth with serious mental illness or substance use disorder; at least half of housing funds for chronically homeless people.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Plan approved June 2026 after a March 18 to April 16, 2026 comment period and an April 16, 2026 public hearing. No NOFA; contact Behavioral Health housing staff about subsidy commitments for PSH units in 2027 and later.)
- Layering notes: Santa Cruz County housing programs sit in the Community Development and Infrastructure Department (CDI) Planning Division, Housing Section (HousingProgramsInfo@santacruzcountyCA.gov, (831) 454-2332), which runs the Measure J inclusionary program, the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, LMIHAF, PLHA and the County housing NOFA; the Human Services Department's Housing for Health Division (H4H, the CoC CA-508 lead) runs HHAP, Homekey and homeless programs; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (HACSC) supplies vouchers. The County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction for CDBG and HOME. County programs apply only to the unincorporated area (about half the population); Santa Cruz, Watsonville, Capitola and Scotts Valley run their own programs (Pass 3). cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov, santacruzcountyca.gov PDFs, santacruzhealth.org and housingforhealthpartnership.org open normally; the Board's IQM2 portal (santacruzcountyca.iqm2.com) renders by script and could not be listed, so Board items were taken from NOFA appendices and press coverage. Santa Cruz will not use BHSA for capital; its value to a developer is a 15 to 20 year project-based rental or operating subsidy on PSH units, which can replace or supplement PBVs while HACSC's RFP is paused. Source is the revised June 2026 Integrated Plan PDF on santacruzhealth.org.

**County Housing Funds NOFA for predevelopment and rehabilitation (LMIHAF, PLHA and Affordable Housing Impact Fee)**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Community Development and Infrastructure, Planning Division, Housing Section
- Type: loan
- Typical award: 2026 NOFA (authorized January 27, 2026; issued March 10, 2026; due April 9, 2026): about $1,500,000 total from the Low and Moderate Income Housing Asset Fund, PLHA (predevelopment only) and the Affordable Housing Impact Fee fund; minimum request $300,000, maximum $500,000, one to three awards. Board approved predevelopment commitments on May 19, 2026 to New Horizons Affordable Housing and MidPen Housing (amounts not published online). Staff stated the fund balances are too small for acquisition or construction loans, which typically need seven figures.
- AMI requirement: 100% affordable to lower income households (at or below 80% AMI); 55-year regulatory agreement recorded against fee title; rehabilitation loans fully deferred 55 years; predevelopment loans unsecured during predevelopment and converted to a secured 55-year deferred loan at building permit, repayable only if the applicant fails to perform.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Funds must be spent within three years of the loan agreement. No second 2026 round was announced; the January 2026 item described this as the first fair-process NOFA after numerous inquiries, so expect a similar early-year NOFA in 2027 if fund balances allow. Applicants must be 501(c)(3) developers that own the site (or in escrow under a PSA dated before the NOFA), with at least one completed and three-year-occupied affordable project in unincorporated Santa Cruz County for predevelopment requests.)
- Layering notes: Santa Cruz County housing programs sit in the Community Development and Infrastructure Department (CDI) Planning Division, Housing Section (HousingProgramsInfo@santacruzcountyCA.gov, (831) 454-2332), which runs the Measure J inclusionary program, the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, LMIHAF, PLHA and the County housing NOFA; the Human Services Department's Housing for Health Division (H4H, the CoC CA-508 lead) runs HHAP, Homekey and homeless programs; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (HACSC) supplies vouchers. The County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction for CDBG and HOME. County programs apply only to the unincorporated area (about half the population); Santa Cruz, Watsonville, Capitola and Scotts Valley run their own programs (Pass 3). cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov, santacruzcountyca.gov PDFs, santacruzhealth.org and housingforhealthpartnership.org open normally; the Board's IQM2 portal (santacruzcountyca.iqm2.com) renders by script and could not be listed, so Board items were taken from NOFA appendices and press coverage. This is the County's only capital program and it is deliberately small: it funds entitlement-stage costs and preservation, not construction gaps, and it is restricted to unincorporated sites and experienced local nonprofits (MidPen, New Horizons, Habitat Monterey Bay, Mid-Peninsula affiliates). A construction gap must come from state programs, Monterey Bay Housing Trust loans and HACSC PBVs. Source is the 2026 NOFA PDF; the Board authorization is Appendix B (January 27, 2026, item 53).
- Source: https://www.cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov/Portals/35/CDI/Planning/Housing/County%20of%20Santa%20Cruz%20NOFA%20Mar%2010%202026_1.pdf

**Density bonus, enhanced density bonus and priority processing (County Code Chapters 17.12 and 17.10.040)**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Community Development and Infrastructure, Planning Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus law implemented in Chapter 17.12 (last amended Ordinance 5286, 2018) with a County enhanced density bonus (Section 17.12.065) above the regular 35% state bonus: projects providing at least 21% low income units earn 36.5% rising 1.5 points per percentage point to a 50% maximum bonus at 30% low income units. Priority processing under Section 17.10.040 for standard-density projects with 25% or more affordable units and for density bonus projects with 35% or more affordable units.
- AMI requirement: Government Code 65915 thresholds (5% very low, 10% low, senior, moderate for-sale) with 55-year recorded restrictions; enhanced bonus requires 21% or more low income units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Requested with the development application; 2024 and 2025 state law changes (AB 2694, AB 1287) apply directly even where the 2018 chapter text lags.)
- Layering notes: Santa Cruz County housing programs sit in the Community Development and Infrastructure Department (CDI) Planning Division, Housing Section (HousingProgramsInfo@santacruzcountyCA.gov, (831) 454-2332), which runs the Measure J inclusionary program, the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, LMIHAF, PLHA and the County housing NOFA; the Human Services Department's Housing for Health Division (H4H, the CoC CA-508 lead) runs HHAP, Homekey and homeless programs; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (HACSC) supplies vouchers. The County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction for CDBG and HOME. County programs apply only to the unincorporated area (about half the population); Santa Cruz, Watsonville, Capitola and Scotts Valley run their own programs (Pass 3). cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov, santacruzcountyca.gov PDFs, santacruzhealth.org and housingforhealthpartnership.org open normally; the Board's IQM2 portal (santacruzcountyca.iqm2.com) renders by script and could not be listed, so Board items were taken from NOFA appendices and press coverage. The enhanced bonus to 50% is a real local incentive for mixed-income projects in the urban unincorporated area (Live Oak, Soquel, Aptos) where zoning density is low; 100% affordable projects rely on state law's unlimited bonus near transit. Source is Chapter 17.12 text on gocodebook.com.
- Source: https://gocodebook.com/library/us/ca/santa-cruz-county-planning/title-17/chapter-17.12-density-bonus
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Homeless Housing, Assistance and Prevention (HHAP) Rounds 5 and 6, County and Watsonville/Santa Cruz CoC CA-508**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Human Services Department, Housing for Health Division (housingforhealth@santacruzcountyca.gov, (831) 454-7312), administering entity for the Housing for Health Partnership CoC
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HHAP-5: $5,080,000 combined County and CoC award announced February 2024 (down $840,000 from HHAP-4) for shelters and transitional housing, outreach, PSH services and flexible assistance. HHAP-6: County $2,111,016.78 and CoC $2,254,602.21 (about $4.4 million block grant); application revisions submitted November 2025 with HCD still requesting changes in December 2025. HHAP-7 preliminary allocations: County $1,880,116.49, CoC $2,014,410.53.
- AMI requirement: Homeless and at-risk households; 10% youth set-aside; regional action plan with the four cities.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: No local capital NOFA; the Policy Board of the Housing for Health Partnership sets allocations. HHAP-7 planning runs through 2027. Contact Housing for Health about PSH operating or services funding tied to Coordinated Entry referrals.)
- Layering notes: Santa Cruz County housing programs sit in the Community Development and Infrastructure Department (CDI) Planning Division, Housing Section (HousingProgramsInfo@santacruzcountyCA.gov, (831) 454-2332), which runs the Measure J inclusionary program, the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, LMIHAF, PLHA and the County housing NOFA; the Human Services Department's Housing for Health Division (H4H, the CoC CA-508 lead) runs HHAP, Homekey and homeless programs; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (HACSC) supplies vouchers. The County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction for CDBG and HOME. County programs apply only to the unincorporated area (about half the population); Santa Cruz, Watsonville, Capitola and Scotts Valley run their own programs (Pass 3). cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov, santacruzcountyca.gov PDFs, santacruzhealth.org and housingforhealthpartnership.org open normally; the Board's IQM2 portal (santacruzcountyca.iqm2.com) renders by script and could not be listed, so Board items were taken from NOFA appendices and press coverage. HHAP underwrites the services side of PSH projects such as Veterans Village and Park Haven Plaza; it has not funded capital. The December 2025 Policy Board packet also records a CoC decision to prioritize HUD CoC rental assistance for PSH projects threatened by federal NOFO changes. **Measure J inclusionary housing program (County Code Chapter 17.10)**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Community Development and Infrastructure, Planning Division, Housing Section
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement dating from the 1978 Measure J initiative: ownership projects of seven or more units provide 15% of units as affordable for-sale homes (Section 17.10.030); alternatives are payment of the Affordable Housing Impact Fee at the unified fee schedule rate (17.10.034), the existing unit conversion program (17.10.037), a financial contribution to a nonprofit off-site project that yields more units than required (17.10.036), or 15% on-site rental units affordable to low income households for the life of the units (17.10.039). Rental projects pay the impact fee unless they provide the 15% rental units. Fees are deposited in the Measure J trust fund (17.10.087). The 2026 Affordable Housing Guidelines (August 2026) govern pricing, resale and eligibility.
- AMI requirement: For-sale units priced for moderate and lower income buyers under County guidelines with resale restrictions in perpetuity; rental alternative units at low income rents in perpetuity under a recorded rent regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies with each entitlement. The County released a Keyser Marston residential nexus study on September 1, 2026 and an inclusionary feasibility study; the Housing Advisory Commission's 2026-27 work program includes recommendations on the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, so fee or percentage changes may come to the Board in 2027.)
- Layering notes: Santa Cruz County housing programs sit in the Community Development and Infrastructure Department (CDI) Planning Division, Housing Section (HousingProgramsInfo@santacruzcountyCA.gov, (831) 454-2332), which runs the Measure J inclusionary program, the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, LMIHAF, PLHA and the County housing NOFA; the Human Services Department's Housing for Health Division (H4H, the CoC CA-508 lead) runs HHAP, Homekey and homeless programs; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (HACSC) supplies vouchers. The County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction for CDBG and HOME. County programs apply only to the unincorporated area (about half the population); Santa Cruz, Watsonville, Capitola and Scotts Valley run their own programs (Pass 3). cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov, santacruzcountyca.gov PDFs, santacruzhealth.org and housingforhealthpartnership.org open normally; the Board's IQM2 portal (santacruzcountyca.iqm2.com) renders by script and could not be listed, so Board items were taken from NOFA appendices and press coverage. For an affordable developer, Section 17.10.036 is the useful piece: market-rate builders may discharge their obligation by funding a nonprofit project that produces more affordable units than their own requirement, which is a potential local gap source on larger subdivisions. Source is Chapter 17.10 text on gocodebook.com (2026-07 edition); program narrative from the County's 2026 Affordable Housing Guidelines.
- Source: https://gocodebook.com/library/us/ca/santa-cruz-county-planning/title-17/chapter-17.10-affordable-housing-requirements

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), County five-year plan**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Community Development and Infrastructure (plan and LMIHAF match) and Human Services Department, Housing for Health Division (homeless activities)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2019-2023 allocations: $565,952, $895,101, $916,102, $480,214 and $332,575 (estimated five-year total $3,395,700); amended 2023 plan spends 75% on Activity 6 homeless assistance through Housing for Health (rapid rehousing, rental assistance, navigation center and shelter operating and capital costs, PSH development or preservation), 20% on Activity 4 as a match deposited to the County LMIHAF for lower-income rental development and predevelopment, and 5% administration. HCD formula allocations for 2024-2028: $457,140 (2024), $467,923 (2025), estimated $2,857,125 five-year. PLHA predevelopment loans were offered through the March 2026 County NOFA.
- AMI requirement: PLHA eligible activities; development uses limited to lower-income rental housing with 55-year affordability.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Development dollars reach projects only through the County housing NOFA (next expected early 2027). The 2024-2028 PLHA plan activities were not located online; confirm with CDI Housing whether the LMIHAF match share continues.)

**Project Homekey permanent supportive housing sponsored by the County (Veterans Village, Park Haven Plaza, Casa Azul, Freedom House)**
- Agency: Santa Cruz County Human Services Department, Housing for Health Division, with nonprofit and private development partners

**Project-Based Vouchers, Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz**
- Agency: Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (2160 41st Avenue, Capitola; (831) 454-9455; PBV RFP contact Eric Johnson, Senior Analyst)
- Type: other
- Typical award: 823 PBVs in 33 developments as of February 2025 (El Centro 44, St. Stephens Senior 39, Water Street 33, Pippin Orchards 31, Resetar 52 and others) plus commitments to projects in development including Veterans Village (15). PBVs are awarded through a competitive RFP (October 2024 was the last, with a plan for two rounds a year timed to CTCAC); the Spring-Summer 2025 RFP was cancelled because of federal funding uncertainty and no RFP has been reissued as of November 2026. Scoring 200 base points (project type 50, HCV waiting list opportunities 30, poverty deconcentration 25, location 25, experience 25, readiness 25, leverage 10, design 10), 150-point threshold, 40-point supportive services supplement.
- AMI requirement: Tenants pay 30% of income; HCV waiting list, site-based lists or Coordinated Entry referrals; HACSC administers special purpose vouchers (Mainstream, FUP, VASH) that can be project-based.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No open RFP; HACSC is not in HUD shortfall but is holding new commitments. Join the PBV RFP interest list to be notified; HACSC publishes rent, utility allowance and waiting list letters for CTCAC applications on its PBV page.)
- Layering notes: Santa Cruz County housing programs sit in the Community Development and Infrastructure Department (CDI) Planning Division, Housing Section (HousingProgramsInfo@santacruzcountyCA.gov, (831) 454-2332), which runs the Measure J inclusionary program, the Affordable Housing Impact Fee, LMIHAF, PLHA and the County housing NOFA; the Human Services Department's Housing for Health Division (H4H, the CoC CA-508 lead) runs HHAP, Homekey and homeless programs; Behavioral Health holds BHSA; and the Housing Authority of the County of Santa Cruz (HACSC) supplies vouchers. The County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction for CDBG and HOME. County programs apply only to the unincorporated area (about half the population); Santa Cruz, Watsonville, Capitola and Scotts Valley run their own programs (Pass 3). cdi.santacruzcountyca.gov, santacruzcountyca.gov PDFs, santacruzhealth.org and housingforhealthpartnership.org open normally; the Board's IQM2 portal (santacruzcountyca.iqm2.com) renders by script and could not be listed, so Board items were taken from NOFA appendices and press coverage. PBVs have been the operating subsidy in nearly every recent Santa Cruz County affordable deal; with the RFP paused since spring 2025, developers are turning to BHSA and HHAP subsidies and to CoC rental assistance. Source is the HACSC request for proposals page (RFP status); project list from the February 2025 in-service PBV list and selection rules from the 2022 owner guidance.

### Solano County

**County conduit multifamily housing revenue bonds (none; CMFA used in Solano County)**
- Agency: Solano County (not an active conduit issuer); California Municipal Finance Authority used instead
- Type: bond
- Typical award: No county issuance program found. Recent Solano deals used CMFA: $75,750,000 for Step Up Housing / Align Finance, Vallejo (Nov 18, 2025); $32,131,176 for Sacramento Street Apartments, Vallejo (74 units, 55-year restriction, 2026).
- AMI requirement: Per CDLAC: at least 20% of units at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI, 55-year regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: Not applicable. Use CMFA, CSCDA or CalHFA; the Board of Supervisors or city council holds the TEFRA hearing.)
- Layering notes: Negative finding recorded so later passes do not search again. Solano 4% deals pair CMFA bonds with CTCAC credits, city HOME or inclusionary funds and the County Housing Trust Fund. Conduit bonds alone do not trigger state prevailing wage.
- Source: https://www.cmfa-ca.com/2025/11/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**HOME and CDBG, Solano County (state non-entitlement status; 2024 CDBG planning grants)**
- Agency: California HCD state CDBG and HOME programs; Solano County Department of Resource Management as applicant
- Type: grant
- Typical award: No county HOME or CDBG entitlement. State CDBG 2024 awards to the County of Solano: $300,000 Affordable Housing Development Site Feasibility Study and $300,000 Pocket/Cottage Housing Feasibility Study (Resolution 2024-213 of Dec 10, 2024; award Sept 3, 2025; $559,909 contract with Lisa Wise Consulting approved May 5, 2026, through June 30, 2028). No County of Solano award appears in HCD's HOME 2022-2023 awardee list. Fairfield, Vacaville and Vallejo are HUD entitlements (Pass 3).
- AMI requirement: State HOME: 20% of HOME units at 50% AMI, rest at 60% AMI, 55-year HCD covenant. State CDBG: 51% of units at or below 80% AMI.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Follow HCD state HOME and CDBG NOFAs. The County's feasibility studies run May 2026 to August 2027 (public workshop Aug 27, 2026, survey closed Sept 7, 2026) and will identify five potential housing areas in the unincorporated county; a developer solicitation could follow in 2027-2028.)
- Layering notes: Negative finding for capital: the County has no HOME or CDBG construction pipeline of its own. The CDBG-funded studies matter because they are the County's first step toward sponsoring affordable multifamily or cottage housing on unincorporated sites under the 2023-2031 Housing Element (adopted Feb 6, 2024). A developer wanting state HOME in Dixon, Rio Vista or the unincorporated area needs the County or city to apply as grantee. HOME and CDBG carry Davis-Bacon (CDBG 8+ units, HOME 12+ units) and state prevailing wage.
- Source: https://solano.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?GUID=B9DE2910-60B7-468B-9A2B-1299341BC12F&ID=8000024&Options=&Search=
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Housing First Solano Continuum of Care (CA-518) and HHAP homelessness funding**
- Agency: Solano County Health and Social Services, Behavioral Health Division (County HHAP grantee); CAP Solano JPA (CoC collaborative applicant, HMIS lead and CoC HHAP administrator)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: County HHAP: Round 1 $1,256,129 (board and care beds), Round 2 $574,231 and Round 3 $1,607,846 (Fair Haven Commons and rental subsidies), Round 4 $1,470,396 ($873,703 to Abode Services for mental health rental subsidies), Round 5 $1,655,549 (accepted April 8, 2025, tenancy supports through June 30, 2029), Round 6 allocation $1,968,380.51 (awarded in the April 2026 batch with the CoC's $2,102,264.22, about $4,100,000 combined, for interim housing operations). CoC HHAP Round 5 $1,725,588.34. The 2026-27 state budget HHAP allocation gives the same pair about $4,070,000. CoC HHAP Round 4 permanent housing RFP offered $470,167.50 (2024). HUD CoC awards: $2,247,299 (FY 2024); FY 2025 round 1 $642,829 for STOP Plus. State dashboard: $13,600,000 HHAP rounds 1-5 to the Solano area, $6,500,000 direct to the County. State ESG 2024 via CA-518: $186,481 to Vacaville Solano Services Corporation.
- AMI requirement: HUD CoC and HHAP target people experiencing homelessness; HHAP allows PSH capital, operating subsidies and rental assistance (no cap on permanent housing uses per HCD's Dec 2025 memo), but Solano has programmed its rounds to rental subsidies, tenancy supports and interim housing.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: CAP Solano JPA runs the HUD CoC local competition each summer and periodic HHAP RFPs; its site (capsolanojpa.gov) and housingfirstsolano.org blocked automated access, so check them directly for the 2026 CoC listing and any HHAP Round 6 or 7 RFP. HHAP Round 5 funds must be 75% obligated by June 30, 2026.)
- Layering notes: For PSH underwriting the usable pieces are County Behavioral Health rental subsidies and tenancy services (Abode Services contract, $26,142,891 for Dec 2023 to June 2026) and HHAP operating commitments, not capital. Solano has no housing sales tax measure. The 2026 PIT count was Jan 29, 2026. HHAP capital is state money and triggers prevailing wage.
- Source: https://solano.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?ID=7291491&GUID=A415697E-4FE2-46DB-929C-E21AE8EAA9B3&Options=&Search=&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), Solano County non-entitlement formula share**
- Agency: Solano County Administrator's Office (HCD formula grantee)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HCD CY 2024-2028 Formula Allocation NOFA: Solano County CY 2024 $569,130 and CY 2025 $596,313; five-year estimate 2024-2028 $3,557,063. Earlier: 2020 award $128,234; 2023 allocation $560,188 (Five-Year Plan amendment affirmed by the Board July 23, 2024). Cities of Fairfield ($390,910 in 2021), Vacaville and Vallejo ($505,369 in 2021) receive their own entitlement shares (Pass 3).
- AMI requirement: PLHA statute: rental assistance at or below 60% AMI unless the plan targets up to 120% AMI (150% in high-cost areas); 55-year covenant for rental development. County five-year plan governs uses.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: HCD releases formula allocations annually; the County files a plan amendment and resolution each round (last affirmed July 23, 2024). No County NOFA to developers found. The 2020 Housing Authority MTW plan proposed using about $25,000 a year of County PLHA for down payment assistance to FSS graduates.)
- Layering notes: About $570,000 to $600,000 a year, more than Napa or Sonoma's county share, but the County has not published how it programs the money. Ask the County Administrator's Office whether the 2024-2028 plan funds multifamily gap loans, ADUs or homeless services before counting it. State funds, so prevailing wage applies to construction.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/sites/default/files/docs/grants-and-funding/plha/2024-2028-plha-formula-allocation-nofa.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers, Solano County Housing Authority (Dixon, Rio Vista and unincorporated area)**
- Agency: Solano County Housing Authority (SCHA), administered under contract by the City of Vacaville Housing Authority, 40 Eldridge Ave Suite 2, Vacaville, 707-449-5675
- Type: other
- Typical award: HCV-only agency authorized for 290 vouchers in its 2020 Moving to Work plan (255 leased; 20 HUD-VASH, 13 Mainstream), listed at 372 vouchers by a 2024 directory. The 2020 MTW plan set aside up to 60 vouchers for project-basing. PBV waitlists opened for Silvey Villa senior units in Dixon (Aug 2024), new PBV lists on Jan 7, 2025, and eight units at Prospera at Homestead, Dixon (June 10, 2025).
- AMI requirement: Households at or below 50% AMI (75% of admissions at 30% AMI); PBV units limited to 25% of a building unless serving elderly, disabled or supportive services households.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No PBV RFP found. Owner proposals go to the Vacaville Housing Authority staff who run SCHA; the Vacaville Housing Authority's own Dec 2024 RFP awarded 85 PBVs to the Elan Apartments in Vacaville (city program, Pass 3).)
- Layering notes: SCHA covers only Dixon, Rio Vista and the unincorporated county; Fairfield, Vacaville, Vallejo, Suisun City and Benicia have their own housing authorities (Pass 3). Small voucher pool, so PBV awards run 8 to 20 units per project, typically on Dixon deals that also take County Housing Trust Fund money. SCHA is a Moving to Work agency (2020 plan), which allows local PBV flexibilities. PBVs alone do not trigger state prevailing wage; Davis-Bacon applies to PBV new construction or rehab of 9 or more contract units. Vacaville's housing pages (cityofvacaville.gov) returned 403 to automated access; the MTW plan on solano.legistar.com is the opened source.
- Source: https://solano.legistar.com/gateway.aspx?M=F&ID=68d29896-8ed9-449f-b711-96eb02a7dc29.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Public facilities fee waiver or deferral for affordable housing (none adopted, Solano County)**
- Agency: Solano County Department of Resource Management (County Code Chapter 11, Article X public facilities fees)
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: None. In Jan 2024 The Hampstead Companies asked the Board to waive $672,829 in public facilities fees for Prospera at Homestead, which would have cut the Housing Trust Fund loan to $2,500,000; the Board did not act and funded the full $3,200,000 loan instead.
- AMI requirement: Not applicable.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No waiver or deferral program exists; requests are handled case by case through the Board.)
- Layering notes: Negative finding. Budget the County's public facilities fees in full for unincorporated and Dixon deals and ask for a Housing Trust Fund loan instead. If a waiver were granted it would count as public funds under Labor Code 1720(b)(4). City fee waivers and deferrals are Pass 3.
- Source: https://www.dailyrepublic.com/townnews/outdoors/supervisors-loan-3-2m-for-dixon-housing-project-grant-habitat-93k-for-homes-project/article_d936c004-af09-11ee-be9f-f794a966670a.html
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Solano County Behavioral Health Services Act (BHSA) housing interventions**
- Agency: Solano County Health and Social Services, Behavioral Health Division (BH Housing and Homelessness 707-553-5525, bhhousing@solanocounty.gov)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: FY 2026-29 Integrated Plan approved by the Board June 9, 2026. Year one (FY 2026-27) Housing Interventions component allocation $9,636,071 (30% of $32,120,235) plus $1,415,984 carryover, $11,052,055 available. Solano's population exceeds 200,000, so it cannot claim the small-county exemption; up to 25% of housing funds may go to capital development, half must serve chronically homeless people. Current delivery is through Abode Services (outreach, navigation, rental subsidy administration, rapid rehousing, PSH services at Blue Oak Landing and Fair Haven Commons) and the BHSA-funded ARCH program; an interim housing placement RFP closed July 9, 2026.
- AMI requirement: Adults and transition-age youth with serious mental illness or substance use disorder who are homeless or at risk; County BH client eligibility required.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Integrated Plan in effect July 1, 2026 with annual updates; the Abode contract term ended June 30, 2026 and its successor was not located. Ask BH Housing whether capital development (up to about $2,400,000 a year at the 25% cap) or project-based operating subsidies will be offered to PSH sponsors.)
- Layering notes: Roughly $9,600,000 a year makes Solano's BHSA housing pot the largest county-controlled housing source after HHAP; most of it will fund rental subsidies and services, which is what a PSH operating budget needs. Pair with Homekey+ or HHAP capital and SCHA or city PBVs. Public funds, so capital triggers prevailing wage. Budget figures from Appendix A of the adopted plan (content.solanocounty.gov).
- Source: https://www.solanocounty.gov/government/health-social-services-hss/behavioral-health/behavioral-health-services-act-bhsa/bhsa-integrated-plan
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Solano County Housing Trust Fund (SCHTF)**
- Agency: Solano County Administrator's Office with the Department of Resource Management
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Capitalized with $2,000,000 ARPA (ordinance introduced Aug 2, 2022), $1,700,000 ARPA added Dec 12, 2023, and about $200,000 a year from the County's General Fund Community Investment Fund ($400,000 CIF in the fund by Oct 2024), for a total near $4,900,000. Awards through a 2023 Notice of Funding Availability: $3,200,000 loan to The Hampstead Companies for Prospera at Homestead in Dixon (107 units at or below 60% AMI plus manager unit, 17-year term at 3% interest, approved Jan 2024) and $93,000 grant to Solano-Napa Habitat for Humanity (three homes, Fairfield). Balance was $1,800,000 in Oct 2024 when staff proposed $1,500,000 for site work on a County-owned 1.3-acre parcel at 580 North 1st Street, Dixon (up to 18 modular workforce units at 80% AMI). Aug 2026 Board item proposed $400,000 loan to EAH Housing for Tabor Commons, Fairfield (67 units, 20% to 50% AMI, 16 IDD units) so EAH could keep its tax credit award, plus $200,000 to Habitat for Harmony Village, Vacaville.
- AMI requirement: Very low to moderate income households; Prospera restricted to 60% AMI, County workforce site to 80% AMI. Loan payments recycle into the fund.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA open as of Nov 2026. The County's housing page says future funding opportunities will be announced; recent awards came from developer requests taken to the Board (Aug 4, 2026 meeting for Tabor Commons). ARPA dollars had to be obligated by Dec 31, 2024 and spent by Dec 31, 2026, so remaining capital is CIF money and loan repayments.)
- Layering notes: Solano's only county gap source and a small one. It is used as the last dollars in a 9% deal (Tabor Commons) or as a larger patient loan where the County wants RHNA-credited units in Dixon. ARPA-funded loans carry federal and state public funds rules, so prevailing wage applies. The County also pushed ARPA housing money through cities before the fund existed: $4,500,000 gap financing via the City of Fairfield for an EAH deeply affordable project (2022), $215,000 for Habitat homes in Fairfield, and $200,000 to MidPen for Sunset Pines acquisition (2021). A separate $2,000,000 ARPA ADU effort put $1,700,000 into the SCHTF and $300,000 into the North Bay ADU Center. Program documents (NOFA, guidelines) were on the County ARPA RFP page, which now returns 404; the Board reports on solano.legistar.com are the live record.
- Source: https://solano.legistar.com/LegislationDetail.aspx?From=RSS&ID=6875909&GUID=E83CDBDF-BE38-44EB-83D5-592A2F6BC9CC&FullText=1
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Solano County density bonus (state law applied; Density Bonus Information Guide, May 2025)**
- Agency: Solano County Department of Resource Management, Planning Services (707-784-6765)
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State Density Bonus Law benefits summarized in the guide: 20% to 50% more units, up to five concessions, unlimited waivers on showing of physical infeasibility, parking caps of 1 space (studio and 1-bdrm), 1.5 (2 and 3-bdrm), 2.5 (4+ bdrm). 100% affordable projects get 80% bonus or no maximum within one half mile of a major transit stop. Guide cites the May 2025 HCD AMI for Solano County of $124,600.
- AMI requirement: Government Code 65915 set-asides: 5% to 15% very low, 10% to 24% low, 10% to 44% moderate (for-sale only); 55-year rental covenant.
- Cycle: rolling
- Layering notes: No Solano County density bonus or inclusionary ordinance surfaced in Chapter 28 (Zoning) searches; recent Chapter 28 work has been ADU conformance (ZT-20-02, ZT-26-02) and the County defaults to state law where its code is silent. Unincorporated Solano is largely agricultural with limited sewer, so multifamily sites are rare outside the Homeacres area and County-owned parcels in Dixon. The guide is a two-page information sheet hosted by the City of Vallejo, which calls it the Solano County Density Bonus Guide; confirm with County Planning whether it is the County's document and whether a local ordinance exists. Municode and Vacaville pages returned 403.
- Source: https://www.cityofvallejo.net/common/pages/GetFile.ashx?key=h5k%2bAUZn
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption processing, Solano County Assessor**
- Agency: Solano County Assessor/Recorder, Exemptions Division, 675 Texas Street Suite 2700, Fairfield, 707-784-6210, exemptions@solanocounty.gov
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Full exemption of property tax on qualifying low-income rental units under Revenue and Taxation Code 214(g) once the BOE issues an Organizational Clearance Certificate (BOE-277); the Assessor rules on property use.
- AMI requirement: Units occupied by households at or below 80% AMI (140% AMI over-income allowance on BOE-267-L3); regulatory agreement, LIHTC or public financing required; managing general partner a qualifying nonprofit or qualifying LLC.
- Cycle: rolling (next: File BOE-267 (first filing) or BOE-267-A (annual) with BOE-267-L, L1 and L2 supplements by February 15 each year; late claims through December 31 receive a partial exemption.)
- Layering notes: Standard statewide exemption (Pass 1 BOE record), recorded per county because the assessor administers it. Solano has no local property tax abatement JPA. The Assessor's page points to BOE Publication 149; office hours Monday to Friday 9 a.m. to 4 p.m.
- Source: https://www.solanocounty.gov/government/assessorrecorder/assessor-division/property-tax-savings/nonprofit-property-tax-exemptions
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


### Sonoma County

**Affordable Housing Program fees (residential in-lieu fee and non-residential Workforce Housing fee)**
- Agency: Permit Sonoma (collection and compliance); fees deposited to the County Fund for Housing administered by the CDC
- Type: other
- Typical award: Residential: every unit in unincorporated Sonoma County must build affordable units or pay an in-lieu fee graduated by size; 2026 schedule $3,108 for units of 1,000 sq ft or less rising roughly $155 per 10 sq ft (about $19,000 at 2,000 sq ft, about $29,000 at 2,750 sq ft), paid before building permit final. Non-residential (Workforce Housing Program) 2026: office, commercial and hotel $3.69 per sq ft; industrial, warehousing and agricultural processing $3.80; retail and services $6.38; first 2,000 sq ft exempt. Both adjust each January 1 by the ENR Bay Area construction cost index.
- AMI requirement: On-site alternative for rental projects: at least 15% of units affordable to low and very low income, or 10% to very low and extremely low income; ownership projects 20% split between low and moderate income. Exemptions include ADUs, farmworker and agricultural employee housing, replacement of fire-destroyed homes within 10 years, and projects complying through other approved means (Code 26-89-045).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Compliance method must be selected with the first permit application; fee schedules reset every January 1 (2027 schedule due January 2027).)
- Layering notes: This is the revenue engine for CFH and the inclusionary rule that mixed-income rental developers in the unincorporated county must satisfy; a 100% affordable LIHTC project satisfies the requirement outright and pays no fee. Developers of market-rate or mixed projects can also meet it by land dedication or restricting existing units. The fee is separate from the traffic and parks impact fees that the Board waived for affordable projects in 2025-2026.
- Source: https://permitsonoma.org/regulationsandlongrangeplans/regulationsandinitiatives/housing/housinginitiatives/residentialprogram
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Community Development Block Grant (CDBG), Sonoma Urban County**
- Agency: Sonoma County Community Development Commission (CDC) for the Urban County
- Type: grant
- Typical award: FY 2026-27 estimate: $3,145,583 for affordable housing projects, non-housing capital projects and microenterprise assistance, plus $247,872 for fair housing public services (the FY 2026-27 figure includes reprogrammed prior-year funds; the Consolidated Plan expects about $1.66M annual allocation). FY 2025-26: $1.88M total, with $941,605 to the countywide housing rehabilitation program and the rest to infrastructure and child care.
- AMI requirement: Units for households at or below 80% AMI (51% of units in assisted rental projects); affordable housing projects ready to proceed to construction preferred.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: FY 2026-27 round closed April 3, 2026; next NOFA expected February 2027 (applications early April 2027).)
- Layering notes: Urban County entitlement area = unincorporated county plus Cloverdale, Cotati, Healdsburg, Rohnert Park, Sebastopol, Sonoma and Windsor; Santa Rosa and Petaluma are separate HUD entitlements (Pass 3). CDC lists affordable housing projects as a CDBG eligible use, but new construction remains limited by federal rules (CBDO carve-out) and most housing CDBG goes to the CDC's rehabilitation program and off-site infrastructure. Davis-Bacon applies at 8 or more units. Useful for site infrastructure or acquisition-rehab tied to a 4% deal.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/notice-of-funding-availability-(nofa)-fiscal-year-(fy)-2026-2027
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**County Fund for Housing (CFH)**
- Agency: Sonoma County Community Development Commission (CDC)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: FY 2026-27 NOFA: $8,000,000 available (up from $1,500,000 in FY 2025-26 and $1.5M in FY 2024-25). FY 2025-26 awards: Freebird Development, Healdsburg, 42 units, $1,340,417; Burbank Housing, Guerneville, 21 units, $159,583. Minimum loan $100,000; maximum limited to one program year's funds unless the Board commits future years. Since 2003 the fund has invested over $24M in more than 550 homes.
- AMI requirement: Rental: all assisted units affordable 55 years, initially to very low income (50% AMI), may float up to low income (80% AMI) on turnover. Priorities: most units at lowest incomes, geographic distribution across cities and unincorporated area, Housing Opportunity Sites, integration with health and human services, PSH and special needs. Homeownership: first-time buyers at or below 120% AMI with 20% of units at 80% AMI.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: FY 2026-27 NOFA issued Feb 21, 2026, applications due April 3, 2026 (mandatory TA workshop March 4 or 5), CD Committee recommendations May 13, Board adoption July 7, 2026. Next NOFA expected late February 2027 with an early April deadline; projects ready to proceed to construction get preference.)
- Layering notes: Sonoma's main local gap source, capitalized by County General Fund, Reinvestment and Revitalization Funds (former RDA), developer in-lieu fees from the residential Affordable Housing Program, non-residential Workforce Housing fees, countywide transient occupancy tax, and loan repayments. Residual receipts loans structured to be compatible with LIHTC investor requirements; interest set by the Commission. Awarded in one annual NOFA together with HOME, CDBG, ESG and LMIHAF so applicants file once. Funds may go to projects inside cities only where the city has a RHNA Sharing Agreement with the County (shelters and congregate facilities exempt). Sponsors must comply with the County Living Wage Ordinance; the policy is silent on prevailing wage, so the Labor Code 1720(c)(6)(E) below-market-loan exemption may apply unless HOME or CDBG is also used. The County does not act as a conduit bond issuer; recent Sonoma deals used CMFA (Burbank Housing $8.12M) and CalHFA. CFH awards are typically $1M to $3M and are counted as local leverage for CTCAC tiebreaker and CDLAC readiness.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/development-services/community-development-commission/divisions/community-development/funding-opportunities/county-fund-for-housing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**HOME Investment Partnerships Program, Sonoma Urban County**
- Agency: Sonoma County Community Development Commission (CDC) for the Urban County
- Type: loan
- Typical award: FY 2026-27 estimate: HOME $743,322 plus CHDO set-aside $99,090 (Consolidated Plan expects about $660,693 per year plus $60,000 program income). FY 2025-26: $495,519 HOME (George's Hideaway, Burbank Housing, $185,519; tenant-based rental assistance $310,000) plus $99,103 CHDO to George's Hideaway.
- AMI requirement: HOME rules: 20% of HOME units at 50% AMI (Low HOME rent), remainder at or below 60% AMI; affordability period 5 to 20 years by subsidy, CDC loan policies extend to 55 years. Ready-to-proceed projects preferred.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: FY 2026-27 round closed April 3, 2026. Next NOFA expected late February 2027, applications early April 2027; Board adopts the Action Plan in July. NEPA review and Section 3 and Build America Buy America compliance required before agreement.)
- Layering notes: Urban County entitlement area = unincorporated county plus Cloverdale, Cotati, Healdsburg, Rohnert Park, Sebastopol, Sonoma and Windsor; Santa Rosa and Petaluma are separate HUD entitlements (Pass 3). Small allocation (well under $1M a year), so HOME is usually a minor layer on one project per year alongside CFH and PLHA. Davis-Bacon applies at 12 or more HOME-assisted units. The County routinely uses HOME for TBRA, leaving even less for development. Programs are awarded in the single CDC NOFA with CFH and CDBG.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/notice-of-funding-availability-(nofa)-fiscal-year-(fy)-2026-2027
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Low and Moderate Income Housing Asset Fund (LMIHAF), Sonoma County Housing Successor**
- Agency: Sonoma County Community Development Commission as housing successor to the former County redevelopment agency
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Up to $250,000 per year for homeless prevention and rapid rehousing services (FY 2025-26 and FY 2026-27 NOFAs). Fund balance available for all uses $2,509,338 at June 30, 2025; loans receivable $31.2M; FY 2024-25 expenditures $3.84M. Not currently offered for development.
- AMI requirement: Community Redevelopment Law housing provisions (very low and low income); current awards are homeless services, not capital.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Awarded in the CDC annual NOFA (FY 2026-27 closed April 3, 2026; next February 2027). Capital use would require a Commission decision; monitor the housing successor annual report.)
- Layering notes: Included so the fund is not mistaken for a development source: the County keeps LMIHAF for services and the former RDA Reinvestment and Revitalization money flows into CFH instead. Loan repayments from legacy RDA projects replenish the fund and could be redirected to capital in future years.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/Main%20County%20Site/Development%20Services/CDC/Documents/Successor%20Agency/Housing%20Successor%20Agency%20Annual%20Reports/Remediated_FY%2024-25%20Housing%20Successor%20Agency%20Annual%20Report.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Measure O Behavioral Health and Homelessness sales tax, housing category**
- Agency: Sonoma County Department of Health Services
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Quarter-cent countywide sales tax (approved 2020, 10-year term) generating about $30M to $33M a year across five categories, one of which is Transitional and Permanent Supportive Housing. Pathways to Sustainability NOFA (released Aug 14, 2025) awarded $10M in Measure O plus $2.4M HHAP to 26 projects on Feb 10, 2026; PSH awards went to Committee on the Shelterless ($392,034 Measure O and $196,017 HHAP) alongside city, shelter and services grants.
- AMI requirement: Transitional and permanent supportive housing for people with behavioral health conditions experiencing homelessness; awards to date are predominantly operating and services, not capital.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2025-26 round awarded Feb 10, 2026. The County says it hopes applicants 'continue to submit proposals for future projects' but has not announced a 2026-27 NOFA; the February 2026 release noted doubts about future funding levels. Measure sunsets around 2030-2031.)
- Layering notes: Best viewed as an operating and services subsidy for PSH units rather than a capital source; pair with HCD Homekey+, MHP supportive units and PBVs. Sonoma's BHSA (Prop 1) housing interventions requirement (30% of BHSA funds, half for chronically homeless) begins July 2026 and is administered by the same department; no BHSA capital NOFA was found. County funds are public funds for prevailing wage purposes if used for construction.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/board-of-supervisors-awards-124-million-in-measure-o-and-state-funds-for-mental-health-and-homeless-services
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Multi-Family Development Fee Deferral Program**
- Agency: Permit Sonoma
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Deferral of County development fees (traffic impact and parkland dedication fees; not school, water agency or sanitation district fees) from building permit final until permanent financing closes, under a recorded Fee Deferral Agreement.
- AMI requirement: Affordable rental or ownership housing for low- or very low-income households, or special needs housing (elderly or disabled) with at least 20% of units for low-income households.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling; request by letter with the permit application. Approval requires a Director finding that the fees are not needed for near-term programmed improvements; agreement signed before building or grading permit.)
- Layering notes: Bridges construction-period cash flow so impact fees are paid from permanent sources at conversion, a common pairing with CalHFA or bond-financed 4% deals. Deferral terms (interest, security) are not published; a deferred fee charged at less than market terms may be treated as public funds under Labor Code 1720(b)(4). Separate single-family ADU affordable-fee deferral exists for restricted ADUs.
- Source: https://permitsonoma.org/regulationsandlongrangeplans/regulationsandinitiatives/housing/housinginitiatives/multi-familydevelopmentfeedeferralprogram
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), County of Sonoma**
- Agency: Sonoma County Community Development Commission (CDC) for the County of Sonoma
- Type: loan
- Typical award: FY 2025-26 distribution $979,795 (Burbank Housing Guerneville 21 units $555,795; Habitat for Humanity Sebastopol 4 ownership units $424,000). Separate NOFAs: Sept 10, 2025 for rental or ownership projects (due Oct 30, 2025); Sept 17, 2026 for affordable owner-occupied workforce housing only, $674,360 over the counter. Residual receipts loans.
- AMI requirement: County PLHA Five-Year Plan: 75% to multifamily rental for households at or below 50% AMI, 20% to owner-occupied workforce housing up to 120% AMI, 5% administration. 55-year restrictions.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Rental PLHA next expected in the CDC's fall 2026 or early 2027 NOFA (2025 pattern: September release, Oct 30 deadline); AOWH PLHA open over the counter since Sept 17, 2026 until the $674,360 is exhausted. Geography excludes the City of Santa Rosa and City of Petaluma, which receive their own PLHA.)
- Layering notes: State formula money with no federal strings; small annual amounts (under $1M) make it a top-up to CFH rather than a primary source. Mandatory technical assistance workshop before applying. Nonprofit and government applicants only. Below-market residual receipts loan, so the Labor Code 1720(c)(6)(E) prevailing wage exemption may apply absent federal funds.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/notice-of-funding-availability-–-permanent-local-housing-allocation-funds
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers for Permanent Supportive Housing, Sonoma County Housing Authority**
- Agency: Sonoma County Housing Authority (division of the CDC); jurisdiction is all of Sonoma County except the City of Santa Rosa
- Type: other
- Typical award: 2025 PSH RFP: up to 100 project-based vouchers for studio, one- and two-bedroom PSH units in new construction or substantial rehab; applications were due June 24, 2025; 20-year HAP contract with automatic 20-year renewal; one-time $500 per voucher administrative fee plus billed-back environmental and legal review. Board of Commissioners committed 150 future PBVs to PSH (Nov 20, 2024) to support Homekey+ applicants. The authority administers about 2,988 vouchers and roughly $45M a year.
- AMI requirement: PSH for chronically homeless households referred through the Sonoma County Coordinated Entry System under its PSH prioritization policy; HCV income limits.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2025 RFP closed June 24, 2025. A further RFP for the 150 committed PSH vouchers is to be released 'when funding is available'; no 2026 RFP was posted as of 2026-11-06. Watch the CDC Doing Business page and HUD's 2026 pause on new PBV commitments.)
- Layering notes: PBV HAP revenue is the operating subsidy that makes Homekey+, MHP supportive units and CFH-funded PSH underwritable in Sonoma; the 150-voucher commitment was adopted specifically to strengthen Homekey+ applications. Santa Rosa projects must use the Santa Rosa Housing Authority (Pass 3). Prior RFPs: 2018 and 2023 general rounds (up to 100 vouchers, $1,000 fee), 2022 existing-housing round (110 vouchers to 8 projects).
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/Main%20County%20Site/Development%20Services/CDC/Documents/2025%20RFP%20for%20PSH%20PBV.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**RED Housing Fund (Renewal Enterprise District, PG&E wildfire settlement capital)**
- Agency: RED Housing Fund, a nonprofit capitalized with $10M each from the County of Sonoma and the City of Santa Rosa (PG&E 2017 fire settlement), affiliated with the Renewal Enterprise District JPA
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Round 1 (Jan 2022 NOFA): about $20,000,000 in subordinate gap loans, designed to cover roughly 10% of cost on four or five higher-density projects and leverage $180M to $220M (known award: $2.4M to Aviara in Santa Rosa). Round 2 (NOFA issued March 4, 2026, applications due April 1, 2026): up to $5,000,000 in loans, recommendations expected by May 18, 2026. Interest rate, term and per-project maximum are in the NOFA PDF, not on the site.
- AMI requirement: Infill, mid- to high-density residential or mixed-use projects in transit-rich or planned areas meeting RED criteria; income mix may be affordable (up to 80% AMI), middle income (80% to 120% AMI), market rate, or mixed.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 round closed April 1, 2026 (recommendations by May 18, 2026). Rounds have run in 2022 and 2026 as capital is repaid; no 2027 date announced. Email apply@redhousingfund.org for a secure upload link when a NOFA is open.)
- Layering notes: Lender of last resort (recycling repaid settlement capital) for projects the County and Santa Rosa want built in downtown Santa Rosa and other priority development areas; it accepts market-rate and middle-income deals that cannot use CFH or LIHTC. Capital is public settlement money, so prevailing wage exposure depends on loan terms. The separate private Sonoma County Housing Fund ($10M, Housing Trust Silicon Valley with the Santa Rosa Metro Chamber, predevelopment and acquisition loans up to five years) belongs in Pass 4.
- Source: https://redhousingfund.org/notice-of-funding-availability/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sonoma County Density Bonus Program (Code Chapter 26, Sections 26-89-050 and 26-89-060)**
- Agency: Permit Sonoma
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State Density Bonus Law (Gov. Code 65915 to 65917) applied directly: the County acknowledges its Chapter 26 provisions are out of date and states it will comply with current state law until Housing Element Program 15 amendments are adopted (adding lower-income student housing, 100% affordable bonuses, and updated bonus and incentive calculations). Affordable Housing Incentives under Section 26-89-060 also available to projects building inclusionary units on site.
- AMI requirement: State thresholds (5% very low, 10% low, 10% moderate for-sale, 100% affordable, senior, special populations); county inclusionary units may be counted toward the bonus.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling with the entitlement application; code update timing not published (Housing Element 2023-2031 Program 15). Contact Permit Sonoma Housing at (707) 565-1391.)
- Layering notes: Because the local code lags the statute, rely on Gov. Code 65915 as amended (up to 80% bonus and unlimited waivers for 100% affordable projects, 3-story height bonus near major transit, reduced parking) and expect staff to process requests case by case. Pair with the inclusionary on-site option so the same units earn the bonus and satisfy the Affordable Housing Program with no in-lieu fee. Permit Sonoma also maintains Streamlined Housing Developments (SB 35 and AB 2011 style) and Rezoning Sites for Housing pages for by-right sites.
- Source: https://permitsonoma.org/regulationsandlongrangeplans/regulationsandinitiatives/housing/housinginitiatives/densitybonusprogram
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Sonoma County Homeless Coalition (CoC CA-504) funding: HUD CoC competition and Homeless Services Consolidated NOFA (HHAP, ESG, HHIP)**
- Agency: Sonoma County Department of Health Services, Homelessness Services Division, as CoC collaborative applicant
- Type: other
- Typical award: 2026 HUD CoC competition: about $4.9M in renewal funding; HUD had not announced CoC Bonus or DV Bonus amounts; local applications due July 21, 2026. Annual Homeless Services Consolidated NOFA (state ESG, one-time HHAP, DHCS HHIP and local sources): about $5.5M expected in the 2025-26 cycle (applications due Feb 7, 2025). HHAP rounds 1 to 5 brought $32.2M to the Sonoma area; HHAP Round 5 allowed one CoC Builds PSH construction project.
- AMI requirement: Homeless households via Coordinated Entry; CoC PSH rental assistance and services; HHAP permits permanent housing capital as an eligible use.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 CoC round closed July 21, 2026 (new project categories offered were TH, SSO and DV Bonus only; no new PSH). Next Consolidated NOFA expected January 2027 with a February deadline, based on the 2025 cycle. HHAP Round 6 and 7 disbursements flow through the County.)
- Layering notes: Operating-side partner for PSH: CoC rental assistance or HHAP operating subsidy can replace or supplement PBVs on supportive units. HHAP capital for permanent housing is allowed but Sonoma has directed most HHAP to shelter, outreach and rehousing. Developers should engage the Homelessness Services Division early so a PSH project appears in the CoC's regional action plan and Coordinated Entry referral agreements.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/2026-notice-of-funding-opportunity-coc-program-competition-and-sonoma-county-coc-request-for-proposals-(rfp)
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Traffic and Parks Impact Fee Waiver for Affordable Housing (2025-2026)**
- Agency: Sonoma County Board of Supervisors; administered through Permit Sonoma
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Waiver of County traffic mitigation and parks impact fees for affordable projects in the unincorporated area that received building permits from Feb 1, 2025 through Dec 31, 2026; staff estimated up to $19.5M in fees waived across 15 eligible projects (over 1,900 affordable units) with a $1.5M General Fund backfill.
- AMI requirement: Units reserved for moderate-, low- and very low-income renters or buyers; recorded deed restriction of at least 55 years for rental and 45 years for ownership units. Minimum affordable percentage not stated in the County release; confirm with Permit Sonoma.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Expires Dec 31, 2026 unless extended; the County is studying its fee structure and may adopt a permanent program. Permits must be issued before the sunset.)
- Layering notes: Worth several thousand dollars per unit in the unincorporated county and reduces basis for the CTCAC cost containment score. Labor Code 1720(b)(4) counts fees waived or forgiven by a public agency as public funds, so a waiver can itself trigger state prevailing wage unless the project is otherwise exempt; most LIHTC projects taking HCD money already pay prevailing wage. Does not cover the affordable housing in-lieu fee, school, water agency or sanitation district fees. Multi-family fee deferral (separate record) remains available after the waiver sunsets.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/sonoma-county-board-of-supervisors-reduces-fees-for-affordable-housing-projects
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption processing, Sonoma County Assessor**
- Agency: Sonoma County Clerk-Recorder-Assessor, Exemption Unit
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Exemption of the qualifying low-income share of assessed value from the roughly 1.1% to 1.2% property tax rate plus voter-approved debt in Sonoma County.
- AMI requirement: Per state law: nonprofit or eligible LLC owner, or limited partnership with a qualifying managing general partner holding an Organizational Clearance Certificate; recorded regulatory agreement or deed restriction; tax savings must maintain affordability or reduce rents; plus government financing, LIHTC, or at least 90% lower-income occupancy.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Annual claims Jan 1 to Feb 15 (next Feb 15, 2027). First filing on newly acquired property due within 90 days after the first day of the month following acquisition or by Feb 15 of the following year, whichever is first; property must be in exempt use or under construction within 180 days of acquisition. File BOE-267 with the OCC, regulatory agreement and the low-income supplemental affidavit. Exemption Unit (707) 565-1881, assessor@sonomacounty.gov.)
- Layering notes: Standard statewide rules (see Pass 1 BOE record). Sonoma's assessor page is unusually explicit about the 180-day use requirement and first-filing deadline, which matters for construction-period exemptions on land acquired mid-year. No county property tax abatement JPA exists.
- Source: https://sonomacounty.gov/administrative-support-and-fiscal-services/clerk-recorder-assessor/assessor/real-property/tax-savings-(exemptions-prop-19-and-more)/exemptions/welfare-exemption
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


### Ventura County

**Behavioral Health Services Act Integrated Plan FY 2026-29 (Housing Interventions component)**
- Agency: Ventura County Health Care Agency, Behavioral Health Department
- Type: other
- Typical award: Public comment on the FY 2026-27 to 2028-29 Integrated Plan closed in spring 2026 and the plan was due to DHCS June 30, 2026; 30% of the County's BHSA allocation is reserved for housing interventions (rental and operating subsidies, outreach, up to 25% capital). Dollar amounts, any capital projects and the flexible housing subsidy pool details were not read because hca.venturacounty.gov blocks automated access; the County issued RFP 226001 for a Flexible Housing Subsidy Pool operator (closed April 8, 2026) and partners with Lewis Road PSH on services.
- AMI requirement: BHSA eligible adults and youth with serious mental illness or substance use disorder; at least half of housing funds for chronically homeless people.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Contact Behavioral Health housing staff; the Lewis Road project (88 PSH units) is the first large BHSA-era PSH site with County services.)
- Layering notes: Ventura County's housing money runs through the County Executive Office, Community Development (CEO-CD, 800 S. Victoria Avenue, Ventura, (805) 654-2681), which administers the Urban County CDBG and HOME programs, the HOME Consortium, PLHA, General Fund housing NOFAs and Homekey sponsorships; the Resource Management Agency Planning Division handles density bonus, RHD zoning and streamlining; the Health Care Agency Behavioral Health holds BHSA; the Ventura County Continuum of Care (CA-611) sits in the CEO; and the Area Housing Authority of the County of Ventura (AHACV) supplies vouchers outside Oxnard, Ventura, Port Hueneme and Santa Paula, which have their own housing authorities. The nonprofit Housing Trust Fund Ventura County (HTFVC) is the de facto county revolving loan fund. The County has no inclusionary ordinance, linkage fee or dedicated housing tax. venturacounty.gov department pages block automated access, so records were built from vcportal.venturacounty.gov PDFs (Action Plan, PLHA plan, funding summaries), rma.venturacounty.gov, housingtrustfundvc.org, ahacv.org, HCD tables and press coverage. **CDBG and HOME, Ventura Urban County Entitlement Area and Ventura County HOME Consortium (FY 2026-27 Annual Action Plan)**
- Agency: Ventura County Executive Office, Community Development
- Type: grant
- Typical award: FY 2026-27: CDBG $1,450,705 annual allocation plus $185,000 prior-year funds ($1,635,705 total); HOME $1,206,409.18 plus $7,568 program income ($1,213,977.18). HOME development award: Many Mansions Mountain View II, Fillmore (49 units, five HOME-assisted) $1,092,580 including the CHDO set-aside; CDBG $120,000 to My Way Home for homebuyer assistance, $100,000 homeowner rehabilitation, up to $337,500 rental rehabilitation (10 units), $1,130,963 public facilities. Recent HOME development awards have been $1 million to $1.2 million per year to one project (Peoples Place 8 units, Villages at Westview II 6 units, Rancho Sierra 2 units). No ESG. Separately, $15,151,024 of state CDBG-DR was committed to Camino de Salud in Ojai (48 units, 24 PSH, construction began March 2026).
- AMI requirement: HOME rents and incomes at or below 80% AMI (County targets 60% and below) with HOME affordability periods of 20 years for new construction; CDBG national objective.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Funding cycle opens with a public hearing in early November (November 6, 2025 for FY 2026-27; 117 attendees), applications due in winter, project feedback meeting April 2, 2026, draft plan comment April 9 to May 9, 2026, Board adoption May 12, 2026. The FY 2027-28 cycle will open at a November 2026 hearing with applications due around January 2027.)

HOME is a $1 million soft loan on a CHDO or tax credit deal, usually paired with County PLHA, General Fund NOFA money and an HTFVC loan. Davis-Bacon applies at 12 or more HOME units. **Density bonus (Non-Coastal Zoning Ordinance Article 16) and Residential High Density zone ministerial approvals with affordability requirement**
- Agency: Ventura County Resource Management Agency, Planning Division (plan.counter@venturacounty.gov, (805) 654-2488)
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus and incentives implemented in NCZO Article 16 (Housing Element program HE-M update completed) and Coastal Zoning Ordinance Section 8175-6. Residential High Density (RHD) zone: seven parcels (four in El Rio/Del Norte, two in Santa Susana, one in the Thousand Oaks area) allow 20 to 22 units per acre by ministerial RHD Zoning Clearance with no hearing; standards updated September 16, 2025 (program HE-H). Each RHD project must choose 20% lower income, 10% very low income, 5% extremely low income, or 30% lower income through alternative compliance (land donation, acquisition assistance to an affordable developer in a same-or-higher resource area, or an equivalent action). Piru expansion area projects must keep 10.5% of units affordable to low income households.
- AMI requirement: Government Code 65915 thresholds for the bonus; RHD affordability options as listed with recorded affordable housing agreements.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Requested with the zoning clearance or permit application; RHD Zoning Clearance checklist on the RMA RHD page.)
- Layering notes: Ventura County's housing money runs through the County Executive Office, Community Development (CEO-CD, 800 S. Victoria Avenue, Ventura, (805) 654-2681), which administers the Urban County CDBG and HOME programs, the HOME Consortium, PLHA, General Fund housing NOFAs and Homekey sponsorships; the Resource Management Agency Planning Division handles density bonus, RHD zoning and streamlining; the Health Care Agency Behavioral Health holds BHSA; the Ventura County Continuum of Care (CA-611) sits in the CEO; and the Area Housing Authority of the County of Ventura (AHACV) supplies vouchers outside Oxnard, Ventura, Port Hueneme and Santa Paula, which have their own housing authorities. The nonprofit Housing Trust Fund Ventura County (HTFVC) is the de facto county revolving loan fund. The County has no inclusionary ordinance, linkage fee or dedicated housing tax. venturacounty.gov department pages block automated access, so records were built from vcportal.venturacounty.gov PDFs (Action Plan, PLHA plan, funding summaries), rma.venturacounty.gov, housingtrustfundvc.org, ahacv.org, HCD tables and press coverage. The RHD zone is the County's one locally designed incentive: by-right density in exchange for a modest inclusionary share, with an alternative compliance path that lets market-rate builders fund an affordable developer's land purchase. Source is the RMA Affordable Housing in the RHD Zone page; the density bonus article is in the NCZO PDF on rmadocs.venturacounty.gov.

**Homekey and Homekey+ projects sponsored by the County (Request for Projects, Lewis Road Camarillo)**
- Agency: Ventura County Executive Office, Community Development, with Behavioral Health and development partners
- Type: grant

**Housing Trust Fund Ventura County revolving loan fund and residual receipts loan program (County-capitalized)**
- Agency: Housing Trust Fund Ventura County (nonprofit CDFI, Camarillo; Linda Braunschweiger, CEO)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Revolving Loan Fund: below-market loans of $250,000 to $5,000,000 for acquisition, predevelopment, gap and construction, generally 1 to 5 years, for projects with 30 to 55 year affordability; $53,795,034 approved or funded since 2013 producing 2,255 affordable homes; $18,425,566 of state LHTF matching funds leveraged 2020-2024 plus a $4,406,179 final-round LHTF award. County capital: $300,000 General Fund (2020), $1,500,000 ARPA (2022) and PLHA Activity 3 grants. New Residual Receipts Loan Program (about $4.6 million from a REAP 2.0 grant): 15 to 18 year residual receipts loans for new affordable rental housing at 0% to 80% AMI meeting infill criteria, in development by June 30, 2031; first round applications June 2 to July 2, 2026. Recent loans: Santa Clara Apartments, Ventura (Ventura Housing) $2,000,000 residual receipts, 18 years (2026); Gateway Grove, Santa Paula (Many Mansions, 21 studios at 30% AMI) $500,000 predevelopment (2026); Lockwood III, Oxnard $500,000 (February 4, 2026); Cypress Gardens Phase II, Oxnard (Peoples Self-Help) $1,500,000 (December 16, 2025); Fillmore Terrace II $1,000,000 (September 26, 2025); Aspire Apartments, Oxnard (Many Mansions) $750,000 (April 30, 2025).
- AMI requirement: Lower income (80% AMI and below) with priority for extremely low income, veterans, transition age youth, homeless households and farmworkers; residual receipts loans require 0% to 80% AMI units and long-term covenants.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Revolving Loan Fund: contact HTFVC to discuss the pipeline; invitations to apply follow. Residual Receipts Loan Program: the first round closed July 2, 2026; watch for a 2027 round timed to CTCAC applications.)
- Layering notes: Ventura County's housing money runs through the County Executive Office, Community Development (CEO-CD, 800 S. Victoria Avenue, Ventura, (805) 654-2681), which administers the Urban County CDBG and HOME programs, the HOME Consortium, PLHA, General Fund housing NOFAs and Homekey sponsorships; the Resource Management Agency Planning Division handles density bonus, RHD zoning and streamlining; the Health Care Agency Behavioral Health holds BHSA; the Ventura County Continuum of Care (CA-611) sits in the CEO; and the Area Housing Authority of the County of Ventura (AHACV) supplies vouchers outside Oxnard, Ventura, Port Hueneme and Santa Paula, which have their own housing authorities. **Inclusionary housing program and housing impact mitigation fee (studied, postponed June 9, 2026)**
- Agency: Ventura County Resource Management Agency, Planning Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Housing Element program HE-K study by Economic and Planning Systems (funded by REAP 2.0 through the Ventura Council of Governments, September 2025) modeled a 5% moderate-income requirement on for-sale projects and 5% low-income on rentals, and a commercial linkage fee of about $4 per square foot on projects generating more than 30 employees (estimated $1.2 million over seven years). On June 9, 2026 the Board directed staff to postpone any ordinance and revisit the question in the next RHNA cycle (five to eight years), citing 225% achievement of the low-income RHNA target and minimal projected yield (about 30 units in ten years).

- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No action expected before the 7th cycle Housing Element (2029-2031). Cities with inclusionary programs: Oxnard, Ventura, Moorpark, Thousand Oaks, Santa Paula, Port Hueneme.)
