**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), County five-year plan for the Urban County and Thousand Oaks**
- Agency: Ventura County Executive Office, Community Development
- Type: loan
- Typical award: HCD formula allocations to the County: $603,486 (2024), $646,422 (2025), estimated $3,771,788 for 2024-2028. Plan activities: rental capital development at or below 60% AMI (loans or operating subsidies), affordable ownership workforce housing grants, grants to Housing Trust Fund Ventura County (matched by the state LHTF program), homeless assistance (added May 3, 2022) and down payment assistance (added May 14, 2024). Awards: Rancho Sierra Senior Apartments (Camarillo area, 50 units) $751,263 (2021-22 and 2022-23); Camino de Salud (Ojai, 49 units) $955,873 (2023-24); Mesa Ojai construction gap and operations (2024-25); 2025 amendment of the 2023 allocation: Topa Vista Villas (Santa Paula, 112 units at 60% AMI) $750,000, Hillcrest Homes (Thousand Oaks, 78 for-sale units) $241,703, HomeNow down payment assistance $300,000 (approved April 29, 2025).
- AMI requirement: Rental units funded with PLHA restricted to 60% AMI and below (55 years); ownership workforce housing to 80% AMI (120% to 150% in recent amendments); 80% of HTFVC grant funds for 60% AMI households.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: PLHA is awarded through the same November to May cycle as HUD funds and through plan amendments when projects are ready; the County administers allocations for the Urban County cities and Thousand Oaks. The 2026 allocation year will be programmed in the FY 2027-28 cycle opening November 2026.)
- Layering notes: Ventura County's housing money runs through the County Executive Office, Community Development (CEO-CD, 800 S. Victoria Avenue, Ventura, (805) 654-2681), which administers the Urban County CDBG and HOME programs, the HOME Consortium, PLHA, General Fund housing NOFAs and Homekey sponsorships; the Resource Management Agency Planning Division handles density bonus, RHD zoning and streamlining; the Health Care Agency Behavioral Health holds BHSA; the Ventura County Continuum of Care (CA-611) sits in the CEO; and the Area Housing Authority of the County of Ventura (AHACV) supplies vouchers outside Oxnard, Ventura, Port Hueneme and Santa Paula, which have their own housing authorities. The nonprofit Housing Trust Fund Ventura County (HTFVC) is the de facto county revolving loan fund. The County has no inclusionary ordinance, linkage fee or dedicated housing tax. venturacounty.gov department pages block automated access, so records were built from vcportal.venturacounty.gov PDFs (Action Plan, PLHA plan, funding summaries), rma.venturacounty.gov, housingtrustfundvc.org, ahacv.org, HCD tables and press coverage. PLHA has been spread across rental projects, down payment assistance and HTFVC capitalization; the rental share is a $750,000 to $1 million soft loan at 60% AMI, typically layered with County HOME and General Fund money on the same project (Topa Vista Villas took PLHA, General Fund NOFA and city funds). Source is the April 29, 2025 PLHA plan amendment on vcportal.venturacounty.gov; 2024-2028 figures from HCD's PLHA NOFA tables.
- Source: https://vcportal.venturacounty.gov/CEO/community-dev/docs/pub-hearing/20250429/County_of_Ventura_PLHA_Plan_Amendment_website.pdf

**Streamlined ministerial approvals for affordable, supportive and farmworker housing (SB 35, AB 2162, AB 1783, SB 330) and Prohousing Designation**
- Agency: Ventura County Resource Management Agency, Planning Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Because the County has not met its above-moderate RHNA progress, qualifying multifamily projects with the required affordability are approved ministerially under SB 35 using the County's Streamlined Multifamily Zoning Clearance application; supportive housing is by right where multifamily is allowed (AB 2162); farmworker housing on agricultural land qualifies under AB 1783 and the County's Agricultural Worker Housing regulations (Somis Ranch farmworker complex, AMCAL). The County applied for the state Prohousing Designation in 2026 (public comment through May 29, 2026; Board resolution April 28, 2026), which adds points in state funding programs, and is completing a countywide Farmworker Housing Study and Action Plan (approved September 22, 2026).
- AMI requirement: SB 35: 10% or 50% affordable depending on RHNA shortfall category; AB 2162 supportive housing thresholds; AB 1783 farmworker housing requirements.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Submit the Streamlined Multifamily Zoning Clearance application with objective standards compliance; SB 330 preliminary applications lock in standards.)
- Layering notes: Ventura County's housing money runs through the County Executive Office, Community Development (CEO-CD, 800 S. Victoria Avenue, Ventura, (805) 654-2681), which administers the Urban County CDBG and HOME programs, the HOME Consortium, PLHA, General Fund housing NOFAs and Homekey sponsorships; the Resource Management Agency Planning Division handles density bonus, RHD zoning and streamlining; the Health Care Agency Behavioral Health holds BHSA; the Ventura County Continuum of Care (CA-611) sits in the CEO; and the Area Housing Authority of the County of Ventura (AHACV) supplies vouchers outside Oxnard, Ventura, Port Hueneme and Santa Paula, which have their own housing authorities. The nonprofit Housing Trust Fund Ventura County (HTFVC) is the de facto county revolving loan fund. The County has no inclusionary ordinance, linkage fee or dedicated housing tax. venturacounty.gov department pages block automated access, so records were built from vcportal.venturacounty.gov PDFs (Action Plan, PLHA plan, funding summaries), rma.venturacounty.gov, housingtrustfundvc.org, ahacv.org, HCD tables and press coverage. No County fee waiver or deferral for affordable housing was found; the County's incentives are procedural (ministerial approval, no CEQA) and locational (RHD sites, farmworker housing on agricultural land). SB 35 projects of 10 or more units require prevailing wage. Source is the RMA Streamlined Affordable Housing Development page.

### Yolo County

**Behavioral Health Services Act (BHSA) housing interventions and capital set-aside, Yolo County HHSA Behavioral Health**
- Agency: Yolo County Health and Human Services Agency, Behavioral Health (Director Tony Kildare); Integrated Plan FY 2026-2029 approved by the Board of Supervisors June 23, 2026
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Projected BHSA funding $19.4 million in FY 2026-27 with Housing Interventions at 30% ($5,834,453); the Board's March 3, 2026 budget approach (Scenario 3) transfers 7% of housing interventions to Behavioral Health Services and Supports, leaving about $4.4 million for housing in FY 2026-27. The adopted plan commits to a procurement to allocate 25% of Housing Interventions funds (roughly $1.1 to $1.5 million a year) to capital development: property acquisition, new construction, and rehabilitation or renovation of existing units. Remaining funds pay rental subsidies after Transitional Rent ends, landlord damage mitigation, participant assistance and a new Housing Interventions Team. The Board also added 8 behavioral health case managers ($1 million a year) for permanent supportive housing sites.
- AMI requirement: Medi-Cal eligible adults with serious mental illness or substance use disorder who are chronically homeless, homeless or at risk; at least half of housing funds for chronically homeless people. BHSA housing interventions are available only after CalAIM Community Supports and the six-month Medi-Cal Transitional Rent benefit (provider Turning Point Community Programs) are exhausted.
- Cycle: rolling (next: The capital procurement (25% of housing interventions) has not been released as of November 6, 2026; the plan says it will be conducted during FY 2026-27. Behavioral Health Bridge Housing funding expires June 30, 2027, after which BHSA absorbs those costs. Annual plan updates begin FY 2027-28 (draft due to DHCS March 31).)
- Layering notes: Yolo is one of the few small counties committing the full 25% capital share, but at about $1.1 to $1.5 million a year it buys a handful of units: realistic uses are acquisition of small existing properties, a capital contribution to a PSH project with a BHSA referral agreement, or rehab of existing supportive housing (the Board asked staff whether capital can go to existing facility needs). Pair with YCH PBVs and the County's PSH case management staffing (8 FTE) to make a small PSH deal pencil. BHSA money is state funding and triggers prevailing wage. Behavioral Health is also the County's lead on Homekey+ interest and on the Encampment Resolution Fund application.
- Source: https://public.destinyhosted.com/yolocdocs/2026/BOS/20260623_3937/16572_Final_Yolo_County_BHSA_Integrated_Plan_FY_2026-2029.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Davis, Woodland/Yolo County Continuum of Care (CA-521, Homeless and Poverty Action Coalition) and HHAP, Yolo County share**
- Agency: Yolo County Health and Human Services Agency (County HHAP administrative entity and HPAC staff; HMIS lead) with the Homeless and Poverty Action Coalition (CoC board) and the City of Davis
- Type: grant
- Typical award: HHAP Round 6 allocations (HCD, based on the 2024 count): Yolo County $1,074,906.92 and the Davis, Woodland/Yolo County CoC $1,148,019.07, about $2.2 million for the region including Davis, awarded in the state's April 2026 batch. HHAP rounds 1 to 5 brought $8.1 million to the Yolo area, $3.9 million of it to the County ($2.6 million spent). Round 5 County subawards approved March 3, 2026 include $135,000 to the City of West Sacramento for interim housing operating subsidies and $15,000 for rapid rehousing, both through February 2028. 2024 point-in-time count: 942 people, 633 unsheltered, 983 year-round beds.
- AMI requirement: People experiencing or at risk of homelessness; HHAP-6 requires a regionally coordinated action plan, a 10% youth set-aside and allows permanent housing capital, operating subsidies and rental assistance among its categories.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: County and CoC HHAP-6 funds are being contracted in 2026 through 2029; the County's BHSA plan says HHAP is used for existing programs (Housing Our Youth type youth transitional housing, rapid rehousing, interim housing operations). HHAP Round 7 preliminary allocations were posted by HCD with final figures expected after September 2026. HPAC posts its own RFPs for CoC and HHAP funds on yolocounty.gov (page blocked to automated access on November 6, 2026).)
- Layering notes: Yolo's HHAP is about $1 million a year each to the County and the CoC and goes to operations, not capital. For a PSH project the value is Coordinated Entry referrals through HPAC, possible HHAP or CoC operating subsidies for a handful of units, and the County's Behavioral Health Bridge Housing and Transitional Rent pipeline for tenants. The City of Davis receives its own HHAP (about $1 million in Round 6) and the City of West Sacramento has run interim housing with County HHAP pass-throughs; both are Pass 3.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/sites/default/files/docs/grants-and-funding/calich/hhap-6-allocations.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Development fee incentives for affordable units: 50% application and processing fee waiver (Sec. 8-8.118) and Housing Density Bonus (Chapter 8-12), unincorporated Yolo County**
- Agency: Yolo County Community Services Department, Planning Division; fee waivers subject to Board of Supervisors approval
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Section 8-8.118(a) allows a fifty percent waiver of development-related application and processing fees for affordable units, subject to Board approval, plus modified standards for road widths, parking, lot coverage and lot size, priority processing and a density bonus. Chapter 8-12 (Ordinance 1445, August 14, 2014) codifies state density bonus law as of 2014: 20% base bonus for 10% low or 5% very low income units, rising to a 35% maximum, one to three concessions, parking caps of 1 space (studio and 1 bedroom), 2 spaces (2 to 3 bedrooms) and 2.5 spaces (4 or more bedrooms). No impact fee waiver program exists; County development impact fees are not waived for affordable housing.
- AMI requirement: Fee waiver applies to the affordable units in an inclusionary or 100% affordable project. Density bonus tiers per Government Code 65915 (the County code's 35% cap is superseded by current state law allowing 50% and unlimited density for 100% affordable projects).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Request with the planning application; the fee waiver needs a Board action. Chapter 8-12 has not been updated since 2014 and state law controls where more generous.)
- Layering notes: The waiver covers planning application and processing fees only, not the County's development impact fees, school fees or the cities' fees, so the dollar value is modest (tens of thousands on a typical project). Waived fees are public funds under Labor Code 1720(b)(4), which is why the prevailing wage flag is unclear; most projects using the waiver also carry state funds that trigger it anyway. Use state density bonus law directly rather than the 2014 County chapter, and expect staff to process it under Government Code 65915 as amended.
- Source: https://gocodebook.com/library/us/ca/yolo-county/title-8-land-development-and-zoning/chapter-12-housing-density-bonuses
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (County Code Chapter 8-8), in-lieu fee and the Affordable Housing Program account, unincorporated Yolo County**
- Agency: Yolo County Community Services Department, Planning Division (ordinance and affordable housing agreements); in-lieu fees held by the County in the Affordable Housing Program account
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Regulatory requirement, not a funding source. Ordinance 1451 (effective January 1, 2015) requires on-site affordable units: for-sale projects of 10 or more units set aside 20% (half low income, half moderate income); rental projects of 20 or more units set aside 25% very low income plus 10% low income; rental projects of 7 to 19 units set aside 15% very low plus 10% low. Smaller projects (9 or fewer single-family, 7 or fewer multifamily) pay an in-lieu fee assessed on a unit percentage basis. The in-lieu fee equals a percentage of the estimated cost to construct the required units, is set in the County Master Fee Schedule (last adopted June 24, 2025) and may be reduced up to 50% based on available subsidy; the current per-unit amount was not published in the opened documents (the 2009 schedule was $12,920 for-sale and $17,610 rental per required unit).
- AMI requirement: Rental inclusionary units must be permanently affordable (Sec. 8-8.108(b)); for-sale units carry a 20-year covenant that resets at each resale to a qualified buyer. Alternatives, only if on-site is shown infeasible: off-site units in the unincorporated county, land dedication (2 acres minimum), in-lieu fee, or housing credits transferable within 3 miles and 5 years. Density bonus units do not generate an inclusionary obligation.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at discretionary approval; fee estimate given at approval and paid before final map, first building permit or certificate of occupancy. In-lieu fees must be spent or encumbered within five years of receipt unless the Board extends.)
- Layering notes: The Affordable Housing Program account (Sec. 8-8.116) is the County's only local housing fund and it is fed solely by in-lieu fees from small unincorporated projects, so expect a balance in the low hundreds of thousands at most; no NOFA or award from it was found. For a 100% affordable project the ordinance imposes no obligation and offers the incentives in Sec. 8-8.118 (fee waiver, standards modifications, streamlining, density bonus). For a mixed-income rental project of 20 or more units the 35% combined set-aside (25% very low plus 10% low) is one of the steepest in the region and is a real feasibility constraint in Esparto, Madison and Dunnigan; the ordinance predates current state density bonus law and the Latinos Unidos v. Napa decision, so confirm how the County applies it today. Davis, Woodland and West Sacramento have their own inclusionary programs (Davis in-lieu fee about $215,000 per unit) covered in Pass 3.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/yolo-county-unincorporated-CA/chapter_8
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA), Yolo County formula allocation**
- Agency: Yolo County (Board of Supervisors resolutions; County Administrator's Office and Health and Human Services Agency)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: 2019-2023 five-year formula allocation $819,661 (Board resolution adopted February 27, 2024 with the PLHA plan). HCD's 2024-2028 formula NOFA lists Yolo County at $270,934 (2024), $303,869 (2025) and an estimated $1,693,338 over five years. For comparison the cities receive their own allocations: Davis about $1.33 million, Woodland about $1.09 million and West Sacramento about $1.04 million over five years.
- AMI requirement: PLHA guidelines: rental housing at or below 60% AMI (up to 80% AMI) with 55-year covenants, or homeownership and homelessness activities as chosen in the County's five-year plan. The County's adopted plan activities were not readable in the opened documents.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: The County must adopt a new five-year plan and resolution for the 2024-2028 allocations under HCD's amended formula NOFA; no 2026 Board action was found in the minutes reviewed. Ask the County Administrator's Office which activities the plan funds before counting on PLHA.)
- Layering notes: At $270,000 to $300,000 a year, Yolo County PLHA is a small, deal-specific source and the County's plan uses have not been published in a readable form online. It could support a single predevelopment or gap contribution for a project in the unincorporated area, or be subgranted to Yolo County Housing. State funds trigger prevailing wage.
- Source: https://public.destinyhosted.com/yolocdocs/2024/BOS/20240227_3705/14411_14411_Resolution_-_1.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Project-Based Vouchers and HUD-VASH PBVs, Yolo County Housing (YCH)**
- Agency: Yolo County Housing (Housing Authority of the County of Yolo, CA044), 147 West Main Street, Woodland; (530) 662-5428; Executive Director Ian Evans; Housing Program Manager Anisa Vallejo
- Type: other
- Typical award: YCH leases about 1,500 to 1,600 Housing Choice Vouchers countywide and owns 431 public housing units. It awarded 155 PBVs over the three years to January 2026 (84 to new construction) through RFPs, most recently a December 2023 pair of RFPs (existing housing, and new construction or rehabilitation) offering up to 40 PBVs with up to 3 HUD-VASH vouchers. The FY 2026 Agency Plan commits to allocating additional PBVs to new and existing projects until YCH reaches HUD's PBV cap (about 30% of vouchers) by the end of 2029, and to project-basing about 36 tenant protection vouchers from a 2026 Section 18 disposition at its Tupelo development in Woodland.
- AMI requirement: Very low and extremely low income households (at or below 50% AMI); PBV HAP contracts up to 20 years with extensions. The PBV waiting list has been closed since February 2023 and the HCV program has had intermittent voucher shortfalls since 2023 while Emergency Housing Voucher households are moved onto HCV.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: No PBV RFP was open on November 6, 2026 (YCH's current solicitations are landscaping and painting). YCH issues PBV RFPs as voucher budget allows; contact Anisa Vallejo (avallejo@ych.ca.gov) to be notified. YCH's jurisdiction is the whole county, including Davis, Woodland, West Sacramento and Winters.)
- Layering notes: PBVs are the one countywide operating subsidy for PSH in Yolo and YCH is unusually willing to project-base (goal of reaching the HUD cap by 2029), but federal HCV funding shortfalls since 2023 limit new awards; the County's BHSA plan calls voucher capacity 'extremely limited.' Nine or more PBV units in a project trigger Davis-Bacon under HUD rules; PBVs alone do not trigger state prevailing wage. Coordinated Entry through the Homeless and Poverty Action Coalition (HPAC, CoC CA-521) supplies referrals for homeless set-aside units. YCH is also converting or disposing of public housing (Vista Montecito, Yolito, Ridgecut in 2026; Yolano Village and Donnelly Circle in 2027 or later), which will generate more PBVs tied to its own redevelopment.
- Source: https://www.ych.ca.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/CA044-FY2026-Annual-Plan-for-Posting-1.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**State CDBG and HOME (non-entitlement county) and County housing programs, Yolo County Administrator's Office**
- Agency: Yolo County Administrator's Office (Mark Bryan, housing programs; housingprograms@yolocounty.gov) with grant administration contracted to Thurmond Consulting LLC through November 30, 2026; Yolo County Housing formerly administered the County CDBG program
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Small. Yolo County is a non-entitlement jurisdiction and applies to HCD's state CDBG and HOME competitions. The Board held the required housing public hearing on March 24, 2026 and authorized a $300,000 application for the 2025 state CDBG funding year (Resolution 26-34). Current CDBG use is an owner-occupied housing rehabilitation program with loans up to $100,000 for low and moderate income homeowners in the unincorporated area. Earlier HOME and CDBG awards funded first-time homebuyer assistance. No County loan to a multifamily development was found.
- AMI requirement: CDBG at or below 80% AMI (low and moderate income benefit) in the unincorporated area only (not Davis, Woodland, West Sacramento or Winters). HOME at or below 80% AMI with HCD's affordability covenants.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Follows HCD's state CDBG and HOME NOFA calendar; the County held its 2026 CDBG public meeting on March 18, 2026 and hearing on March 24, 2026. A developer wanting the County as applicant or co-applicant for a state rental program should approach the County Administrator's Office before the HCD NOFA opens.)
- Layering notes: Yolo County has no county gap loan program for rental housing and no development NOFA. Its state CDBG and HOME capacity is small and committed to homeowner rehab and homebuyer programs, and the County issued an RFP in September 2026 for housing program consulting (loan management, grant writing) to run these programs. The practical County role for a developer is the resolution or co-applicant for state programs (HOME rental, Joe Serna farmworker housing, MHP) in the unincorporated communities (Esparto, Madison, Knights Landing, Dunnigan, Clarksburg). The Board created a Farmworker Housing Ad Hoc Subcommittee in May 2025 and the March 2026 Board calendar listed hearings on a farmworker housing planning grant application. The three cities (Davis, Woodland, West Sacramento) run their own programs and are Pass 3. State funds trigger prevailing wage and federal cross-cutting rules.
- Source: https://public.destinyhosted.com/yolocdocs/2026/BOS/20260324_3929/16187_Template_CDBG_meeting_presentation___SPANISH_DRAFT.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Tax-exempt multifamily bonds: no County or YCH conduit program; CMFA and CSCDA issuances with local TEFRA approval**
- Agency: Issuers California Municipal Finance Authority and California Statewide Communities Development Authority; TEFRA approval by the host city council or, in the unincorporated area, the Yolo County Board of Supervisors
- Type: bond
- Typical award: Project-sized conduit issuances. Example: CMFA issued $6,668,698 of tax-exempt bonds for Esperanza Crossing Apartments (Mercy Housing, 40 units on Woodland Avenue, 39 units at or below 55% AMI). No County of Yolo or Yolo County Housing bond issuance and no County TEFRA resolution appeared in the 2026 Board minutes reviewed (January 27, March 3 and March 24, 2026).
- AMI requirement: Federal bond and 4% LIHTC rules: at least 20% of units at 50% AMI or 40% at 60% AMI (or income averaging), 55-year CDLAC regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Request the TEFRA hearing through the issuer; allow six to eight weeks before the CDLAC application. For unincorporated sites the hearing goes on a Board of Supervisors consent agenda; for Davis, Woodland and West Sacramento sites the city council holds it (Pass 3).)
- Layering notes: Neither the County nor YCH runs a bond program, so budget issuer fees for CMFA or CSCDA. Bonds alone do not trigger state prevailing wage, but tax credits plus state or County funds in the same deal usually do. The County's TEFRA practice in the unincorporated area could not be confirmed from opened documents because no recent unincorporated bond-financed project was found.
- Source: https://www.cmfa-ca.com/?p=2377
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-06)

**Welfare Exemption (property tax), Yolo County Assessor**
- Agency: Yolo County Assessor/Clerk-Recorder/Elections (ACE), Jesse Salinas, 625 Court Street, Woodland, CA 95695; (530) 666-8135; ace.yolocounty.gov; with the State Board of Equalization (Organizational Clearance Certificate)
- Type: tax abatement
- Typical award: Exemption of property taxes on units restricted to lower income households under Revenue and Taxation Code 214(g): full exemption for qualifying units in LIHTC or publicly financed projects and partial exemption for the qualifying share in mixed projects. In Yolo County the 1% base rate plus voter-approved debt applies to the exempt value.
- AMI requirement: Nonprofit owner or limited partnership with a nonprofit managing general partner holding a BOE Organizational Clearance Certificate; recorded regulatory agreement (tax credits, bonds, HOME, PLHA, housing authority or County agreement) restricting rents to lower income households; BOE-267-L2 tenant data where there is no government financing or tax credits.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: File BOE-267 (first year) or BOE-267-A (annual) with the Assessor by February 15 each year with BOE-267-L, L1, L2 and L-A supplements as applicable; late filings through December 31 receive a partial exemption. The Yolo ACE website does not post exemption instructions; call the Assessor's office.)
- Layering notes: Standard underwriting item. Yolo County Housing is actively using the exemption as a financing tool: its FY 2026 plan commits to working with private investors to build affordable workforce housing 'utilizing the benefits of the welfare exemption,' and the December 2025 KIND West Sacramento deal deed-restricted 30 units for moderate income households with a YCH operating loan structured to meet exemption requirements. Check carefully that moderate income (above 80% AMI) units qualify only within the AB 84 over-income allowance; the core exemption is for lower income units.
- Source: https://www.boe.ca.gov/proptaxes/countycontacts.htm
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)

**Yolo County Housing and New Hope CDC development partnerships, surplus land and farmworker housing sites**
- Agency: Yolo County Housing (YCH) with its nonprofit affiliate New Hope Community Development Corporation; Executive Director Ian Evans, ievans@ych.ca.gov, (530) 669-2219
- Type: other
- Typical award: YCH acts as co-developer, land owner and partner rather than lender. Current pipeline: Tupelo, 73 units in Woodland (one to three bedrooms, 30% to 60% AMI) with a $27,999,100 AHSC award announced December 10, 2025 (City of Woodland $1.5 million loan commitment, Sutter Health $1.5 million, Brinshore Development and Operative Office as partners, construction as early as 2027, about 36 project-based TPVs); 641 5th Street, West Sacramento, 37 units (New Hope CDC, groundbreaking planned 2026, completion target 2027); Yolano and Donnelly redevelopment in Woodland (HUD Choice Neighborhoods Planning Grant, September 2024, over 240 mixed-income units planned, three phases by 2029); El Rio Villa Phase I by 2029. YCH posted a Surplus Land Act Notice of Availability for its Dunnigan property on September 10, 2026 (60-day response window, priority to proposals with at least 25% affordable units) and is seeking developers for its vacant Esparto lots for farmworker homeownership by 2027. In December 2025 YCH partnered with Fulcrum Property and Pacific Housing to deed-restrict 30 existing West Sacramento units for moderate income households for 35 years using the welfare exemption.
- AMI requirement: Set deal by deal: 30% to 60% AMI at Tupelo (AHSC and tax credit rules); moderate income (80% to 120% AMI) in the acquisition and deed-restriction model; farmworker households at the Esparto lots. YCH's strategic plan targets a 15% increase (about 390 units) in affordable units it owns, manages or supports by 2029.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Dunnigan surplus land letters of interest were due about November 9, 2026 (60 days from September 10, 2026), followed by at least 90 days of negotiation. Esparto lot partnerships and future Section 18 redevelopment RFPs (Yolano and Donnelly, 2027 or later) are the next openings; YCH's board meets monthly (October 14 and December 9, 2026 remaining).)
- Layering notes: In Yolo the housing authority, not the County, is the development partner that brings land, PBVs, migrant center operating experience and public housing repositioning capital. A developer can join as YCH's co-developer (Brinshore model at Tupelo), buy or ground lease YCH surplus land under the Surplus Land Act, or use YCH or New Hope CDC as the nonprofit managing general partner to qualify existing buildings for the welfare exemption (the KIND West Sacramento structure, where YCH provided a $25,000 operating loan and the deed restriction). AHSC, tax credits and HUD funds in these deals trigger prevailing wage and Davis-Bacon. YCH also operates the Davis, Madison and Dixon migrant centers for the state Office of Migrant Services, so it is the natural partner for Joe Serna farmworker housing applications in the unincorporated area.
- Source: https://www.ych.ca.gov/yolo-county-housing-celebrates-75-years/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-06)


## City-Level Programs


### City of Burlingame

**Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (rolling, FY 2025-26 and FY 2026-27)**
- Agency: City of Burlingame Community Development Department, Economic Development and Housing
- Type: loan
- Typical award: About $12 million available (announced July 2025): $9 million for preservation of existing or naturally occurring affordable housing and $3 million for production; news reports put the fund at $14.3 million. Prior support: a $1.4 million 55-year loan considered for the 69-unit Eucalyptus Grove (1875 California Drive, CRP and Abode, 20% to 50% AMI, County AHF $5,698,107, 30 HACSM PBVs) and donated land for the 132-unit Village at Burlingame (150 Park Road, completed 2023).
- AMI requirement: Up to 80% AMI, 55-year regulatory agreement, County rent limits with utility allowances, rent increases capped at the lesser of County limits or 5%; mixed-income projects may have up to 49% restricted units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No deadline; applications reviewed first come first served (scored against each other if several arrive within 60 days) and taken to Council. Confirm remaining balance with Joseph Sanfilippo, jsanfilippo@burlingame.org, (650) 558-7264.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. Terms are standard county soft debt: 3% simple interest residual receipts, 55 years, DCR 1.15 (1.10 for the first two years of acquisitions), developer fee 5% to 10%, $125 per unit monitoring fee, asset management fee capped at $1,000 per unit or $25,000, 10% hard and 5% soft contingencies, City subordinates its deed of trust but not its regulatory agreement; matching funds score up to 15 points (300% match). The preservation emphasis follows the HOPE advisory committee's priorities. Source is the 2025 NOFA PDF; fund page at burlingame.org/1274.
- Source: https://burlingame.org/DocumentCenter/View/7046/City-of-Burlingame-2025-Affordable-Housing-Fund-NOFA?bidId=
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Residential Impact Fee and Commercial Linkage Fee ordinances with on-site affordable alternative (BMC 25.44 and 25.45)**
- Agency: City of Burlingame Community Development Department, Planning Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Fee-based program adopted after a legal challenge to the former inclusionary ordinance (Ordinance 2000 of 2021, amended by Ordinance 2046 on March 2, 2026). Residential impact fee on residential floor area for rental projects over 10 units and for-sale projects over 6 units, with rates in the Master Fee Schedule (not opened this pass); on-site alternative deems impacts mitigated with 10% low income plus 5% very low income rental units in perpetuity or 15% low income for-sale units for 55 years. Commercial linkage fee on hotel, retail, restaurant, service and office projects adding 5,000 square feet or more, with rates in the Master Fee Schedule; the first four years produced more than $3.8 million. Both feed the Affordable Housing Fund.
- AMI requirement: Affordable units built concurrently, same size as comparable market units, finishes at least 60% of market value; density bonus projects pay the fee on market-rate units only, not on bonus affordable units; Council may waive fees for public or nonprofit public benefit uses.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Fees due before the first building permit at the schedule then in effect; Council signaled further 2026 updates to add incentives for certain development types.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. Because Burlingame has no inclusionary mandate above the fee, 100% affordable projects rely on the Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (see separate record), County AHF and HACSM PBVs; the fee program is why the fund reached $12 million to $14 million by 2025. Source is the published code chapter on gocodebook.com (Burlingame's code host); residential chapter at .../chapter-25.45-residential-impact-fees. Current per-square-foot rates must be confirmed in the Master Fee Schedule with Planning at (650) 558-7250.
- Source: https://gocodebook.com/library/us/ca/burlingame-zoning/title-25/chapter-25.44-commercial-linkage-fees
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of Citrus Heights

**Affordable housing incentives and density bonus (Citrus Heights Zoning Code Chapter 106.32)**
- Agency: City of Citrus Heights Planning Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus law implemented in Chapter 106.32 (Ord. 2019-004, 2022-007): eligibility at 10% lower income, 5% very low income, senior housing, 10% moderate for-sale, 10% foster youth/disabled veteran/homeless, 20% low-income student housing, or land donation of at least one acre; bonus table follows Government Code 65915; minimum project size five units; bonus and incentives for projects with child care facilities; density bonus agreement recorded.
- AMI requirement: Per state law; moderate income bonuses only for for-sale units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling at entitlement; Council determines the bonus.)
- Layering notes: No local enhancement beyond state minimums identified. Citrus Heights is built out, so bonus use is on infill and commercial corridor sites (Sunrise Boulevard, Auburn Boulevard) and the Sunrise Tomorrow Specific Plan area at Sunrise Mall, where a 2026 public-private agreement started redevelopment; housing phases there are a future opportunity.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/citrus-heights-CA/chapter_10
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Citrus Heights Affordable Housing Fee on commercial development (Resolution 2022-048) and City Housing Trust Fund**
- Agency: City of Citrus Heights Housing and Grants Division (fund); Building Division (fee collection)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Fee source, not a subsidy program with published terms. Per the July 9, 2026 development impact fee sheet: office (including medical and assisted living) $1.95, hotel $2.35, commercial and retail $1.77, industrial (flex and R&D) $1.67 per square foot on new commercial construction of 5,000 square feet or more or added commercial square footage; residential is exempt. Rates were raised in June 2022 to the regional average after no update since 1992. Revenue goes to the City Housing Trust Fund administered by the Housing and Grants Division; fund balance and any lending terms not published.
- AMI requirement: Unknown; linkage fee funds are for very low income housing per the original 1992 ordinance title (Nonresidential Construction Fees for Funding Very Low Income Housing).
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA found; projects should approach the Housing and Grants Division directly, (916) 727-4830, housing@citrusheights.net.)
- Layering notes: Citrus Heights has no inclusionary ordinance and no residential affordable housing fee, and no city fee waiver program for affordable units was found; the fee sheet also lists multimodal ($1,591 per multifamily unit), park, Measure A transportation, drainage, sewer, school and fire fees that apply to apartments. The trust fund is small because the city is built out with little new commercial construction; the realistic city layer is the CDBG, HOME Consortium and PLHA record below plus SHRA bonds under a cooperation agreement.
- Source: https://www.citrusheights.net/DocumentCenter/View/21440
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Citrus Heights CDBG entitlement, Sacramento County HOME Consortium share, and Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA)**
- Agency: City of Citrus Heights Housing and Grants Division (CDBG and PLHA); SHRA administers the HOME Consortium
- Type: grant
- Typical award: CDBG: about $600,000 a year in entitlement funds plus about $50,000 program income (2024 Action Plan), used mostly for public improvements, public services (15% cap, competitive round each summer) and a housing rehabilitation revolving loan fund. HOME: the City is a member of the Sacramento County HOME Consortium with the County, City of Sacramento and Rancho Cordova; its consortium share funded $3,580,000 to the 47-unit Sunrise Pointe permanent supportive housing project (approved 2018, completed August 2023). PLHA: $1,876,554 five-year allocation from HCD, partly for the Sayonara Drive redevelopment project and a navigator program.
- AMI requirement: Per federal and PLHA rules; HOME at or below 60% AMI with 20% of HOME units at 50% AMI.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: CDBG public service applications are due in early August each year (2027 round announced); capital and housing allocations are negotiated through the Annual Action Plan (public hearing each October for the following calendar year). Multifamily HOME requests go through SHRA's two-round schedule with a Citrus Heights consortium set-aside, confirm with SHRA (916) 440-1393.)
- Layering notes: The HOME Consortium share is the only meaningful city-level capital source: Citrus Heights directed its share to Sunrise Pointe via SHRA rather than running its own NOFA. CDBG is largely spent on infrastructure and services. Federal funds trigger Davis-Bacon (HOME at 12 or more units, CDBG at 8 or more) and NEPA. Sayonara Drive is a City-led acquisition and redevelopment site that may seek a development partner.
- Source: https://www.citrusheights.net/DocumentCenter/View/20610
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of Daly City

**Daly City Housing Development Finance Agency land offerings and housing successor fund (Carter/Martin site RFQ)**
- Agency: Daly City Housing Development Finance Agency (housing successor to the former Redevelopment Agency), Economic and Community Development
- Type: other
- Typical award: Request for Developer Qualifications issued August 14, 2026 for an approximately 12.5-acre Agency-owned site at Carter and Martin Streets in the Bayshore neighborhood next to the Cow Palace (APNs 005-050-240 and 005-380-020, zoned C-1, former RDA housing asset with Community Redevelopment Law affordability obligations), marketed under Surplus Land Act exemptions for 100 or more units with at least 25% lower income or 100% affordable mixed use. Housing Asset Fund (Fund 11) balance $4,461,128 at June 30, 2025 with net deposits of $298,048 in FY 2024-25.
- AMI requirement: At least 25% of units restricted to lower income households (or 100% affordable with 75% lower income) under the Surplus Land Act exemption; housing successor funds limited to 80% AMI and below with 30% minimum for extremely low income over each five-year period.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: RFQ responses were due October 16, 2026 at 5:00 pm; developer selection and exclusive negotiation follow. No other Agency sites are offered.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. Carter/Martin is the largest publicly owned housing site in northern San Mateo County and will need extensive site work (slopes, wetlands, soils); the Agency can contribute land value and successor funds but will look to the County AHF, HACSM PBVs and state programs for the capital stack. Source is the RFQ document on dalycity.org; successor fund figures from the FY 2024-25 Housing Successor Annual Report (dalycity.org/DocumentCenter/View/12949).
- Source: https://www.dalycity.org/DocumentCenter/View/13545
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (DCMC Chapter 17.47, Ordinance 1423) and Affordable Housing Trust Fund**
- Agency: City of Daly City Economic and Community Development, Housing and Community Development Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Residential developments of 5 or more units: for-sale projects provide 20% of units at moderate income; rental projects provide 10% at low income (rents at 30% of 70% AMI). On-site units required at 15 or more for-sale units and 20 or more rental units; smaller projects pay the Required In Lieu Fee and larger projects may pay an Alternative In Lieu Fee per the City's Affordable Housing Fee schedule with Council approval. Fees go to the Affordable Housing Trust Fund, which the City uses with state and HUD funds for 100% affordable projects (Daly City is a CDBG and HOME entitlement city).
- AMI requirement: 55-year restrictions; on-site units earn state density bonus, waivers and concessions under the Affordable Housing Plan; local preference ordinance (September 2026) gives Daly City residents and workers preference for 70% of affordable units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at entitlement; in-lieu fee amounts are set by resolution in the Housing Impact Fees and In-Lieu Fees sheet (dalycity.org/DocumentCenter/View/925, an image file that could not be read this pass). Daly City is a participant in the 2026 multi-city nexus study.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. Daly City's rental requirement (10% low income) is the lightest among the eight cities covered, and the City's own money for 100% affordable deals comes from the Trust Fund, its HOME and CDBG entitlements and the housing successor fund rather than a NOFA; the County's $14 million Midway Village loan and City loans reported at about $7.7 million for the 72-unit 493 Eastmoor project (CMFA bonds up to $25 million authorized February 9, 2026, per news summaries not opened) show the pattern. Source is the ordinance PDF on dalycity.org; the fee amounts are unconfirmed, hence needs_confirmation. Contact hcd@dalycity.org, (650) 991-8068.
- Source: https://www.dalycity.org/DocumentCenter/View/926/Inclusionary-Housing-Ordinance-PDF
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of Davis

**Affordable Housing Ordinance (Davis Municipal Code Article 18.05, Ordinance 2646) and Rental Housing Inclusionary Guidelines (Resolution 25-002, in-lieu fee)**
- Agency: City of Davis Social Services and Housing Department, Affordable Housing Manager Iris Grace and Director Dana Bailey; Community Development Department for entitlements
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Rental projects of 20 or more units must provide 15% affordable units, half low income and half very low income (ordinance adopted July 2023). Resolution 25-002 (adopted January 7, 2025, 4 to 1) set the in-lieu fee for non-discretionary rental projects at $250,000 per required affordable unit and $125,000 for a fractional unit when the whole units are built on site. Staff had proposed $608,000 per unit (cost of the most recent all-affordable project) and the Council rejected $369,000. The fee is indexed annually by the construction cost index with a one-time payment or a multi-year option at a 10-year net present value. Ownership projects have separate land dedication and in-lieu options approved case by case.
- AMI requirement: 15% of rental units (7.5% low income at 80% AMI and 7.5% very low income at 50% AMI) with long-term covenants; alternatives (in-lieu fee, land dedication, Project Individualized Plan) must deliver equivalent affordability value and discretionary projects need Council approval of the alternative.
- Cycle: rolling (next: None (ordinance). Council on December 16, 2025 directed staff to amend the land dedication option as soon as possible; an amendment requiring land dedicators to also provide funding was introduced in March 2026 per local coverage. A broader revision of the ownership inclusionary rules is in progress. Verify current text with the Affordable Housing Manager, (530) 757-5602.)
- Layering notes: The ordinance is the engine behind Davis's two main local tools: in-lieu fees and negotiated contributions flow to the City Housing Trust Fund, and land dedication produces fully improved affordable sites (see the Housing Trust Fund and land dedication records). For a 100% affordable developer this means partnering with a market-rate applicant on a dedicated site, or seeking Housing Trust Fund gap money. Staff's October 21, 2025 informational report found production lagging RHNA and proposed a mandatory financial review when a developer claims infeasibility; Councilmembers asked staff to look at 10% extremely low income in place of part of the 15% requirement. Section 40.27.110 of the zoning code ties Article 18.05 to state density bonus law. Stack with county records: no Yolo County housing trust fund or county NOFA exists; Yolo County Housing project-based vouchers and the state CDBG and HOME competitions are the county-level layers. Access: cityofdavis.org (Akamai) returns 403 to scripts and to WebFetch; the Council minutes on documents.cityofdavis.org open and were used. The Resolution 25-002 text (showpublisheddocument/20364) and the October 21, 2025 staff report could not be opened.
- Source: https://documents.cityofdavis.org/Media/CityCouncil/Documents/PDF/CityCouncil/CouncilMeetings/Minutes/2025/Minutes-2025-01-07-City-Council-Meeting.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Affordable housing land dedication sites under Article 18.05 (Village Farms 16-acre dedication, Project Individualized Plan Exhibit E)**
- Agency: City of Davis Community Development Department and Social Services and Housing Department (Council subcommittee negotiates development agreements)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Land, not cash. Village Farms Davis (Development Agreement Exhibit E, approved December 16, 2025 and revised January 13, 2026) dedicates 16 acres to the City for 280 low and very low income units on fully improved sites (no more than 4 parcels, none under 2.5 acres), to be developed with a qualified affordable housing developer selected by the City, with the City to endeavor to add 80 moderate income units. Baseline features now require construction to commence on 100 affordable units before Phase 3 and no permits beyond the first 150 until that starts; the City holds an option after 150 units. Together with the $6 million Housing Trust Fund payment the package is valued by the applicant at over $6 million plus land.
- AMI requirement: 280 low and very low income units (plus up to 80 moderate) on dedicated land; affordability covenants set in the City's developer agreement.
- Cycle: rolling (next: City selection of the affordable developer for the Village Farms sites has not been scheduled; it depends on voter approval of the project (June 2026 Measure J/R/D vote) and on the first land dedication. Watch Council agendas and the Social Services Commission.)
- Layering notes: This is how 100% affordable developers get sites in Davis: the inclusionary ordinance's land dedication option plus Housing Trust Fund money, then compete for 9% credits, MHP, AHSC or HCD Super NOFA money. Vice Mayor Vaitla's December 2025 direction to amend the land dedication option (so dedicators also provide resources) led to a March 2026 ordinance introduction, so later projects should carry cash with the land. Earlier dedications (Cannery, Chiles Ranch, Bretton Woods) produced sites now built or in pipeline. Davis has no city-owned surplus land program beyond these dedications. Pair with Yolo County Housing PBVs and the CoC HHAP money for supportive units.
- Source: https://documents.cityofdavis.org/Media/CityCouncil/Documents/PDF/CityCouncil/CouncilMeetings/Minutes/2026/Minutes-2026-01-13-City-Council-Meeting.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**CDBG and HOME entitlement allocations (2025-2029 Consolidated Plan, Resolution 25-061) and PLHA formula allocation**
- Agency: City of Davis Social Services and Housing Department, Housing and Grants Management (Director Dana Bailey)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Davis is a CDBG entitlement city and a HOME participating jurisdiction. Council adopted the 2025-2029 Consolidated Plan and the Program Year One (2025) Annual Action Plan on April 15, 2025 (Resolution 25-061). Local coverage puts year one CDBG plus HOME at about $1.06 million; HOME has historically funded first-time homebuyer loans and rental project gap loans, CDBG funds public services, facilities and the Pathways to Employment and homelessness programs. HCD's 2024-2028 PLHA NOFA lists Davis at about $1.33 million over five years; staff told Council on February 18, 2025 that PLHA would be used for homeownership opportunities.
- AMI requirement: CDBG at or below 80% AMI; HOME at or below 80% AMI with at least 90% of rental assistance at 60% AMI and 20% of units at 50% AMI in projects with 5 or more HOME units.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Program Year 2026-27 Action Plan process runs winter to spring 2027 (public hearing typically April). Confirm amounts and the HOME set-aside with the Grants Management office, (530) 757-5602.)
- Layering notes: Federal money is the only recurring Davis source for rental projects, but the annual HOME amount is small (hundreds of thousands) and competes with homebuyer uses; combine with Housing Trust Fund gap money and state credits. HOME triggers federal Davis-Bacon only at 12 or more HOME-assisted units; state prevailing wage applies when City funds are added. Davis also receives HHAP through the Davis, Woodland/Yolo County CoC (see county record) for supportive housing operations. Yolo County's own CDBG and HOME (state non-entitlement) cover only unincorporated areas, so there is no overlap. Blocked: the Grants Management page and the Consolidated Plan PDF on cityofdavis.org (403); the April 15, 2025 minutes on documents.cityofdavis.org were opened and record the adoption.
- Source: https://documents.cityofdavis.org/Media/CityCouncil/Documents/PDF/CityCouncil/CouncilMeetings/Minutes/2025/Minutes-2025-04-15-City-Council-Meeting.pdf
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)

**City of Davis Housing Trust Fund (in-lieu fees and negotiated developer contributions, Article 18.05)**
- Agency: City of Davis Social Services and Housing Department (Housing Trust Fund administered by the Affordable Housing Manager; Social Services Commission reviews affordable housing plans)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Project-specific gap contributions sized by Council; no published NOFA or guidelines found. Revenue is in-lieu fees under Resolution 25-002 ($250,000 per unit) plus negotiated contributions from peripheral Measure J/R/D projects: Village Farms Exhibit E (approved December 16, 2025, revised January 13, 2026) commits $6,000,000 to the Housing Trust Fund ($2 million with dedication of the first land site, $1 million at the first building permit, $3 million before any Phase 3 building permit); Willowgrove's plan reportedly adds $1.8 million. Earlier years the fund was small (the 2023 single-family in-lieu fee was about $82,000 per unit) and the City has historically funded projects such as Paul's Place and Creekside from it. Current balance not published in any document opened this pass.
- AMI requirement: Low and very low income units with long-term covenants; Council direction prioritizes very low and extremely low income and deed-restricted ownership.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA. Contributions arrive with Village Farms and Willowgrove milestones only if voters approved the projects (Village Farms was placed on the June 2026 ballot and Willowgrove on the November 2026 ballot); verify election outcomes and timing with the Affordable Housing Manager, (530) 757-5602.)
- Layering notes: The only local soft money in Davis. Sized to projects on the dedicated land sites first (the Village Farms plan says the money should support affordable housing on the dedication sites but may be used elsewhere consistent with the plan). Layer as the last gap after 9% or 4% credits, HCD MHP or AHSC, Yolo County Housing project-based vouchers and the Davis CDBG and HOME allocations; City money is public funds and triggers prevailing wage. Councilmembers raised formalizing the fund into a structured trust (April 2026). Davis has no RDA successor housing fund. Blocked: cityofdavis.org pages and the Village Farms Exhibit E staff reports (403); facts are from the December 16, 2025 and January 13, 2026 Council minutes on documents.cityofdavis.org, which did open.
- Source: https://documents.cityofdavis.org/Media/CityCouncil/Documents/PDF/CityCouncil/CouncilMeetings/Minutes/2025/Minutes-2025-12-16-City-Council-Meeting.pdf
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)

**Density bonus (Davis Municipal Code Section 40.27.110, deviations under Article 18.05 and state density bonus law)**
- Agency: City of Davis Community Development Department, Planning Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Regulatory. Section 40.27.110 (Ordinance 2322, 2008) allows deviations from zoning standards in accordance with the density bonuses and incentives provided for affordable housing by Article 18.05 and state density bonus law (Government Code 65915). Davis has no local enhancement beyond the state schedule; Article 18.05 alternatives (in-lieu fee, land dedication) are the local layer.
- AMI requirement: State density bonus law thresholds (5% very low, 10% low, 100% affordable up to 80% bonus and unlimited near transit) or Article 18.05 inclusionary units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: None; applied with the entitlement application. Davis is updating its General Plan (2025 to 2027), which may fold density bonus text into a zoning rewrite.)
- Layering notes: Standard state law implementation; Davis downtown zones (Article 40.13) are form-based and the 2021-2029 Housing Element rezoned sites to meet RHNA, so the bonus matters most on R-3 and R-HD infill sites. Davis is not Prohousing designated. No fee waiver program for affordable units exists at the City level; development impact fees are set by Council annually. The code mirror (zoneomics) was used because qcode.us is blocked; confirm against the official code.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/davis-CA/chapter_34
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of East Palo Alto

**Affordable Housing Preservation Program (over-the-counter fund)**
- Agency: City of East Palo Alto Housing Division
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Council allocated just over $2 million to the program; applications accepted over the counter once the NOFA is released (anticipated March 2026 or later, launch planned for 2026). Draft guidelines (November 26, 2024) were developed with the County Equity Innovation Fund and HEART.
- AMI requirement: Conversion of market-rate housing to income-restricted housing or preservation and extension of existing restrictions; anti-displacement focus.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Over the counter after NOFA release; status of the 2026 release not confirmed. Contact housing@cityofepa.org, (650) 853-3189.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. Small but flexible; pairs with the County's preservation financing and the Bay Area Preservation Pilot. The City's broader funding plan (Affordable Housing Strategy 2024-2028, adopted February 6, 2024) commits 50% of Measure HH office parcel tax revenue ($2.50 per square foot on office over 25,000 square feet, 2018) to creation and preservation of affordable housing, cites capacity to fund up to 122 units in 100% affordable developments, and adds Measure JJ gross receipts tax revenue (about $1.45 million to $1.7 million a year, approved November 2024); the City has not posted a new-construction NOFA, so developers should approach the Housing Division directly. Source is the preservation program page.
- Source: https://www.cityofepa.org/housing/page/affordable-housing-preservation
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (Chapter 18.37) with in-lieu fees and Affordable Housing Commercial Linkage Fee**
- Agency: City of East Palo Alto Housing Division, 1960 Tate Street
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Projects of 5 or more units: rental 20% on-site (5% at 35% AMI, 10% at 50% AMI, 5% at 60% AMI) or, with Council approval, 25% off-site or an in-lieu fee of $299,200 per required unit (FY 2025-26); for-sale 20% on-site (10% median, 10% moderate) or 25% off-site or $268,800 per unit in lieu. Projects under 5 units pay a per-unit share ($59,840 to $239,360 rental). Commercial linkage fee (Resolution 118-2020, effective October 31, 2020, ENR-indexed each April 1) applies to office, R&D and medical office; retail, restaurant, service and hotel are charged $0; the City plans to update it in 2027 after the multi-city nexus study.
- AMI requirement: Ordinance 425 (November 19, 2019) and Guidelines (Resolution 142-2020); income limits follow HCD; SB 9 lot splits exempt (September 2025); in-lieu fees reset each July (FY 2025-26 adopted July 16, 2025).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at entitlement; fee schedule updated each July (comprehensive fee schedule effective July 18, 2026). East Palo Alto is a 2026 multi-city nexus study participant.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. East Palo Alto has the deepest rental targeting in the county (a 35% AMI tier) and uses the fees plus Measure HH and Measure JJ revenue to fund 100% affordable projects such as Colibri Commons and Light Tree; 148 inclusionary units were in the pipeline in 2024. Source is the City's inclusionary housing page; fee amounts from the in-lieu fee page and the linkage fee notice (updated October 16, 2026).
- Source: https://www.cityofepa.org/housing/page/inclusionary-housing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of Elk Grove

**City of Elk Grove Affordable Housing Fund and Affordable Housing Impact Fee (Municipal Code Chapters 16.88 and 16.89)**
- Agency: City of Elk Grove Housing and Public Services Division (fund and RFPs); Finance Department (fee collection)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Fee-funded local gap loan fund. Current (2025) fees per the 2026 EPS nexus study: $6,396 per single or two-unit dwelling and $3,837 per multi-unit dwelling; nonresidential $1.05 commercial/retail, $3.11 hotel, $1.19 manufacturing, $1.28 warehouse and $0.00 office per square foot, indexed annually. Staff recommended (Sept 23, 2026 hearing) converting to per square foot: $3.59 single-unit, $3.29 owner-occupied multi-unit, $1.46 renter-occupied multi-unit, plus commercial $2.15 shopping center, $2.29 office, $0.39 industrial, $1,146.75 per hotel room and other categories (maximums supportable were $47.80, $43.80 and $19.50 per square foot). The City releases an RFP when the fund balance can support a project (generally about $5 million); the City reports $84 million lent to sixteen projects since inception (developer page, not opened this pass).
- AMI requirement: Fund restricted to housing for low and very low income households (HCD limits); 2026 update would also allow a local preference for City-based workers where fair housing law permits. Density bonus affordable units are exempt from the residential fee (Zoning Code 23.50.080).
- Cycle: rolling (next: No open RFP found as of 2026-11-08; watch elkgrove.gov/affordable-housing/developers and the City's purchasing RFP page. Outcome of the September 23, 2026 fee update hearing (ordinance amending 16.88 and 16.89 and a fee resolution) was not confirmed; contact Joshua Tovar, Management Analyst II, jtovar@elkgrove.gov, or the Housing and Public Services Division, (916) 683-7111.)
- Layering notes: Elk Grove is an impact-fee jurisdiction (like the City and County of Sacramento) with no inclusionary option, so market-rate rental projects pay the fee rather than build units; the 2026 renter-occupied rate was deliberately set low ($1.46 per square foot recommended) because the nexus study found multifamily infeasible at higher fees. Elk Grove is outside the SHRA lending area (SHRA finances in other cities only under cooperation agreements), so this fund, state sources and Elk Grove's own PLHA are the local layer. Past RFPs tied fund loans to City-owned sites (Sheldon Farms North and Old Town, 2023). elkgrove.gov and codepublishing.com return 403 to automated access; facts here come from the City's Sept 23, 2026 public hearing notice and the June 2026 EPS nexus study PDFs on elkgrove.gov.
- Source: https://elkgrove.gov/sites/default/files/city-files/city-government/city-council/council-meetings/public-hearing-notices/09-23-26-affordable-housing-fee-2026-nexus-update-08-21-26-and-08-28-26.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Density bonus and developer incentives (Elk Grove Municipal Code Chapter 23.50) and streamlined housing approvals (Chapter 23.17)**
- Agency: City of Elk Grove Development Services, Planning Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus law implemented in Chapter 23.50 (eligibility at 10% lower income, 5% very low income, senior, 10% moderate for-sale, special needs, 100% affordable), one bonus with concessions per Government Code 65915; Section 23.50.080 (Ord. 12-2022) exempts affordable units built under the chapter from the Chapter 16.88 affordable housing fee. Chapter 23.17 provides ministerial streamlined approvals for infill affordable projects, affordable housing in commercial zones, mixed-income projects along commercial corridors, middle-class housing, adaptive reuse, faith and higher-education land, and urban subdivisions (titles confirmed; detailed standards not read this pass).
- AMI requirement: Per state law; fee exemption applies only to the deed-restricted affordable units.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling at entitlement.)
- Layering notes: No local enhancement beyond state minimums identified. The fee exemption in 23.50.080 is the useful local layer: a density bonus project avoids the per-unit affordable housing fee on its affordable units, and the 2026 fee update proposed keeping the renter multi-unit fee low. Elk Grove adopted its Chapter 23.17 streamlining menu in response to the 2021-2029 Housing Element.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/elk-grove-CA/chapter_4
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Elk Grove Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA) and CDBG entitlement funds for affordable housing**
- Agency: City of Elk Grove Housing and Public Services Division
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Not verified this pass. Elk Grove is believed to receive its own CDBG entitlement and a PLHA formula allocation (a December 2024 Council resolution referencing the 2024 PLHA NOFA appeared in search results) and is not in the Sacramento County HOME Consortium (members per the Citrus Heights 2024 Action Plan: County, City of Sacramento, Citrus Heights, Rancho Cordova). Amounts and multifamily eligibility unknown.
- AMI requirement: PLHA: per the City's HCD-approved five-year plan; CDBG: HUD rules.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: unknown, verify with agency)
- Layering notes: Recorded so the Elk Grove layer is complete; the City's housing pages (elkgrove.gov/affordable-housing) return 403 to automated access and the Council resolution PDFs moved off the old elkgrovecity.org paths. Confirm with Housing and Public Services, (916) 683-7111, whether PLHA is pooled with Affordable Housing Fund RFPs.
- Source: https://elkgrove.gov/affordable-housing/developers
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of Folsom

**Density bonus and other developer incentives (Folsom Municipal Code Chapter 17.102)**
- Agency: City of Folsom Community Development Department, Planning Services
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus law implemented in Chapter 17.102 (Ord. 1145, 2011) for very low, low, moderate and senior households, with additional incentives or concessions per Government Code 65915(i), an added bonus for child care facilities, and resale and occupancy restrictions on moderate income units. Multifamily objective design standards were adopted in late 2025 to enable ministerial review.
- AMI requirement: Per state law (affordable rent defined as 30% of 50% or 80% AMI).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling at entitlement.)
- Layering notes: No local enhancement beyond state minimums identified; the chapter predates recent state amendments and defers to Government Code 65915 where they conflict. The 2024 General Plan and Folsom Plan Area amendments added capacity for about 6,046 units in targeted multifamily areas, which is where bonus requests will land. Inclusionary projects may also request fee waivers or deferrals under 17.104.070 and Chapter 16.60.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/folsom-CA/chapter_53
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Folsom Affordable Housing Loan (AHL) Program (Folsom Housing Fund 238 and Housing Trust Fund 221)**
- Agency: City of Folsom Community Development Department, Housing Services (loan consultant TDA, Inc.)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Gap loans from the City's inclusionary in-lieu fee fund and commercial housing trust fund. Unaudited cash available for projects at June 30, 2025: $23.9 million (Fund 238 $22.8M, Fund 221 $0.9M, legacy funds $0.2M). Track record: 11 loans since 2005 totaling over $36 million supporting 1,095 units (e.g., a $2.7M conditional loan for 52 units in 2020). Guidelines adopted by Resolution 11427 on August 26, 2025 standardize terms and allow the City to issue NOFAs.
- AMI requirement: Minimum 55-year income, rent and affordability restrictions recorded senior to foreclosable liens; serves extremely low, very low and low income households; priority to sites outside concentrations of existing regulated housing and to Housing Element goals (seniors, large families, special needs). Fund 238 must spend at least 50% on construction or major rehab of multifamily rentals affordable to low and very low income households (Sacramento Housing Alliance settlement).
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA issued as of 2026-11-08; the 2025 guidelines contemplate strategic NOFAs when resources are available, and staff planned a Council policy discussion on priorities for the housing funds. Requests may still be submitted ahead of tax credit rounds; contact Housing Services via the Community Development Department (Pam Johns, Director), (916) 461-6000.)
- Layering notes: The largest city-controlled pool in Sacramento County outside the City of Sacramento (about $23M) with few competing applicants, because Folsom's rental projects are scarce and the Folsom Plan Area south of Highway 50 generates most in-lieu revenue. Loans are repaid from 50% of residual cash flow after allowed expenses; operating and replacement reserves required for the 55-year term; underwriting and compliance aligned to TCAC and LIHTC rules. Folsom is outside the SHRA lending area (cooperation agreement needed), so this fund plus state sources is the local layer; a Folsom Plan Area MOU committing to 890 deed-restricted affordable units (2024 General Plan amendments, not opened this pass) creates sites. Chapter 17.104.070 also allows fee waivers or deferrals and density bonuses for inclusionary units. folsom.ca.us returns 403 to automated access; the staff report and resolution were read from the City's public agenda host.
- Source: https://mccmeetingspublic.blob.core.usgovcloudapi.net/folsomca-meet-20667254009747e4aa5f68a4d63539d6/ITEM-Attachment-001-3c80e00338b5420aafa72ac45dae4a5d.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Folsom Housing Trust Fund commercial development fee (Municipal Code Chapter 3.90, Fund 221)**
- Agency: City of Folsom Community Development Department (fee) and Housing Services (fund)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Commercial linkage fee of $2.08 per square foot on new commercial development (established 2002), deposited in the Housing Trust Fund (Fund 221) and used mainly to fill financing gaps in affordable rental projects through the AHL Program. Fund 221 balance was $918,311 at June 30, 2025.
- AMI requirement: Fund supports affordable housing development; AHL Program 55-year restrictions apply to loans.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Deployed through the AHL Program (no NOFA as of 2026-11-08). Fee paid at building permit.)
- Layering notes: Minor source compared with Fund 238; Folsom's rate ($2.08) is below the City of Sacramento ($2.68 commercial) and above the County ($2.17). Chapter 3.90 text itself was not opened (codepublishing.com and folsom.ca.us return 403); the rate and balance come from the August 26, 2025 staff report on the City's public agenda host.
- Source: https://mccmeetingspublic.blob.core.usgovcloudapi.net/folsomca-meet-20667254009747e4aa5f68a4d63539d6/ITEM-Attachment-001-54dff11a603148f68a0203d4b6b13245.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Folsom Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (Municipal Code Chapter 17.104) and in-lieu fee (Ordinance 1359, Resolution 11510)**
- Agency: City of Folsom Community Development Department, Housing Services
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. For-sale residential projects of 10 or more units (and rental projects of 10 or more units receiving City assistance) must make 10% of units affordable: 3% very low income and 7% low income; market-rate rental apartments are exempt. In-lieu fee effective February 12, 2026 (replacing 1% of the lowest sales price): $3.00 per square foot for single-family for-sale units and $2.50 per square foot for multifamily for-sale units of 1,500 square feet or less, paid at building permit into the Folsom Housing Fund (Fund 238); at least 50% of fee revenue must go to lower-income rental development. Prior-year collections exceeded $3.8M, mostly from the Folsom Plan Area. Alternatives: land dedication, off-site units, acquisition-rehab or conversion of market-rate units, inclusionary credits, combinations.
- AMI requirement: Rental inclusionary units restricted at least 30 years (or longer if state law requires); for-sale units resale-restricted; projects of nine or fewer market-rate units exempt.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at entitlement; fee per unit in effect at permit issuance. Council adopted Ordinance 1359 on January 13, 2026 (first reading December 9, 2025) and set fees by Resolution 11510 the same night; next review expected with the Housing Element cycle. Developers paying fees only need a letter of intent, not a full inclusionary housing plan.)
- Layering notes: Nearly all Folsom Plan Area builders pay the fee, which is why Fund 238 holds about $22.8M and funds the AHL Program (record above). Rental multifamily was kept exempt in 2026 despite AB 1505 authority, so apartment developers owe no inclusionary units or fee. Developers who provide more than 10% can bank and sell inclusionary housing credits for five years (17.104.070). The ordinance text was read from the City Council's January 13, 2026 agenda packet on the City's public agenda host and the zoneomics code mirror.
- Source: https://mccmeetingspublic.blob.core.usgovcloudapi.net/folsomca-meet-e0a349248e64470eac3fc0fa94532290/ITEM-Attachment-001-881e809b1b1f4a71b18b405a5e2e979f.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Folsom legacy housing funds: Oaks at Willow Springs inclusionary fund (Fund 274) and former redevelopment bond proceeds (Fund 280)**
- Agency: City of Folsom Community Development Department, Housing Services
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Largely spent. Fund 274 held $25,672 at June 30, 2025 from a 2006 $3.1M inclusionary payment ($2.7M went to the Granite City Apartments site on Sibley Street). Fund 280 held $149,695 of pre-2011 redevelopment bond proceeds that SB 107 (2015) let the City use for housing; over $9.6 million was invested in multiple affordable projects after redevelopment dissolved in 2012.
- AMI requirement: Low and very low income housing per the original sources.
- Cycle: closed/inactive (next: No new rounds; residual balances may be folded into an AHL Program loan.)
- Layering notes: Recorded as negative information: Folsom's redevelopment successor housing money is essentially exhausted, so the City's capacity now rests on the in-lieu fee fund. Figures from the August 26, 2025 Housing Fund update staff report.
- Source: https://mccmeetingspublic.blob.core.usgovcloudapi.net/folsomca-meet-20667254009747e4aa5f68a4d63539d6/ITEM-Attachment-001-54dff11a603148f68a0203d4b6b13245.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of Menlo Park

**Below Market Rate (BMR) Housing Fund NOFA 2026/27**
- Agency: City of Menlo Park Community Development Department, Housing Division
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Approximately $4 million available for the production and preservation of permanent affordable housing, awarded competitively by the City Council; PlanetBids listing cites award sizes of about $300,000 to $800,000. Additional consideration for extremely low income units, special populations and preservation or rehabilitation of multifamily housing. Emergency shelters and transitional housing are not eligible. The prior round (January 2023) offered $1.5 million to $2 million; the City also contributed $1 million to a teacher housing project in November 2024.
- AMI requirement: Permanent affordability consistent with the BMR Housing Program Guidelines (October 21, 2025) and the Housing Element; priority to extremely low income and special needs units.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Applications due 5:00 pm Friday, November 20, 2026 on PlanetBids (invitation 2026-100926-101526; questions closed November 6, 2026); Housing Commission review winter 2027, Council award spring 2027. Housing Element Program H1.I requires a NOFA at least every two years.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. The BMR fund is capitalized by commercial in-lieu fees (office and R&D $23.26 per square foot in FY 2026-27) and residential in-lieu payments, so its size tracks Meta and life science approvals; $4 million is enough for one gap loan or a preservation deal and is typically paired with County AHF and HACSM PBVs. Source is the City's 2026 NOFA page; contact Arianna Milton, ANMilton@menlopark.gov, (650) 330-6705.
- Source: https://www.menlopark.gov/Government/Departments/Community-Development/Housing/NOFA
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Below Market Rate (BMR) Housing Program: residential inclusionary and commercial in-lieu fees (MPMC 16.96)**
- Agency: City of Menlo Park Community Development Department, Housing Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Residential: 10 to 19 units at least 10% BMR, 20 or more units at least 15% BMR (very low, low and moderate income); 5 to 9 units provide a unit if feasible or pay a graduated in-lieu fee of 1% to 3% of sales price; for-sale projects of 10 or more may pay 3% of the sales price per market-rate unit in lieu; rental in-lieu fee set by Council resolution based on the cost of a one-bedroom unit. Each on-site BMR unit earns one additional market-rate unit up to 15% above zoned density. Commercial: buildings of 10,000 square feet or more provide BMR units or pay in-lieu fees of $23.26 per square foot (office and R&D, Group A) or $12.62 (other commercial and industrial, Group B) for July 1, 2026 to June 30, 2027, adjusted each July 1 by a five-year CPI shelter average.
- AMI requirement: BMR rents at or below 30% of the income category limit and no more than 75% of market rents; income categories from 30% to 120% AMI; one-year live or work preference; BMR housing agreement recorded before permits.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at application; Menlo Park leads the 2026 San Mateo County multi-city nexus study (Strategic Economics) that may raise inclusionary percentages and add a residential impact fee later in 2026 or 2027.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. The commercial in-lieu fee is the engine of the BMR fund (see NOFA record); office projects in the Bayfront and downtown specific plan areas often negotiate on-site or off-site BMR units instead.
