Source is the City's residential BMR requirements page; commercial rates from the companion commercial page and the October 21, 2025 Guidelines (Resolution 7003).
- Source: https://www.menlopark.gov/Government/Departments/Community-Development/Housing/Developers/Residential-development-BMR-requirements
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of Placerville

**Affordable Housing Density Bonus, Placerville City Code Title 10 Chapter 12 (Sections 10-12-1 to 10-12-13; 2015 text, state-law update ZC 24-05 pending under ROI 2024-01)**
- Agency: City of Placerville Development Services Department, Planning Division, 3101 Center Street, (530) 642-5252, pv.planning@gmail.com
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. The local chapter still reflects the 2015 version of Government Code 65915: bonus up to 35% for 5% very low, 10% lower or 10% moderate (for-sale) units, 20% for senior housing, land donation bonus up to 35% combined; one to three incentives (reduced lot size, setbacks, lot coverage, height, parking, mixed-use zoning, other cost-reducing regulatory relief) and by-right parking ratios of 1 space (0 to 1 bedroom), 2 (2 to 3 bedrooms) and 2.5 (4 or more). Where the chapter conflicts with state law, state law controls (10-12-1), so the current 80% bonus for 100% affordable projects, unlimited bonus near major transit and up to five concessions apply by operation of state law. The 2025 Annual Progress Report (March 2026) reports the full density bonus ordinance update was deferred to 2026 because of evolving state law.
- AMI requirement: Per state law: income-restricted units for very low (50% AMI), lower (80% AMI) or moderate (120% AMI, for-sale) households with a recorded density bonus housing agreement (10-12-12); rental affordability term per Government Code 65915 (55 years).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Request with the entitlement application (optional pre-application conference). Watch for the Planning Commission and City Council hearings on text amendment ZC 24-05 (density bonus update), dates still TBD on the City's project list; adoption targeted in 2026.)
- Layering notes: No local enhancement beyond state law. Placerville has no local housing trust fund, no inclusionary ordinance or in-lieu fee, no commercial linkage fee, no city NOFA and no redevelopment successor housing funds; the City's affordable housing tools are this chapter, the Housing Opportunity Overlay by-right sites, fee deferral under SB 937, SCIP impact fee financing and sponsorship of state CDBG, HOME and PLHA applications (separate records). Stack with the El Dorado County density bonus and the County PLHA, Trust Fund, HOME/CDBG, PBV and welfare exemption records; the County Traffic Impact Fee offset (Policy B-14) does not apply inside the city, which levies its own traffic impact mitigation fee. Chapter text read from the City's own zoning ordinance PDF; the City page links the amlegal code library, which was not opened.
- Source: https://www.cityofplacerville.org/media/Planning%20Division/Zoning%20Ordinance/ZoningOrdinance.DSDMaster-%20Chapter%2012%20-%20Affordable%20Housing%20Density%20Bonus.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**City-sponsored state CDBG, Competitive PLHA and HOME loans for affordable multifamily (non-entitlement city; Housing Element Program C-4) and partnership with Housing El Dorado**
- Agency: City of Placerville Development Services Department (applicant and lender of record for state CDBG and PLHA competitive funds) with El Dorado County Housing Authority (HHSA) and Housing El Dorado
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Pass-through loans sized to each state award, no local cash. Mallard Apartments (Mallard Lane and MacIntosh, 72 units): City CDBG loan $756,000 for acquisition, Competitive PLHA (CPLHA) loan $756,000 for architecture and engineering, IIG $1,105,000 for frontage and offsite work, MHP $11,000,000. Middletown Apartments (Cold Springs Road and Middletown Road, 82 units): CDBG loan $861,000 for acquisition, CPLHA loan $861,000, IIG $2,158,000, MHP $13,000,000. The 2025 progress report adds a $400,000 PLHA loan secured for Mallard/Middletown. Program C-4 commits the City to meet annually with nonprofit developers, apply or co-apply for state and federal funds (CDBG, HOME, PLHA, Section 202 and 811, CalHome), and to establish priority processing and fee deferrals for extremely low income projects.
- AMI requirement: Follows the state source: Mallard and Middletown are 100% affordable at 30% to 60% AMI for 55 years; CDBG and PLHA require at least 55-year covenants for rental new construction.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Tied to HCD state CDBG and PLHA competitive NOFAs (typically fall or late winter per the Housing Element) and HUD rounds in late spring. Contact Planning at least one cycle ahead so the City can adopt the required resolutions; no city NOFA exists.)
- Layering notes: Placerville competes in the same non-entitlement state CDBG, HOME and competitive PLHA pools as El Dorado County, so coordinate with the County's Affordable Housing Unit (Housing@edcgov.us) to avoid both jurisdictions applying for the same project; the County's own records cover County HOME/CDBG participation requests, County PLHA formula funds and the County Trust Fund (no development NOFA). County PHA project-based vouchers and the welfare exemption apply inside the city. Gap order used on the two Placerville projects: 4% LIHTC and bonds (CMFA TEFRA via the County), MHP, IIG, City CDBG and CPLHA loans. The City has no local trust fund, inclusionary fee, linkage fee or RDA successor housing funds; a first-time homebuyer program and workforce housing study (Program C-8, CDBG planning grant by spring 2025) were not advanced as of the 2025 progress report. Source is the City's 2023 Housing Element Annual Progress Report tables (October 8, 2024 Council packet); the 2025 APR staff report of March 24, 2026 was also read.
- Source: https://www.cityofplacerville.org/media/City%20Council%20Staff%20Reports/2024/10%2008%202024/7.7_AttA%20APR%20Tables%20A,%20A2,%20B,%20D,%20LEAP,%20and%20Summary.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Development impact fee deferral for affordable projects (SB 937 implementation, Housing Element Program C-5), ADU impact fee reductions, and Statewide Community Infrastructure Program (SCIP) impact fee financing (Program C-7)**
- Agency: City of Placerville Development Services Department (Planning and Building Divisions) and Finance; SCIP bonds issued by California Statewide Communities Development Authority (CSCDA)
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Timing relief, not a reduction, for multifamily: the City reports it implemented fee deferrals under SB 937 in 2025 so impact fees on qualifying affordable projects are collected at certificate of occupancy rather than permit issuance. Housing Element Program C-5 also offers deferment to completion or occupancy, payment over 12 months or longer after completion, or fee reductions where the applicant documents lower facility demand. Impact fee reductions (water, sewer, traffic impact mitigation, park fees) have been implemented only for ADUs and JADUs consistent with state law. SCIP lets a developer finance City impact fees and public improvements through tax-exempt CSCDA bonds repaid by a special assessment on the parcel; the City intends to offer it for all projects, especially housing, but no development had used SCIP as of the 2023 and 2025 progress reports. No general fee waiver for affordable housing exists.
- AMI requirement: SB 937 deferral applies to projects meeting the statute's affordability definitions (generally 100% affordable or qualifying mixed-income projects); Program C-5 options are for affordable housing providers. SCIP has no affordability requirement.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Request deferral at building permit; SCIP participation requires a City resolution and CSCDA application ahead of permit issuance. Confirm the current fee schedule and deferral form with the Building Division.)
- Layering notes: Placerville levies its own traffic impact mitigation, park and utility fees, so the El Dorado County Traffic Impact Fee offset (Board Policy B-14) does not apply to city projects; deferral is the only local fee tool. SCIP assessment financing of impact fees is senior to other liens and must be cleared with TCAC and permanent lenders. Pair with the HO overlay by-right sites and City-sponsored CDBG, PLHA and HOME loans (separate records). Sources read: the March 24, 2026 City Council staff report accepting the 2025 Housing Element Annual Progress Report (SB 937 deferral statement), the City's Housing Resources page describing SCIP (updated March 4, 2025) and Programs C-5 and C-7 of the 2021-2029 Housing Element.
- Source: https://www.cityofplacerville.org/media/City%20Council%20Staff%20Reports/2026/03%2024%202026/12.3_SR%202025%20Housing%20Element%20APR%20(CK,DW-Fully%20Executed).pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Housing Opportunity Overlay Zone (HO, Title 10 Chapter 5 Section 24, Zone Change 2016-01) by-right affordable sites and Housing Element Programs A-3 and A-5**
- Agency: City of Placerville Development Services Department, Planning Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Regulatory. Sites in the HO overlay (and the R-5 Very High Density Multi-Family zone) allow owner-occupied or multifamily housing by right at a minimum 20 and maximum 24 units per acre with compliance with local design standards and no discretionary action (Government Code 65583.2 sites). The overlay covers about 17 acres with three additional sites proposed for 2026. Two of the four original HO parcels produced Mallard Apartments (72 units, 30% to 60% AMI, 55 years) and Middletown Apartments (82 units, 30% to 60% AMI, 55 years), both by Pacific Southwest Community Development Corporation and under construction in 2025 (82 Middletown permits issued in 2025). Objective design standards (LEAP $65,000 and SB 2 $160,000 funded) were still being completed as of March 2025.
- AMI requirement: By-right status is tied to lower income housing need under state law; projects on HO sites have been 100% affordable at 30% to 60% AMI with 55-year covenants. No local inclusionary percentage.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Ministerial site plan review on HO or R-5 sites at any time. Three additional HO sites were proposed for 2026 rezoning; confirm status and the objective design standards with Planning.)
- Layering notes: The HO overlay is where Placerville's deal flow has actually occurred; both recent projects paired by-right siting with City-sponsored CDBG and CPLHA loans, IIG and MHP (see the City state-funding record). The 2025 APR reports 244 lower income units of site capacity against a 90-unit lower income RHNA, so no-net-loss is not a constraint. Combine with the state density bonus (Chapter 12) and SB 35 or AB 2011 streamlining. Source is the City's 2021-2029 Housing Element policy document (Programs A-3 and A-5); the 2016 draft HO ordinance was also read on the City's EvoGov agenda host. No local trust fund or NOFA exists in Placerville.
- Source: https://www.cityofplacerville.org/media/Planning%20Division/Housing%20Element/2021-2029%20Housing%20Element%20-%20Amended/PolicyDocSect%20II%20-%202021%20HE%20(12-16-22,%20lmh).pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of Rancho Cordova

**Affordable Housing Plan requirement for new growth areas (Housing Element policy, south of Highway 50)**
- Agency: City of Rancho Cordova Planning Department and Housing Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Per the May 6, 2026 Keyser Marston survey prepared for Sacramento County, Rancho Cordova has no formal inclusionary ordinance but its Housing Element requires new residential projects in newly developing areas (generally south of Highway 50, mostly high-resource areas) to include an Affordable Housing Plan showing how the project contributes to affordable housing production; compliance options are on-site units, land dedication, rehabilitation of existing affordable units, or in-lieu fees negotiated through development agreements for master plans and specific plans.
- AMI requirement: Set project by project in development agreements; no fixed percentage.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at specific plan or development agreement approval.)
- Layering notes: For affordable developers the practical effect is land: master plan developers (Rio del Oro, Suncreek, Arboretum, Westborough, etc.) often satisfy the plan by dedicating sites or paying negotiated fees, which creates site opportunities and sometimes developer contributions for 9% and 4% deals. Source is a county consultant memo; the City's Housing Element text was not opened because the City site blocks automated access.
- Source: https://development.saccounty.gov/content/dam/cd/planning/docs/projects/long-range/SACOG-Region-Comparison-Memo_5-6-26.pdf
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)

**Housing incentives and density bonus (Rancho Cordova Municipal Code Chapter 23.710)**
- Agency: City of Rancho Cordova Planning Department
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus law implemented in Chapter 23.710 (Ord. 17-2021): one density bonus with concessions for 10% lower income, 5% very low income, special needs housing, senior housing, 10% moderate for-sale, 10% foster youth/disabled veteran/homeless units (55-year restriction), or 100% affordable projects; parking capped at 1.0 space for studios and one-bedrooms, 1.5 for two and three bedrooms, 2.5 for four or more; waivers of standards that would preclude the bonus.
- AMI requirement: Per state law.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling at entitlement.)
- Layering notes: No local enhancement beyond state minimums identified; the chapter's special-needs category mirrors state law. Rancho Cordova approved The Liora affordable project through ministerial major design review in 2026, so objective-standards ministerial review is available for qualifying affordable projects.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/rancho-cordova-CA/chapter_4
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Rancho Cordova Housing Trust Development Impact Fee (Municipal Code Chapter 16.89) and city affordable housing gap financing**
- Agency: City of Rancho Cordova Housing Division (Community Development)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Not verified this pass. Rancho Cordova levies a housing trust development impact fee on nonresidential development (Chapter 16.89, confirmed by title in search results and by Sacramento County's Housing Trust Fund page listing Rancho Cordova among five cities with similar fees). Current rates, fund balance and loan terms unknown. The City's PLHA plan directs 100% of its PLHA allocation to gap financing for new affordable and permanent supportive housing (search snippet, document not opened).
- AMI requirement: Unknown; expected low and very low income per nexus.
- Cycle: rolling (next: unknown, verify with agency)
- Layering notes: cityofranchocordova.org and codepublishing.com return 403 to automated access and the City's Legistar instance has no public API, so this record is a placeholder. Rancho Cordova is in the Sacramento County HOME Consortium (SHRA-administered) and is a CDBG entitlement city that runs an annual CDBG NOFA (a 2026 CDBG NOFA workshop was listed on the City's housing page per search results). Recent City-supported deals include Mather Veterans Village Phase 4 and The Liora (ministerial design review 2026). Confirm fee schedule, fund balance and any NOFA with the Housing Division via the City's main line (916) 851-8700.
- Source: https://planning.saccounty.gov/Programs/Pages/HousingTrustFundProgram.aspx
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of Redwood City

**Affordable Housing NOFA and Preservation Program (impact fee fund loans)**
- Agency: City of Redwood City, Housing Services
- Type: loan
- Typical award: May 11, 2026 Council action: about $8 million to Rise City Apartments (94 units, 1304 Middlefield Road, 20% to 70% AMI, 24 PSH units including 19 for adults with intellectual and developmental disabilities, construction early 2027) and a commitment to direct about $5.8 million of expected Elco Yards impact fees to MidPen Housing's 79-unit project at 1580 Maple Street (20% to 40% AMI, 23 special needs units, City-owned land transferred by the County, construction late 2027). Staff had recommended about $12.9 million for the two projects; only two applications were received. The City also runs an Affordable Housing Preservation Program (over-the-counter fund for acquisition and deed restriction; amendments adopted May 11, 2026) and is studying 70 to 104 affordable units on Downtown Library Parking Lot B (1016 Middlefield Road).
- AMI requirement: New multifamily rental for low and extremely low income households; all assisted units at least 10% below 80% AMI; 55-year City regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: 2026 round awarded May 11, 2026; the next NOFA depends on impact fee receipts (Elco Yards payment expected mid-2026). Preservation Program is over the counter.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. City loans are residual receipts loans repaid from cash flow after operating expenses, hard debt and reserves, and are sized to leverage County AHF, HACSM PBVs and 4% or 9% credits; 1580 Maple shows the City-County land plus City fee money pattern. Marked needs_confirmation because redwoodcity.org (NOFA page, Preservation Program guidelines, fee schedule) returned 403 to automated access and figures come from the May 12, 2026 Redwood City Pulse report (source) and the City's meetings portal; confirm award terms with Housing Services.
- Source: https://www.rwcpulse.com/housing/2026/05/12/city-approves-millions-for-2-affordable-housing-projects/
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-07)

**Affordable Housing Ordinance (Zoning Code Article 29): inclusionary units and Affordable Housing Impact Fee**
- Agency: City of Redwood City, Housing Services (City Manager's Office) and Community Development
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Rental projects of 20 or more units: 20% affordable (10% moderate, 5% low, 5% very low income). Ownership projects of 20 or more units: 15% moderate income. Projects of 5 to 19 units and nonresidential projects over 5,000 square feet pay the Affordable Housing Impact Fee (Resolution 15462 of 2015; nonresidential rates raised December 20, 2021 to $23.62 per square foot for office, medical office and R&D and $5.91 for hotel, retail, restaurant and services; residential small-project rate reported at about $20 to $25 per square foot, with a temporary 25% reduction for 5 to 19 unit projects that pull vertical permits by June 30, 2027). Fees fund the City's affordable housing NOFA.
- AMI requirement: Rental units restricted at least 55 years, ownership at least 30 years, recorded covenants in first position; fractional units met by an extra unit or in-lieu fee set by Council resolution; 100% affordable projects exempt from the fee.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at entitlement; fees due before the first building permit. The 2026 San Mateo County multi-city nexus study (Strategic Economics, Redwood City is a participant) may change percentages and fees in 2027.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. Redwood City is the deepest inclusionary program in the county at 20% for rental with a very low income tier, which is why most downtown projects negotiate on-site units rather than pay the fee and why the impact fee fund grows mainly from commercial projects (Elco Yards is expected to pay about $5.8 million in 2026). Alternative compliance plans (off-site, land, conversions) go to Council. The ordinance text is on the City's code host (source, Article 29, Ordinances 1130-375 of 2018, 2498 of 2021, 1130-387 of 2023 and 1130-890 of 2024); fee amounts are from the December 20, 2021 staff report on meetings.redwoodcity.org because redwoodcity.org returns 403 to automated access. Confirm the current residential fee and the 25% reduction with Housing Services at (650) 780-7000.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/redwood-city-CA/chapter_28
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of Sacramento

**City of Sacramento Development Impact Fee Deferral Program**
- Agency: City of Sacramento Community Development Department, Building Division (Form CDD-0330)
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Deferral, not waiver: City impact fees may be paid at final inspection instead of permit issuance, useful for phased or multi-building projects. No cost figure published. Fee estimates available for a low-cost application. The Statewide Community Infrastructure Program (CSCDA SCIP) is also accepted for financing eligible City impact fees.
- AMI requirement: None; open to all residential projects.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Apply with CDD-0330 before permit issuance; fees due at final inspection.)
- Layering notes: Carries impact fees through construction so they can be paid from permanent loan proceeds; pair with the Zero-Dollar Rate reduction on the affordable units and SCIP bond financing of remaining fees. The County runs a separate deferral program for unincorporated projects (County record).
- Source: https://www.cityofsacramento.gov/community-development/planning/housing/housing-development-kit/housing-development-incentives/help-me-reduce-the-cost-of-development
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**City of Sacramento Housing Trust Fund (commercial linkage fee, City Code Chapter 18.56 Article IV)**
- Agency: City of Sacramento Community Development Department, Planning Division (fee assessment); SHRA (fund administration and lending)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Fee source for SHRA City gap loans. Nonresidential rates effective July 1, 2024 through June 30, 2026: office $3.33, hotel $3.18, commercial $2.68, manufacturing $2.08, warehouse $0.91 per square foot, adjusted annually by the San Francisco construction cost index (the FY 2026-27 notice was not posted as of 2026-11-08). 2024 fund: $411,805 fees collected, $140,000 spent, $4.85M ending balance of which $807,861 uncommitted; $1.0M posted as available June 1, 2026.
- AMI requirement: Fund is restricted to housing for workforce households up to 80% AMI (very low and low income wage earners) under the 1989 jobs-housing nexus; 55-year SHRA regulatory agreement.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Deployed through the SHRA two-round schedule (next pre-application likely early January 2027). Fee is paid at building permit on nonresidential projects; contact Ryan Dodge, (916) 808-8044, rdodge@cityofsacramento.org for fee questions.)
- Layering notes: Adopted 1989 (Chapter 17.708), moved into the Development Impact Fee chapter 18.56 on Feb 14, 2017; Council raised the fee 81.3% in 2004 (phased) and allows changes by resolution. Small and lumpy: about $0.4M to $0.6M a year in fees, so it is the smallest of the four City pools and is usually blended into a single SHRA loan with HOME and Mixed Income money. Parallel County Housing Trust Fund covers the unincorporated area (County rates $3.26 office to $1.08 warehouse since March 28, 2025). Annual report posted each May by SHRA.
- Source: https://www.cityofsacramento.gov/community-development/planning/housing/housing-trust-fund
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Density bonus (City Code Chapter 17.704) and ministerial infill housing permits (Chapter 17.860 and SB 35/SB 423)**
- Agency: City of Sacramento Community Development Department, Planning Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: State density bonus law as implemented in Chapter 17.704 (no local enhancement beyond state minimums identified): e.g., 5% very low income units earns a 20% bonus plus one concession and unlimited waivers; up to 50% bonus and the 100% affordable 80% bonus per Government Code 65915. City ministerial permit (CDD-0428, adopted Aug 27, 2020) covers 2 to 200 unit residential or mixed-use infill projects meeting objective standards, no affordability or prevailing wage requirement, no CEQA, 60 to 90 day review; the state SB 35 permit (CDD-0427) requires 10% lower-income units at 11 or more units. Density bonus requests can be processed ministerially with either permit.
- AMI requirement: Per state law for the bonus; none for the City ministerial permit.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Rolling at entitlement. Parking: none required within a quarter mile of light rail, 50% reduction within a half mile.)
- Layering notes: Sacramento is the state's first Prohousing jurisdiction (renewed Dec 23, 2025 with the top score) and already has high base densities, so the bonus is mostly used for height, parking and setback waivers rather than unit count. Combining the City ministerial permit with a density bonus avoids hearings and CEQA for 100% affordable infill without the SB 35 prevailing wage trigger. Missing Middle zoning (2024) allows multi-unit on former single-family lots citywide.
- Source: https://www.cityofsacramento.gov/community-development/planning/housing/housing-development-kit/housing-development-incentives/help-me-build-more-housing-units-or-tailor-my-project-to-my-site
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Mixed Income Housing Ordinance and Housing Impact Fee (City Code Chapter 17.712)**
- Agency: City of Sacramento Community Development Department, Planning Division (fee); SHRA (Mixed Income Housing fund and 100-acre housing strategies)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement and fee, not a subsidy. Housing Impact Fee effective July 1, 2026 through June 30, 2027: $3.58 per square foot for single-unit, duplex and multi-unit dwellings below 20 and 40 dwelling units per net acre respectively; $0.00 for single-unit or duplex at 20 du/acre or more, multi-unit at 40 du/acre or more, and nonresidential-to-residential conversions; $1.55 per square foot in the Housing Incentive Zone. Fee revenue (about $1.7M in 2024, $4.2M lent to projects that year) goes to the SHRA-administered Mixed Income Housing fund; $2.5M posted available June 1, 2026. Projects over 100 gross acres must adopt a Council-approved mixed income housing strategy.
- AMI requirement: Exempt from the fee: developments with 10% regulated low-income units (80% AMI), room additions, second units, owner-builder single homes, mobilehome parks, projects with pre-ordinance development agreements or pre-November 2015 entitlements. No mandatory inclusionary requirement citywide.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Fee assessed at building permit, adjusted every July 1. The Housing Element Program H1 review ended Dec 9, 2025 when Council voted to make no changes, so the current structure holds through at least 2027. Contact Greta Soos, (916) 808-2027.)
- Layering notes: Most 4% and 9% multifamily deals in Sacramento pay nothing: at 40 du/acre or more the rate is $0, and any project with 10% low-income units is exempt, so the fee mainly hits single-family subdivisions and low-density apartments. The fund it feeds is the largest City gap pool after HOME ($2.5M June 2026). Compare the County Affordable Housing Ordinance ($3.91 per square foot with on-site credit) for unincorporated sites. A 2023 to 2025 study considered mandatory inclusionary and higher fees; Council declined, so no inclusionary units are required citywide.
- Source: https://www.cityofsacramento.gov/community-development/planning/housing/housing-impact-fee
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Multifamily Mortgage Revenue Bonds, Housing Authority of the City of Sacramento (SHRA conduit issuance)**
- Agency: Sacramento Housing and Redevelopment Agency for the Housing Authority of the City of Sacramento
- Type: bond
- Typical award: Tax-exempt and taxable conduit bonds for rental projects of 12 or more units in the City; no minimum or maximum issue size (cost-effectiveness usually sets a floor). Applications for bonds without an SHRA loan are accepted year round; bond-plus-loan requests follow the SHRA pre-application schedule. Private placements require accredited or qualified institutional buyers in $250,000 denominations with investor letters.
- AMI requirement: Federal minimum 20% at 50% AMI or 30% at 60% AMI plus 10% at 50% AMI (H&S 34312.3); CDLAC and CTCAC rules govern; SHRA adds its 15% at 50% AMI and 5% at 30% AMI policy when an SHRA loan is included; non-competitive CTCAC deals averaging above 60% AMI must average at or below 59% AMI. Refundings that extend maturity require at least 10% of units at 50% or 60% AMI for 15 more years.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Bond-only applications any time; bond-with-loan applications on the SHRA schedule (next pre-application likely early January 2027). SHRA Commission then City Council approval, then CDLAC allocation.)
- Layering notes: SHRA prefers to be the issuer for City projects and pairs bonds with its gap loan; CSCDA or CMFA remain alternatives if timing does not fit. Bond policy requires the nonprofit owner to have material day-to-day involvement for projects claiming the welfare exemption, matching BOE scrutiny of managing general partners (see County Assessor record). Policies: 2026 City Multifamily Lending and Mortgage Revenue Bond Policies (Section 9).
- Source: https://www.shra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/2026-SHRA-Multifamily-Lending-Policies-City-final.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Prohousing Designation (state scoring preference for City of Sacramento projects)**
- Agency: City of Sacramento Community Development Department (designation held by the City; HCD administers the program)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Not a direct subsidy. Projects in a Prohousing jurisdiction receive priority processing or bonus points in state programs including AHSC, Infill Infrastructure Grant, TCC, TIRCP and related transportation programs, and the City may apply to the Prohousing Incentive Program for added funds. Sacramento was the first designated city (Feb 10, 2022) and renewed Dec 23, 2025 with 108 points, the highest score in the state as of June 2026.
- AMI requirement: None at the project level; benefit flows to any eligible affordable project in the City.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Designation runs on HCD's renewal cycle; next renewal due around 2029. Contact Angel Anguiano, (916) 808-5519.)
- Layering notes: Relevant to layering: AHSC and IIG applications for City sites score Prohousing points that unincorporated-county and most other Sacramento-area cities (Elk Grove, Rancho Cordova, Citrus Heights, Folsom were not confirmed as designated this pass) do not get. Check HCD's designated-jurisdictions tracker before assuming points for a non-City site.
- Source: https://www.cityofsacramento.gov/community-development/planning/housing/Prohousing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Reduced Residential Development Impact Fees for Affordable Dwelling Units (Zero-Dollar Rate Program)**
- Agency: City of Sacramento Community Development Department, Planning Division (Form CDD-0410); affordability confirmed by SHRA or CADA
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Reduction of City-controlled development impact fees to a $0 rate for deed-restricted affordable units, capped at $10,000 per qualifying unit, funded by a $3 million annual General Fund/Measure U appropriation (reauthorized July 2025). Eligible fees: park development impact fee (Ch. 18.56 Art. II), sewer, combined sewer, water system and storm drainage development fees (Ch. 13), and the 65th Street, Central City, Jacinto Creek, North Natomas, Railyards, River District and Willowcreek area fees. School fees and SAFCA fees are not City-controlled and still apply.
- AMI requirement: Rental: occupied by low or moderate income households (80% or 120% AMI per H&S 50093) at affordable rent (H&S 50053), restricted at least 30 years by a recorded regulatory agreement with the City, SHRA or another public agency. For-sale: low income (80% AMI) at an affordable price, 30 years.
- Cycle: rolling (next: First come, first served against each fiscal year's $3M; LIHTC and Homekey projects may reserve funds when awarded. Apply with Form CDD-0410 before permit issuance; contact planning@cityofsacramento.org early. Program resolutions 2018-0428, 2019-0185 and 2024-0233 (June 25, 2024).)
- Layering notes: Worth roughly $5,000 to $10,000 per unit depending on area fees; stackable with the SacSewer impact fee waiver (County record) which covers the regional sanitation fee the City program does not touch, and with the Housing Impact Fee $0 rate. Because Labor Code 1720(b)(4) treats waived fees as public funds, a fee reduction can by itself trigger state prevailing wage for a project that is otherwise exempt; most LIHTC projects already pay prevailing wage. The 2018 version waived fees without a cap; the 2024 modification added the $10,000 cap and the $3M budget, so large projects should not assume full waivers. Deferral of remaining impact fees to final inspection is available separately (CDD-0330).
- Source: https://www.cityofsacramento.gov/community-development/planning/housing/reduction-of-development-impact-fees-for-new-adus
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**SHRA Multifamily Lending Program (City of Sacramento gap financing: HOME, Housing Trust Fund, Mixed Income Housing fund, PLHA)**
- Agency: Sacramento Housing and Redevelopment Agency (SHRA) on behalf of the City of Sacramento and the Housing Authority of the City of Sacramento
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Gap loans sized to the gap after all other sources, no stated per-project cap. City funds posted June 1, 2026: HOME $5,000,000, Housing Trust Fund/Workforce Housing $1,000,000, Mixed Income Housing/Workforce Housing $2,500,000, PLHA $680,000 (about $9.2M). Applicants request gap funds without naming a source. January 2026 City pre-applications: Hotel Berry (Jamboree, preservation, $5.1M) invited; Florin Family Apartments (CCH, $5.1M) and Q Street Apartments (Community HousingWorks, $2.0M) invited; requests of $6.5M to $12M (Mercy 2601 5th Street, Danco 6779 Q Street, TWG 1220 H Street, Dos Rios) declined for insufficient funds. Projects must have 12 or more units.
- AMI requirement: 55-year regulatory agreement (HOME units per federal rules). SHRA minimum: at least 15% of units at or below 50% AMI and 5% at or below 30% AMI based on feasibility; regulated share at least proportional to SHRA share of total development cost; restricted rents at least 10% below market. Housing Trust Fund and Mixed Income fund money is limited to workforce households (up to 80% AMI, jobs-housing nexus). Priorities: preservation at risk, then new construction and nonresidential conversion (PSH and homeless housing, veterans, workforce 30% to 60% AMI, other), then rehab of regulated stock, then market-rate to affordable conversion.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: Two rounds a year aligned to CTCAC 9% rounds. 2026: pre-applications Jan 2 and July 1 (Builder's Patch), full applications March 2 and Sept 1, Council approvals June 16, 2026 and Jan 19, 2027. SHRA was not accepting pre-applications as of 2026-11-08; the 2027 schedule and a new funds-available notice are expected on the SHRA multifamily page at least 30 days before the next pre-application deadline, likely early January 2027. Phone (916) 440-1393.)
- Layering notes: This is the City-side pool parallel to the County SHRA record (Pass 2); a project uses one or the other based on location, both run on the same schedule and policies (2026 City Multifamily Lending and Mortgage Revenue Bond Policies, amended July 29, 2026). Terms: up to 3% simple interest or the AFR, term up to 40 years from conversion or 55 years when senior public lenders require, 1.10x to 1.20x combined DSCR, payments deferred during construction and deferred developer fee repayment, residual receipts allowed; nonprofit managing general partner required; developer fee limited to $25,000/$15,000/$12,000 per unit tiers for non-tax-credit deals and deferred maximum fee for LIHTC deals. Federal HOME (12 or more units) and CDBG (8 or more units) trigger Davis-Bacon; SHRA treats state prevailing wage as applying unless the below-market loan exemption (40% of units at 80% AMI for 20 years) fits. Stack with the City Zero-Dollar Rate impact fee reduction, Housing Impact Fee exemption at 40 du/acre, SacSewer fee waivers (County record) and Housing Authority of the City of Sacramento bonds. SHRA does not expect a project-based voucher RFP in 2026. SB 802 moves SHRA into the Sacramento Area Housing and Homelessness Agency by Jan 1, 2028, so confirm the lender entity on 2027 and later commitments.
- Source: https://www.shra.org/multi-family-housing-financing-lending-resources/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of San Carlos

**Below Market Rate (BMR) Ordinance (SCMC Chapter 18.16, revised June 2022)**
- Agency: City of San Carlos Community Development Department, Planning and Housing
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. 15% of units in market-rate developments: rental 10% very low plus 5% low income (or an option of 15% very low income to align with state density bonus law); ownership 10% moderate plus 5% low income, raised to 20% low income by the June 2022 revisions. On-site units required at 7 or more rental units and 5 or more ownership units; smaller projects pay an in-lieu fee.
- AMI requirement: Deed restrictions with City BMR eligibility rules (SCMC 18.16.080), San Carlos resident and worker preference, County income limits (2026 AMI $200,800 for four).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at entitlement; in-lieu fee amount in the Master Fee Schedule (not opened).)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. The very low income rental tier makes San Carlos BMR units eligible to pair with HACSM PBVs; recent downtown projects at 1232 to 1360 Cherry Street used the ordinance's density bonus alignment. Source is the Housing Resources chapter of the 2023-2031 Housing Element.
- Source: https://cms3.revize.com/revize/sancarlos/Document%20Center/Housing%20Element/05%20San%20Carlos%20Housing%20Resources_Final.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Commercial Linkage Fee fund loans and City land for affordable housing (Cherry Street Commons)**
- Agency: City of San Carlos Community Development Department, Housing Division (housing successor to the former Redevelopment Agency)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Commercial Linkage Fee (SCMC Chapter 8.51, adopted 2016) on large office and life science projects had produced over $10 million by 2023 with more than $30 million projected over the following five years. Council approved a $12,860,000 construction loan on June 26, 2023 for Cherry Street Commons (Eden Housing and HIP Housing, 33 family units, one to three bedrooms, replacing a six-unit HIP building at 1244 Cherry Street on City land including 1232 Cherry Street acquired in 2022, occupancy targeted 2026) and forgave a $100,000 1995 loan on 1244 Cherry in FY 2024-25. Housing successor assets total about $9 million (817 Walnut Street ground lease and $10.4 million of loans receivable) with a slight cash deficit. A NOFA 25-26 is listed on the City housing page without details.
- AMI requirement: Lower income households under City regulatory agreements; housing successor funds limited to 80% AMI with extremely low income minimums.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No open solicitation found; the City lists a NOFA 25-26 but the link resolves to the homepage. Contact the Housing Division at (650) 802-4265.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. San Carlos is cash-rich from life science linkage fees and land-rich downtown (Walnut Studios at 817 Walnut was the first City-financed project), so it funds projects at a much higher per-unit level than its neighbors; the per-square-foot linkage rate is in the Master Fee Schedule and was not confirmed this pass (codepublishing.com returned 403). Source is the City's Cherry Street Commons page; loan amount from the June 27, 2023 announcement (vmwp.com) and the FY 2024-25 Housing Successor Annual Report.
- Source: https://www.cityofsancarlos.org/city_hall/departments_and_divisions/community_development/housing/cherry_street_commons.php
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of San Mateo

**Affordable Housing Commercial Linkage Fee fund (SMMC Chapter 23.61)**
- Agency: City of San Mateo Community Development Department
- Type: other
- Typical award: Fee on net new nonresidential construction over 5,000 square feet, adopted September 2016 (Ordinance 2016-8): office, R&D and medical office $32.76 per square foot, hotel $13.11, retail, restaurant and services $6.55 (FY 2025-26 and FY 2026-27). 25% rebate for projects that sign a Standard Wage (area standard or prevailing wage) agreement; alternative of building 8 low-income units per 100,000 square feet of office, 3 per 100,000 of hotel, 1 per 100,000 of retail. Fund activity FY 2025-26: beginning $3,551,934, fees $1,090,711, loan repayments $514,717, ending balance $5,292,314 with $1,000,000 set aside for the Bespoke project and $4,000,000 programmed for housing land purchase.
- AMI requirement: Fund must support creation or preservation of housing for lower and moderate income workers; projects carry City regulatory agreements.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA. The City commits linkage fee money by Council action; the FY 2025-26 report programs $4 million toward housing land purchase. Contact housing@cityofsanmateo.org, (650) 522-7220.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. With about $4.3 million unprogrammed, the City can make one meaningful land or gap commitment per cycle rather than run a competitive round; developers should pair a request with the County AHF spring NOFA. The City is a CDBG entitlement (FY 2026-27 CDBG NOFA ran through the City) and joins the County HOME Consortium. Source is the FY 2025-26 Mitigation Fee Act (AB 1600) annual report; fee rates confirmed in the FY 2026-27 Comprehensive Fee Schedule and the Development Fees handout (DocumentCenter/View/98263).
- Source: https://www.cityofsanmateo.org/DocumentCenter/View/107918/FY2025-26-AB1600
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Below Market Rate (BMR) inclusionary program and in-lieu fees (SMMC 27.16.050)**
- Agency: City of San Mateo Community Development Department, Neighborhood Improvement and Housing Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Projects of 11 or more units: 15% of rental units for lower income households (rents at 30% of 50% to 70% AMI, 4% annual increase cap) and 15% of ownership units for moderate income (100% to 110% AMI). Projects of 5 to 10 units and fractional units pay the BMR Housing Fee (Resolution 121 of 2008; FY 2026-27 schedule: rental $171,273 for a 5-unit project up to $342,545 for 10 units, ownership $157,187 to $314,373; fractional units $34,255 per 0.1 rental unit and $31,438 per 0.1 ownership unit). In-lieu fees are otherwise not allowed. BMR Housing Fee fund balance $254,281 at June 30, 2026 with $2,560,924 programmed for housing land purchase.
- AMI requirement: 55-year restrictions; BMR units may be up to 10% smaller than market units (20% where market units exceed 2,000 square feet); density bonus projects must provide BMR units equal to at least 15% of the base density; off-site and applicant-proposed alternatives need Council findings (Guidelines Resolution 72, June 16, 2025).
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at planning application; fees indexed in the annual Comprehensive Fee Schedule each July 1. The City is a participant in the 2026 multi-city nexus study.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. San Mateo's program is unit-first: no fee option above 10 units, so inclusionary units are a given in every downtown and Hillsdale project and the City's cash sources are the Commercial Linkage Fee and the small BMR fee. Source is the adopted FY 2026-27 Comprehensive Fee Schedule (pages 11.1 to 11.2); program rules from the BMR Guidelines (cityofsanmateo.org/DocumentCenter/View/99291) and the FY 2025-26 Mitigation Fee Act report (DocumentCenter/View/107918).
- Source: https://www.cityofsanmateo.org/DocumentCenter/View/105420
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of South Lake Tahoe

**City direct gap and predevelopment financing for affordable housing (HOME program income, Housing Successor and Redevelopment Agency program income, PLHA formula funds, Prohousing Incentive Program funds, general fund, residential units of use) and state application sponsorship**
- Agency: City of South Lake Tahoe Development Services Department, Housing Division (Housing Manager, mmanuel@cityofslt.gov, 530-542-7417); City Council approves each commitment
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Deal-by-deal Council commitments, no NOFA or written guidelines. Track record: over $2.5 million of HOME program income, Redevelopment Agency program income and general fund to Sugar Pine Village (248 units, Related California and Saint Joseph Community Land Trust on state surplus land; Phase 1A 68 units opened fall 2024, Phase 2A 60 units fall 2025, Phase 1B 60 units summer 2026, Phase 2B seeking financing), plus City-sponsored AHSC ($17,359,470), MHP ($19.6 million), IIG, LGMG, HOME and PLHA awards; a $564,073 predevelopment loan plus $500,000 of Prohousing Incentive Program funds to Novin Development for the 75-unit 3900 and 3908 Lake Tahoe Boulevard project (SB 35 approval 2025, AHSC pending); about $1,791,669 CDBG-CV and $120,000 PIP for rehabilitation of three Homekey properties (72 supportive units) with Tahoe Coalition for the Homeless; 21 multifamily residential units of use donated to Lake Tahoe Community College's 100-bed dorm (about $315,000 market value); three Al Tahoe lots donated to Saint Joseph Community Land Trust. City awards 2020 to 2025 included PLHA formula about $880,000 total, PLHA competitive $15 million over three awards, HOME $6,538,240 and PIP Round 2 $620,000. Legacy RDA Low and Moderate Income Housing Fund loans (AB 987 database) total $16.46 million HOME, $2.15 million RDA and $1.18 million CDBG across 296 units in eight projects.
- AMI requirement: Follows the state source layered in: Sugar Pine Village and 3900 Lake Tahoe Boulevard are 55-year deed restrictions at 30% to 80% AMI; TRPA deed restriction also required when bonus units are used. HOME program income follows HOME rules.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No NOFA. Approach the Housing Division ahead of the AHSC, MHP and PLHA competitive rounds; the City committed to apply for at least one state or federal grant per year (Housing Element Program 2-1). Phase 2B of Sugar Pine Village still lacked a local match as of the August 2026 draft Housing Element. Final 2027-2035 Housing Element adoption was scheduled for a September 8, 2026 Council hearing; confirm the adopted program list.)
- Layering notes: South Lake Tahoe is a non-entitlement city that has out-raised most small cities by sponsoring state applications (PLHA competitive, AHSC, HOME, IIG, LGMG) for a nonprofit or private developer, so the City's own cash is small but its sponsorship is the key to the state layer. Gap order used on Sugar Pine Village: LIHTC and bonds, AHSC and MHP, IIG and LGMG, HOME and PLHA, City program income last. County layers available in the Tahoe Basin: El Dorado County PLHA formula and County HOME/CDBG participation requests, County Affordable Housing Trust Fund (no development NOFA), County PHA project-based vouchers, CMFA TEFRA approvals, welfare exemption, BHSA housing funds; the County Traffic Impact Fee offset (Policy B-14) does not apply inside the city because the City levies its own fees. TRPA layer: bonus units and the allocation exemption are required for any deed-restricted project (see the TRPA record), and TRPA application fees are waived for affordable projects. Residential allocations are scarce (33 market-rate per year citywide, one third reserved for multifamily), which is why deed-restricted projects using bonus units are the only route to scale. Workforce Housing Fund in-lieu fees (inclusionary record) are a separate, newer pool. No local housing trust fund, no city NOFA, no local tax-exempt issuer. Source is the City's August 2026 public review draft 2027-2035 Housing Element on the City DocumentCenter; the City's CivicPlus pages opened directly but council packets were not reachable (Granicus and Legistar hosts not found).
- Source: https://www.cityofslt.gov/DocumentCenter/View/24379/SLT_Housing-Element_Public-Review-Draft_August-2026
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**City permit fee waiver for nonprofit and public agency affordable housing (City Code Section 6.55.090), lot consolidation fee waiver for deed-restricted units (Housing Element Program 1-7) and Sugar Pine Village inspection fee reduction**
- Agency: City of South Lake Tahoe Development Services Department (Planning and Building Divisions); waivers approved by the City Council at a public hearing
- Type: fee waiver
- Typical award: Case by case. Nonprofits and public agencies developing affordable housing may request waiver of City permit fees, granted by Council resolution (about 45 days from request to approval, run concurrently with permit review). The City also reduced building inspection fees for Sugar Pine Village because HCD inspects the modular units. Program 1-7 commits the City to waive the lot line adjustment fee when the resulting project includes deed-restricted affordable units. City planning fees are modest relative to TRPA fees; City multifamily impact charges are mainly utility connection fees set by South Tahoe Public Utility District (not waived; $7,500 deposit and 10% of capacity fees at plan review). No waiver of TRPA water quality ($1.86 per sq ft) or excess coverage mitigation fees ($8.50 per sq ft), though TRPA application fees are waived for affordable, moderate and achievable bonus-unit projects and the mobility mitigation fee may be waived.
- AMI requirement: Affordable housing project sponsored by a nonprofit or public agency; deed restriction consistent with TRPA affordable, moderate or achievable definitions when bonus units are used. No specific AMI threshold published for the City waiver.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Request with the entitlement or building permit application; Council hearing required. Verify current practice with the Housing Division (530-542-7417) because the code text could not be opened.)
- Layering notes: Fee relief is small in dollar terms; the real cost drivers in the Basin are TRPA mitigation fees, land coverage purchases and development rights, which the TRPA bonus unit program (separate record) addresses. Combine with the TRPA application fee exemption and with the Phase 2 Housing Amendments height, coverage and density relief. Private for-profit developers are not eligible for the 6.55.090 waiver unless partnered with a nonprofit or public agency. The County Traffic Impact Fee offset (Board Policy B-14) is not available inside the city. The City's code host (codepublishing.com) returned 403 and the gocodebook mirror had no Chapter 6.55 page, so Section 6.55.090 was not read directly; the description is taken from the fees and exactions analysis in the City's August 2026 public review draft 2027-2035 Housing Element (pages 4-203 to 4-206) and from Program 1-7 of the adopted 2022-2027 Housing Element.
- Source: https://www.cityofslt.gov/DocumentCenter/View/24379/SLT_Housing-Element_Public-Review-Draft_August-2026
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (City Code Chapter 6.27, Ordinance 1180, adopted December 2024) and Workforce Housing Fund in-lieu fees**
- Agency: City of South Lake Tahoe Development Services Department, Planning Division and Housing Division (fund held by the Finance Director); 1052 Tata Lane, 530-542-7417
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. New residential construction, redevelopment of 50% or more of a building with dwellings, or condo conversion must provide inclusionary units or pay an in-lieu fee scaled to unit size (habitable square feet): 2,000 sq ft or less exempt; 2,001 to 2,500 sq ft 5% of units or $5.29 per sq ft; 2,501 to 3,000 sq ft 6% or $5.84 per sq ft; 3,001 to 5,000 sq ft 9% or $16.77 per sq ft; over 5,000 sq ft 17% or $17.65 per sq ft. Fees are paid before building permit issuance (by phase for phased projects) into the Workforce Housing Fund for housing affordable to low and moderate income households. Fund balance and collections to date are not published; the 2027-2035 draft Housing Element commits the City to post requirements, fee amounts and annual collections by mid-2027 and to publish a five-year in-lieu fee report under AB 2663.
- AMI requirement: Inclusionary units deed restricted for rent or sale to lower income households (80% AMI or below, El Dorado County), priced at no more than 30% of gross income including utilities, dispersed on site, comparable in exterior quality and bedroom mix, built concurrently with market-rate units. No affordability term is stated in the chapter text that was read.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at building permit; fee schedule in effect at permit issuance. Annual monitoring review each June under draft Program 2-10 with amendments within six months if warranted; first AB 2663 reporting for calendar 2026 due in 2027. Verify any fee indexing with the Planning Division before permit.)
- Layering notes: This is the only locally generated housing fund in South Lake Tahoe. Multifamily rental projects are exempt from both the unit requirement and the fee, so apartment developers owe nothing; the fund is fed by large single-family and condo construction, which is the dominant product in the Basin. Spending the Workforce Housing Fund is at Council discretion with no NOFA or guidelines published; pair requests with the City's HOME program income, PLHA formula and Prohousing Incentive Program dollars (see the City direct financing record) and with the County Affordable Housing Trust Fund, County PLHA and County HOME/CDBG records, which also have no standing NOFA. The ordinance grew out of Housing Element Program 2-11 (2022 to 2027 cycle) and the January 2023 Analysis of Local Funding Sources, which modeled TOT and transfer tax increases, a vacancy tax, a commercial linkage fee and an in-lieu fee against a $3.35 million annual local funding need; Council proceeded only with the inclusionary fee. No vacation rental (Measure T) or TOT revenue is earmarked for housing, and Measure T itself was struck down by the El Dorado County Superior Court in March 2025 and replaced by VHR Ordinance 2025-1200 (effective July 17, 2025) and a 2026 ordinance. Blocked hosts: codepublishing.com (the City's code host) returned 403 and the City Agenda Center search errored; the chapter was read on the gocodebook code mirror, and the adoption date and background come from the City's August 2026 public review draft 2027-2035 Housing Element (cityofslt.gov DocumentCenter View 24379).
- Source: https://gocodebook.com/library/us/ca/south-lake-tahoe-zoning/title-6-development-services/division-2-local-amendments-to-adopted-codes/chapter-6.27-inclusionary-housing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**TRPA Residential Bonus Unit Program (TRPA Code Chapter 52), allocation exemption (Chapter 50.5.1) and Phase 2 Housing Amendments as applied in South Lake Tahoe Town Centers (Tourist Core, Tahoe Valley and Midtown Area Plans)**
- Agency: Tahoe Regional Planning Agency (TRPA), with City of South Lake Tahoe Planning Division as lead reviewer inside Area Plan boundaries; City holds a voting seat on the TRPA Governing Board
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Regulatory, not cash, but it substitutes for development rights that otherwise must be bought. Deed-restricted affordable (80% AMI), moderate (120% AMI) or achievable (TRPA-set percentage tied to median home price) units receive residential bonus units at no cost in place of a residential unit of use (potential unit plus allocation), are exempt from the City's annual allocation (33 market-rate allocations per year citywide, one third reserved for multifamily), and are exempt from TRPA application fees. About 946 of the 1,124 bonus units set aside in 2018 remained as of the 2026 draft Housing Element, all reserved for deed-restricted housing and prioritized for Centers and multifamily zones. Phase 2 Housing Amendments (TRPA Governing Board, December 2023; MAP settlement July 2024) remove the maximum density cap for 100% deed-restricted projects in Town Centers, allow heights up to 65 feet in Centers (step-back, shade and scenic findings above 56 feet), allow 100% deed-restricted projects outside Centers to transfer in coverage up to 70% of high-capability land if runoff is treated by a public stormwater system, and allow use of the TRPA basin-wide parking study to reduce parking. Affordable projects using these tools have reached 61 to 67 units per acre in the city (Sugar Pine Village, 3900 Lake Tahoe Boulevard).
- AMI requirement: Permanent TRPA deed restriction recorded as a permit condition: occupancy limited to affordable, moderate or achievable households (or local employees for achievable units), no second-home or vacation rental use, annual compliance report to TRPA (penalties up to $5,000 per day), real estate disclosure on transfer; project within one half mile of transit. Height, coverage and density incentives under Phase 2 require 100% deed-restricted units plus stormwater and transportation contributions.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Bonus units assigned at TRPA permit (about 120 days of TRPA review, or City-only review for qualifying projects inside the TCAP and TVAP). Watch: TRPA Phase 3 (Cultivating Community, Conserving the Basin) EIS scoping closed March 16, 2026 with further code changes pending; City Midtown Area Plan (MTAP) adoption was pending a September 2026 hearing; TRPA 2025-2026 income limits and achievable percentages are updated each July.)
- Layering notes: Flag for every South Lake Tahoe deal: the Tahoe Basin is dual-jurisdiction. The City cannot grant a state density bonus law bonus or concessions that exceed TRPA standards; TRPA gives full density relief only to 100% deed-restricted projects and gives no comparable benefit or mandatory concessions to mixed-income projects that set aside a share of units, which the draft 2027-2035 Housing Element names as a continuing constraint (Program 2-8 asks TRPA to allow state density bonus compliance). Land coverage (Bailey system, 30% base outside Area Plans, 70% inside TCAP and TVAP), scenic and height limits and the allocation system govern feasibility more than zoning. Projects using bonus units must carry a TRPA deed restriction in addition to TCAC, HCD or City covenants; coordinate the three so income limits (TRPA uses county AMI for a family of three) and terms do not conflict. The TRPA application fee waiver stacks with the City Section 6.55.090 waiver (separate record). County layers (PLHA, Trust Fund, PBVs, welfare exemption) apply as in the rest of El Dorado County. Source pages at trpa.gov opened directly; City-specific figures (946 bonus units remaining, 61 to 67 du per acre, 65-foot height, MTAP status) come from the City's August 2026 draft Housing Element on the City DocumentCenter.
- Source: https://www.trpa.gov/housing/
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)


### City of South San Francisco

**Commercial Linkage Fee fund (Fund 823) and Affordable Housing Trust Fund loans**
- Agency: City of South San Francisco Economic and Community Development Department, Housing Division
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Commercial Linkage Fee (SSFMC 8.69, adopted 2018): office and R&D $17.38 per square foot, hotel $5.80, retail, restaurant and services $2.90 (January 2025 handout). Fund 823 revenue swung from $7.5 million (FY 2022-23) to a projected $1.5 million in FY 2025-26, $19.3 million in FY 2026-27 and $8.5 million in FY 2027-28 (Genentech, Trammell Crow and Healthpeak payments); projected year-end FY 2024-25 balances were $14 million in Fund 823, $200,000 in the Affordable Housing Trust Fund (Fund 205) and $1 million in the housing successor fund (Fund 241). City loans: Rotary Gardens Phase 1, 500 Linden Avenue, up to $7,867,409 (August 11, 2026); Synergy Community Development acquisition of the Metropolitan Hotel, 220 Linden, up to $3,115,000 from Fund 823 (February 24, 2026); 1051 Mission Road (BRIDGE, 158 units, PUC site) $2,000,000 raised to $2,362,000 as the LHTF match plus up to $14.6 million committed April 22, 2026 from the first market-rate phase for Measure K and tax credit applications; 428-432 Baden (For the Future Housing, 36 units) $4,050,000; 201-219 Grand and 418 Linden (ROEM, 84 units) $3,500,000; 201 Baden (Eden, 82 units) $2,362,500 LHTF. City also holds a $28,817,500 IIG grant for 1051 Mission and $890,000 PIP.
- AMI requirement: Trust Fund serves low and moderate income households; LHTF-matched loans require extremely low and very low income units; 55-year City regulatory agreements.
- Cycle: rolling (next: No standing NOFA; the Affordable Housing Financing Plan (May 2025, FY 2025-27) says the City will time NOFAs, acquisitions and rehabilitation around linkage fee payouts, with 66% of projected revenue arriving in FY 2026-27. Requests go to Council through the Housing Division (housing@ssfca.gov, (650) 829-6620).)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. South San Francisco has the largest city housing fund in the county after the linkage fee rebound and uses it as the first-in local loan (residual receipts) ahead of County AHF, state IIG and LHTF money; the City also waives or pays permit fees from Fund 823 for public housing work (C Street electrification, August 2026) and holds TEFRA hearings for CMFA bonds (Rotary Gardens, August 2026). City-owned sites: 1051 Mission Road (PUC site, Development Agreement amended April 24, 2026), 220 Linden and 905 Linden under negotiation in 2026. Source is the Housing Division funding page; figures from the Affordable Housing Financing Plan PDF and Council files on ci-ssf-ca.legistar.com (Legistar API client ci-ssf-ca).
- Source: https://www.ssfca.gov/Departments/Economic-and-Community-Development/Divisions/Housing-Division/Funding-Sources-for-Affordable-Housing
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (SSFMC Chapter 20.380) and replacement housing rules**
- Agency: City of South San Francisco Economic and Community Development Department, Housing Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Market-rate projects of 5 or more units: rental 15% (10% lower income, 5% very low income); homebuyer 15% (7.5% moderate, 7.5% lower income); extended to rental housing in 2018. In-lieu fee amounts are not posted on the developer page. Demolition requires one-for-one replacement, relocation assistance and right of first refusal.
- AMI requirement: County income and rent limits (2026-27 schedule posted), utility allowances deducted, regulatory agreements control; Procedures and Guidelines for Inclusionary Units updated September 2024.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Applies at entitlement; the City is not listed among the 2026 multi-city nexus study participants, so no change is pending.)
- Layering notes: County layer: every San Mateo County city project competes for the County Affordable Housing Fund NOFA (AHF 14 closed July 15, 2026, next fall 2027), HEART bridge loans and HACSM project-based vouchers; city money is usually the first commitment that unlocks those. The 15% rental requirement with a very low income tier plus the linkage fee is why South San Francisco's biotech corridor funds housing elsewhere in the city; 100% affordable projects are exempt and instead draw on Fund 823 loans (see separate record). Source is the Housing Requirements for Developers page.
- Source: https://www.ssfca.gov/Departments/Economic-and-Community-Development/Divisions/Housing-Division/Housing-Requirements-for-Developers
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-07)


### City of West Sacramento

**Affordable Housing, Density Bonuses and Incentives (West Sacramento Municipal Code Chapter 17.23, Ordinance 19-1)**
- Agency: City of West Sacramento Community Development Department, Planning Division
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Regulatory. Chapter 17.23 applies in all zones allowing residential use and grants qualifying developments and qualifying land transfers the density bonus, incentives and concessions specified in Government Code 65915 by reference (no local enhancement, no local cap). Requests are processed with the entitlement and approved if state criteria are met; a continued availability agreement is required before approval. Section 17.23.070 restates Housing Accountability Act limits on denying projects with at least 20% low income units. The 2019 zoning code update raised minimum and maximum densities in medium, high density and mixed-use zones (about 2,025 acres), so the Housing Element targets only about 5 density bonus units for the cycle.
- AMI requirement: State density bonus law thresholds (5% very low, 10% low, 10% moderate for-sale, senior, 100% affordable) with covenants per Government Code 65915(c).
- Cycle: rolling (next: None; request in writing to the Planning Division with the project application. The City reviews the chapter in its biannual zoning code update.)
- Layering notes: West Sacramento's leverage is zoning (high base densities in the Bridge District, Washington and Pioneer Bluff specific plan areas, mixed-use and waterfront zones) rather than the bonus itself. SB 35 and now SB 423 streamlining accepted for 10% or more affordable projects. Chapter 15.40 incentives (parking reductions, priority processing, standards waivers) can be combined. The code mirror (zoneomics) matches ecode360's section list; confirm against the official code.
- Source: https://www.zoneomics.com/code/west-sacramento-CA/chapter_3
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**CDBG entitlement program (2026-2030 Consolidated Plan) and HOME program income**
- Agency: City of West Sacramento Economic Development and Housing Department, Housing Division
- Type: grant
- Typical award: West Sacramento became a CDBG entitlement city in FY 2016/17 and receives about $546,000 a year (the City's March 2026 notice anticipated an amount similar to the prior year's $546,333). The draft 2026-2030 Consolidated Plan and FY 2026-27 Annual Action Plan were out for comment March 6 to April 7, 2026. Housing uses have been infrastructure for affordable projects ($1,191,428 of CDBG program income for West Gateway Place Phase 1), fair housing services, homeless coordinator staffing, first-time homebuyer help and owner rehabilitation. The City is not a HOME participating jurisdiction but holds HOME program income from earlier state HOME awards ($2,000,000 went into West Gateway Place II). State LHTF applications ($1,000,000) were not awarded.
- AMI requirement: CDBG at or below 80% AMI or area benefit; HOME program income at or below 80% AMI with HOME rent limits.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: FY 2027-28 Action Plan comment period expected March to April 2027; subrecipient applications typically open in winter. Confirm with the Housing Division.)
- Layering notes: Use for off-site infrastructure and public improvements around a Bridge District or Washington project rather than vertical construction; HOME program income is the more flexible pot and has gone straight into City-sponsored projects. Yolo County's state CDBG and HOME cover only unincorporated areas. Facts come from the HCD-hosted Housing Element and search snippets of the City's 2026 notices; the CDBG page and Consolidated Plan PDFs on cityofwestsacramento.org returned 403.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/housing-elements/docs/west-sacramento-6th-adopted071521.pdf
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)

**Inclusionary Housing Ordinance (West Sacramento Municipal Code Chapter 15.40, Ordinance 14-4) with Housing Trust Fund Program in-lieu alternative and Urban Infill Area incentive**
- Agency: City of West Sacramento Economic Development and Housing Department, Housing Division (Housing Manager Raul Huerta, (916) 617-4555)
- Type: density bonus
- Typical award: Requirement, not a subsidy. Residential projects of 5 or more units citywide (adopted April 2014, replacing the redevelopment-area Chapter 15.10): rental projects set aside 10% of units (5% very low income, 5% low income); for-sale projects 10% low income, with rental units substitutable. Units on site, dispersed, at least 90% of the smallest market unit size, concurrent with each 25% of market units. Alternatives at Council discretion: payment into the Housing Trust Fund Program at the amount set in the Book of City Imposts Section VIII-N (a regional comparison in late 2025 listed West Sacramento's in-lieu fee at about $7,699 per dwelling unit, assessed per market-rate unit), acquisition and rehabilitation of market units, off-site construction, or preservation of at-risk units. Projects in the priority Urban Infill Area earn a discount on the in-lieu fee based on adjusted density (dwelling unit equivalents for structured parking, public open space and ground floor commercial). Incentives include reduced parking, priority processing, standards waivers and technical assistance; financial incentives only to the extent City programs exist.
- AMI requirement: Rental: 5% very low (50% AMI) and 5% low income (80% AMI) for 55 years; for-sale: 10% low income with a 45-year goal and recapture on early release.
- Cycle: rolling (next: None (ordinance). The Housing Element (Program HE-PR-1.2) commits to refining Chapter 15.40 to target lower incomes and reduce the burden on market-rate housing; six affordable housing agreements were approved 2018 to 2020. Confirm the current Book of City Imposts fee with the Housing Division.)
- Layering notes: The in-lieu alternative is the main revenue source for the City's Housing Trust Fund (Fund 261-9385), which funds gap loans (separate record). Because the per-unit fee is low relative to Davis ($250,000) and Folsom, most subdivisions in Southport pay the fee rather than build, so West Sacramento's affordable production comes from City-assisted 100% affordable projects in the Bridge District and Washington (West Gateway Place I and II, Landmark Lofts, the Grand Gateway 114-unit project). Overconcentration cap: no more than 40% of a project's units may be inclusionary. No county inclusionary applies inside the city. ecode360 hosts the official West Sacramento code and opened directly; cityofwestsacramento.org (Akamai) returns 403 to scripts and WebFetch, and the Book of City Imposts could not be opened.
- Source: https://ecode360.com/43204558
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**Permanent Local Housing Allocation (PLHA) formula funds, Resolution 25-15 five-year plan**
- Agency: City of West Sacramento Economic Development and Housing Department, Housing Division (Housing Manager Raul Huerta)
- Type: grant
- Typical award: Council adopted Resolution 25-15 on February 19, 2025 (5 to 0) authorizing the PLHA application and a five-year plan for $763,253 of 2021, 2022 and 2023 formula allocations: 55% ($419,789) for the affordable housing component of the Grand Gateway Master Plan, specifically a proposed 114-unit affordable project serving households at 30%, 50%, 60% and up to 80% AMI; 40% ($305,301) for owner-occupied rehabilitation grants up to $30,000 through existing nonprofit contracts; 5% administration. HCD's 2024-2028 PLHA NOFA lists West Sacramento at about $1.04 million over the next five years. The City filed a CEQA notice of exemption for the plan on February 19, 2025.
- AMI requirement: Rental component at or below 80% AMI with deeper targeting (30% to 60% AMI) at the Grand Gateway site; owner rehabilitation for low income owners in qualifying neighborhoods.
- Cycle: annual NOFA (next: The Grand Gateway affordable project has no published developer selection or construction date; later PLHA years (2024 onward) need a plan amendment. Verify with the Housing Division.)
- Layering notes: Small but useful: about $420,000 earmarked to one City-sponsored project in the Grand Gateway District (City-owned land, see successor agency record). Any developer of that site should expect PLHA plus Housing Trust Fund plus land, then compete for MHP or AHSC. State funds trigger prevailing wage. Yolo County's own PLHA is unincorporated only. Source is the state CEQA notice of exemption filed by the City; the resolution and staff report on cityofwestsacramento.org returned 403, and dollar figures are from local press coverage of the February 19, 2025 meeting.
- Source: https://ceqanet.lci.ca.gov/2025020729
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)

**Redevelopment Successor Agency housing assets, Enhanced Infrastructure Financing District and City-owned land (Grand Gateway Master Plan area, Bridge District, Washington Specific Plan)**
- Agency: City of West Sacramento as Housing Successor and Redevelopment Successor Agency (Economic Development and Housing Department; Yolo Countywide Successor Agency Oversight Board)
- Type: other
- Typical award: Land and tax increment tools rather than a cash fund. With the loss of redevelopment the City repealed Chapter 15.10 and formed an Enhanced Infrastructure Financing District with 14 project areas that, with Measure G sales tax, funds infrastructure for infill, high-density, transit-oriented housing in the riverfront districts (Community Investment Program HE-PR-1.4). The City owns the Grand Gateway Master Plan area (former West Capitol Avenue redevelopment parcels; the Oversight Board approved sale of three Successor Agency parcels at 485 Lighthouse Drive and 2400 and 2600 West Capitol Avenue to the City for $1,310,000 in 2024) where a 114-unit affordable project is planned, and has contributed land to West Gateway Place I and II in the Bridge District. Housing successor Low and Moderate Income Housing Asset Fund balance and SB 341 reports were not accessible this pass.
- AMI requirement: LMIHAF uses follow Health and Safety Code 34176.1 (30% of spending at or below 30% AMI, remainder at or below 60% AMI, 55-year covenants); City land deals carry negotiated covenants.
- Cycle: rolling (next: Unknown, verify with agency. Watch for a developer solicitation for the Grand Gateway affordable site and for the annual SB 341 housing successor report (due by April 1 with the APR).)
- Layering notes: West Sacramento is the only Yolo County city with an active successor agency land program and an EIFD; the brief's note that it has active successor programs is confirmed in the Housing Element but current LMIHAF numbers could not be read. Developers should ask the Housing Division about (1) City-owned sites in Grand Gateway, Washington and Bridge District, (2) EIFD or Measure G infrastructure contributions that reduce off-site costs, and (3) any LMIHAF balance for extremely low income units. Layer with the Housing Trust Fund, PLHA and state AHSC (the 2016 to 2021 cycle brought $4.13 million AHSC infrastructure plus $2.6 million for West Gateway Place). Blocked: cityofwestsacramento.org and yolocounty.gov (403); the Oversight Board facts come from search snippets of Yolo County documents, so confirm.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/housing-elements/docs/west-sacramento-6th-adopted071521.pdf
- Confidence: needs_confirmation (researched 2026-11-08)

**West Sacramento Housing Development Corporation (City-affiliated nonprofit co-developer and property owner)**
- Agency: West Sacramento Housing Development Corporation (501(c)(3), EIN 94-3184534, 2950 Beacon Boulevard) working with the City Housing Division
- Type: other
- Typical award: Partnership capacity rather than a funding program. The Housing Element names the West Sacramento Housing Development Corporation (WSHDC) alongside Yolo County Housing as the City's standing nonprofit partners for pursuing state and federal funds (Programs HE-PR-1.6 and 1.9). WSHDC co-developed West Gateway Place (77 units, 2017, $29.8 million) and West Gateway Place II / The Bridge at West Gateway (60 units, 2025) with Jamboree Housing on City-contributed land, and owns scattered rental properties managed by a third-party manager.
- AMI requirement: Projects to date serve 20% to 60% AMI families with permanent supportive units; terms set by the City and state funding.
- Cycle: rolling (next: None; WSHDC has no NOFA. Approach through the City Housing Division when proposing a joint venture on City or successor agency land.)
- Layering notes: For an outside developer WSHDC is the local partner that unlocks City land, Housing Trust Fund and HOME program income commitments and local-nonprofit scoring; Jamboree has been its repeat co-developer. Mercy Housing (Esperanza Crossing, CMFA bonds) and New Hope CDC (9% award for a West Sacramento project) have also worked in the city without WSHDC. Pair with Yolo County Housing PBVs and the welfare exemption (county records). WSHDC has no website; corporate facts are from nonprofit registries and the Jamboree fact sheet.
- Source: https://www.hcd.ca.gov/housing-elements/docs/west-sacramento-6th-adopted071521.pdf
- Confidence: verified (researched 2026-11-08)

**West Sacramento Housing Trust Fund (Fund 261-9385) gap financing loans, HOME program income and City land contributions**
- Agency: City of West Sacramento Economic Development and Housing Department, Housing Division (Housing Manager Raul Huerta; Community Investment Principal Planner David Tilley, (916) 617-4661)
- Type: loan
- Typical award: Project-sized gap loans from Chapter 15.40 in-lieu receipts, used for gap financing to projects with affordable units, infrastructure supporting affordable housing and predevelopment.
